# The Greek
# Magical Papyri
## Texts of the Greek Magical Papyri <note>original: "Papyri Graecae Magicae"; often abbreviated as PGM, this is a famous collection of ancient spells, hymns, and rituals found on papyrus in Egypt.</note>

<column-break/>

->**A Collection of Ancient Wisdom**<-

<margin>Translated from the original Greek and Latin sources</margin>

The following pages contain the transcriptions and translations of the <term>Papyri Graecae Magicae</term> <note>A technical term for the body of magical texts from Greco-Roman Egypt, dating roughly from the 2nd century BCE to the 5th century CE.</note>. These documents provide a unique window into the private religious practices, folk medicine, and sorcery of the ancient Mediterranean world.

<summary>This is the title page for the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM), a seminal collection of ancient spells and ritual texts from Greco-Roman Egypt. It introduces the scope of the manuscript collection and its historical significance.</summary>

<keywords>Greek Magical Papyri, PGM, ancient magic, papyrology, Greco-Roman Egypt, ritual texts</keywords>

# Acquiring a Supernatural Assistant

## Greek Magical Papyri

Pnouthios to Keryx, a god-fearing man <note>The original Greek likely used "theosebes," a term for a pious person or a specific class of Gentile sympathizers to Judaism</note>, greetings. As one who knows, I have prescribed for you this spell for acquiring an assistant <note>The "paredros" or supernatural assistant was a familiar spirit or lesser deity bound to serve the magician</note> to prevent your failing as you carry out this rite. After detaching all the prescriptions bequeathed to us in countless books, one out of all I have shown you this spell for acquiring an assistant as one that is serviceable to you... for you to take this holy assistant and only... O friend of aerial spirits that move... having persuaded me with god-given spells... but not I have dispatched this book so that you may learn thoroughly. For the spell of Pnouthis <note>Pnouthios, the purported author, claims his name itself carries ritual power</note> has the power to persuade the gods and all the goddesses. And I shall write you from it about acquiring an assistant.

The traditional rite for acquiring an assistant: After the preliminary purifications, abstain from animal food and from all uncleanliness and, on whatever night you want to, go up onto a lofty roof after you have clothed yourself in a pure garment... and say the first spell of encounter as the sun's orb is disappearing... with a wholly black Isis band <note>A ritual headband or blindfold associated with the mourning of the goddess Isis</note> on your eyes, and in your right hand grasp a falcon's head <note>The falcon was the sacred animal of the sun god Helios or Horus</note> and... when the sun rises, hail it as you shake its head and... recite this sacred spell as you burn uncut frankincense and pure rose oil, making the sacrifice in an earthen censer on ashes from the plant heliotrope <note>Literally "sun-turner," a plant believed to follow the sun's path</note>. And as you recite the spell there will be this sign for you: a falcon will fly down and stand in front of you, and after

<summary>This page contains a letter from the magician Pnouthios to Keryx detailing the preliminary instructions and purification rites for summoning a "paredros," or supernatural assistant. The ritual involves rooftop invocations, specific dietary restrictions, and the use of solar symbols like the falcon and heliotrope.</summary>

<keywords>Pnouthios, Keryx, supernatural assistant, paredros, Greek Magical Papyri, ritual purification, Isis band, falcon sacrifice, frankincense, heliotrope, solar magic</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
flapping its wings in mid-air and dropping an oblong stone, it will immediately take flight and ascend to heaven. You should pick up this stone; carve it at once and engrave it later. <note>The text suggests a two-stage process: shaping the stone immediately while the divine energy is fresh, then adding specific symbols or names later.</note> Once it has been engraved, bore a hole in it, pass a thread through and wear it around your neck. But in the evening, go up to your housetop again and, facing the light of the goddess, <note>Likely referring to the Moon or Selene.</note> address to her this hymnic spell as you again sacrifice troglodyte myrrh <note>original: "myrrh troglitis" — a high-quality, fragrant resin from the Red Sea coast, prized in ancient perfumery and ritual.</note> in the same fashion. Light a fire and hold a branch of myrtle... shaking it, and salute the goddess.

At once there will be a sign for you like this: A blazing star will descend and come to a stop in the middle of the housetop, and when the star has dissolved before your eyes, you will behold the angel <note>term: angel — from the Greek 'angelos' meaning messenger; here a divine intermediary or supernatural servant.</note> whom you have summoned and who has been sent to you, and you will quickly learn the decisions of the gods. But do not be afraid: approach the god and, taking his right hand, kiss him and say these words to the angel, for he will quickly respond to you about whatever you want. But you adjure him with this oath that he meet you and remain inseparable and that he not keep silent or disobey in any way. 

But when he has with certainty accepted this oath of yours, take the god by the hand and leap down, and after bringing him into the narrow room where you reside, sit him down. After first preparing the house in a fitting manner and providing all types of foods and Mendesian wine, <note>A famous wine from the Mendesian region of the Nile Delta, often used in Egyptian religious offerings.</note> set these before the god, with an uncorrupted boy <note>A common requirement in ancient magic; a child who has not yet reached puberty was considered ritually pure and able to witness divine manifestations.</note> serving and maintaining silence until the angel departs. And you address preliminary words to the god: "I shall have you as a friendly assistant, a beneficent god who serves me whenever I say..."

<summary>This ritual text describes the summoning of a divine assistant or angel through the use of a fallen stone, myrrh offerings, and a celestial sign. It outlines the hospitality and oaths required to bind the spirit to the practitioner's service.</summary>
<keywords>ritual magic, divine assistant, angel summoning, myrrh troglitis, Mendesian wine, celestial signs, talisman engraving</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the ritual of the "Paredros" or supernatural assistant, detailing the spirit's capabilities and the specific taboos associated with its service.</meta>

> "Quickly, by your power now appear on earth to me, yea verily, god!"

And while reclining, you yourself quickly speak about what you propose. Test this oath of the god on whatever you wish. But when 3 hours have passed, the god will immediately leap up. Order the boy to run to the door. And say, "Go, lord, blessed god, where you live eternally, as you will," and the god vanishes.

This is the sacred rite for acquiring an assistant. It is acknowledged that he is a god; he is an aerial spirit which you have seen. If you give him a command, straightway he performs the task: he sends dreams, he brings women, men without the use of <term>magical material</term> <note>original: "ousia"; refers to physical ingredients like hair or clothing fragments usually needed for attraction spells</note>, he kills, he destroys, he stirs up winds from the earth, he carries gold, silver, bronze, and he gives them to you whenever the need arises. And he frees from bonds a person chained in prison, he opens doors, he causes invisibility so that no one can see you at all, he is a bringer of fire, he brings water, wine, bread and whatever you wish in the way of foods: olive oil, vinegar—with the single exception of fish—and he will bring plenty of vegetables, whatever kind you wish, but as for pork, you must not ever tell him to bring this at all! <note>Dietary restrictions were common in ancient magic to maintain ritual purity or respect the specific nature of the summoned spirit.</note>

And when you want to give a dinner, tell him so. Conjure up in your mind any suitable room and order him to prepare it for a banquet quickly and without delay. At once he will bestow chambers with golden ceilings, and you will see their walls covered with marble—and you consider these things partly real and partly just illusionary—and costly wine, as is meet to cap a dinner splendidly. He will quickly bring <term>daimons</term> <note>In this context, "daimons" refers to lesser spirits or divine intermediaries, not necessarily "demons" in the modern sense</note>, and for you he will adorn these servants with sashes. These things he does quickly. And as soon as you

<summary>This passage describes the vast powers of a summoned divine assistant, ranging from providing wealth and food to performing acts of destruction and invisibility. It highlights specific dietary prohibitions against fish and pork and the spirit's ability to create elaborate, semi-illusory banquets.</summary>

<keywords>Paredros, ritual magic, aerial spirit, invisibility, dietary taboos, banquet, daimons, ancient sorcery</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the ritual of the 'Paredros' or supernatural assistant, detailing the specific powers the spirit grants the practitioner and the final steps of the invocation.</meta>

If you order him to perform a service, he will do so, and you will see him excelling in other things: He stops ships and again releases them; he stops very many evil spirits <note>original: "daimons"; in this context, these are lesser supernatural entities that could cause harm</note>; he checks wild beasts and will quickly break the teeth of fierce reptiles; he puts dogs to sleep and renders them voiceless. He changes into whatever form of beast you want: one that flies, swims, a four-legged animal, or a reptile. He will carry you into the air, and again hurl you into the billows of the sea's current and into the waves of the sea; he will quickly freeze rivers and seas in such a way that you can run over them firmly, as you want. 

And especially will he stop, if ever you wish it, the sea-running foam; and whenever you wish to bring down stars, and whenever you wish to make warm things cold and cold things warm, he will light lamps and extinguish them again. And he will shake walls and cause them to blaze with fire; he will serve you suitably for whatever you have in mind, O blessed initiate <note>a person who has been formally admitted into the secret knowledge of a mystery cult</note> of the sacred magic, and will accomplish it for you—this most powerful assistant <note>term: "paredros"; a supernatural helper or familiar spirit bound to the magician</note>, who is also the only lord of the air. And the gods will agree to everything, for without him nothing happens. Share this great mystery with no one else, but conceal it, by the Sun <note>original: "Helios"</note>, since you have been deemed worthy by the lord god.

And this is spoken next: 

-> "Hither to me, King, I call you God of Gods, might, boundless, undefiled, indescribable, firmly established Eternity <note>original: "Aion"; the personification of cyclical or eternal time</note>. Be inseparable from me from this day forth through all the time of my life." <-

Then question him by the same oaths. If he tells you his name, take him by the hand, descend and have him recline as I have said above, setting before him part of the foods and drinks which you yourself are eating.

<summary>This text describes the miraculous powers of a summoned spirit assistant, including shapeshifting and elemental control, and provides the specific prayer to bind the entity 'Aion' to the practitioner.</summary>
<keywords>supernatural assistant, shapeshifting, elemental magic, Aion, Helios, ritual secrecy, Paredros</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the ritual of the "paredros" or supernatural assistant, focusing on the lifelong benefits, the post-mortem journey of the practitioner, and the secrecy required for these mysteries.</meta>

partake of. And when you release him, sacrifice to him after his departure what is prescribed and pour a wine offering <note>original: "libation"</note>, and in this way you will be a friend of the mighty angel. When you go abroad, he will go abroad with you; when you are destitute, he will give you money. He will tell you what things will happen both when and at what time of the night or day. And if anyone asks you "What do I have in mind?" or "What has happened to me?" or even "What is going to happen?," question the angel, and he will tell you in silence. But you speak to the one who questions you as if from yourself. When you are dead, he will wrap up your body as befits a god, but he will take your spirit and carry it into the air with him. 

For no aerial spirit which is joined with a mighty assistant <term>paredros: a supernatural "assistant" or "companion" bound to a magician through ritual</term> will go into Hades <note>The Greek underworld; the text promises the magician will escape the common fate of the dead</note>, for to him all things are subject. Whenever you wish to do something, speak his name alone into the air and say, <insert>"Come!"</insert> and you will see him actually standing near you. And say to him, "Perform this task," and he does it at once, and after doing it he will say to you, "What else do you want? For I am eager for heaven." If you do not have immediate orders, say to him, "Go, lord," and he will depart. 

In this fashion, then, the god will be seen by you alone, nor will anyone ever hear the sound of his speaking, just you yourself alone. And he will tell you about the illness of a man, whether he will live or die, even on what day and at what hour of night. And he will also give you both wild herbs and the power to cure, and you will be worshipped as a god since you have a god as a friend. These things the mighty assistant will perform competently. Therefore share these things with no one except your legitimate son alone when he asks you for the magic powers imparted by us. Farewell.

<summary>This text describes the extraordinary powers granted by a supernatural assistant, including divination, financial aid, healing, and the promise of an afterlife in the air rather than the underworld. It concludes with a strict command to keep these rituals secret, sharing them only with a legitimate heir.</summary>
<keywords>supernatural assistant, paredros, divination, immortality, Hades, healing, ritual secrecy, Greek Magical Papyri</keywords>

# Greek Magical Papyri III.1-59

*[The Greek Magical Papyri (P.G.M. III.1-164) is a multipurpose magical recipe. This section deals with restraining charioteers (lines 15-30). The instructions begin with the ritual drowning of a cat while reciting a formula to Sekhmet-Bastet, a "cat-faced" goddess mentioned in several other Egyptian magical texts.]*

Take the cat, and make <term>three lamellae</term><note>Thin metal plates, usually lead or gold, used for inscribing spells</note>, one for its anus, one for ..., and one for its throat; and write the formula <note>original: "logos"</note> [concerning the] deed on a clean sheet of papyrus, with <term>cinnabar ink</term><note>A bright red mineral pigment often used in high-stakes or underworld magic</note>, and [then the names of] the chariots and charioteers, and the chariot boards and the racehorses. Wind this around the body of the cat and bury it. Light seven lamps upon [7] unbaked bricks, and make an offering, fumigating <term>storax gum</term><note>A fragrant resin used as incense to attract spirits</note> to it, and be of good cheer. Take its body [and preserve] it by immuring it either in a tomb or in a burial place ... with colors, ... bury ... looking toward the sunrise, pour out (?) ..., saying:

> "Angel, ... [SEMEA], <term>chthonic</term><note>Relating to the underworld or the earth's depths</note> ... lord (?), grant [safety?], ... O chthonic one, in [the] horse race, IAKTORE; hold ... restrain ..., PHOKENSEPSEUARE[KTATHOUMISONKTAI] <note>These are 'voces magicae' or 'magical words'—strings of powerful, untranslatable sounds meant to compel spirits</note>, for me, the spirit ... the <term>daimon</term><note>A supernatural being or guiding spirit, not necessarily 'evil' in this context</note> of [the] place ... and may the [deed] come about for me immediately, immediately; quickly, quickly, because I conjure you, at this place and at this time, by the implacable god ... THACHOCHA EIN CHOUCHEOCH, and by the great chthonic god, ARIOR EUOR, and by the names that apply to you; perform the NN deed" (add the usual).

<summary>This passage describes a ritual involving the sacrifice of a cat and the use of inscribed metal plates to magically sabotage opponents in a chariot race. It invokes underworld deities and uses powerful "magical words" to ensure the spell's immediate success.</summary>
<keywords>chariot racing, binding spell, cat sacrifice, chthonic deities, voces magicae, Greek Magical Papyri, ancient sports magic</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the ritual instructions from P.G.M. III, following the preparation and burial of the cat. The practitioner now utilizes the "drowning water" to sanctify the site of the competition and activate the spirits.</meta>

Then take up the water in which the drowning took place, and sprinkle it [on] the <term>stadium</term><note>The hippodrome or racecourse where the chariot races were held</note> or in the place where you are performing [the rite].

***The formula to be spoken***, while you are sprinkling the drowning water, is as follows: 

> I call upon you, Mother of all men, you who have brought together the limbs of <term>Meliouchos</term><note>A powerful deity name often found in these papyri, likely referring to a solar or underworld figure whose myth involves being dismembered and restored</note>, even Meliouchos himself, OROBASTRIA NEBOUTOSOUALETH <note>These are "voces magicae"—strings of secret, powerful sounds meant to compel the gods</note>, Entrapper, Mistress of corpses, Hermes, Hekate, [Hermes?], <term>Hermekate</term><note>A "syncretic" or combined deity merging Hermes, the messenger, and Hekate, the goddess of magic, both of whom could walk between the worlds of the living and the dead</note>, LETH AMOUMAMOUTERMYOR <note>Additional magical sounds or names of power</note>; I conjure you, the <term>daimon</term><note>A supernatural spirit or power associated with a specific place, not necessarily evil</note> that has been aroused in this place, and you, the daimon of the cat that has been endowed with spirit; come to me on this very day and from this very moment, and perform for me the <term>NN</term><note>A shorthand used in magical manuals for "the name" (Latin: *nomen*); the practitioner would speak the actual name of their target here</note> deed" (add the usual, whatever you wish).

[*The process continues, with the word IAEO being written on the first and third <term>lamellae</term><note>Small, thin metal sheets, usually made of lead, used for inscribing curses or prayers</note> and a more elaborate <term>conjuration</term><note>A formal ritual command or spell used to summon spirits</note> spell being written on the second lamella.*]

# The Greek Magical Papyri: IV.1-25

This spell calls upon Egyptian and Jewish powers for the purpose of revelation <note>A "divine encounter" or "vision" where the practitioner seeks knowledge or a future-telling from a deity</note>. The text includes initial words of power, brief instructions for the use of the invocation, a series of greetings, and a concluding request for revelation. In syncretistic <note>The blending of different religious traditions, such as Greek, Egyptian, and Jewish beliefs</note> fashion, the invocation combines traditional Egyptian lore with other references. Part of this spell is paralleled in the Demotic <note>The late-stage Egyptian script and language used for everyday documents and some magical texts</note> papyrus PDM XIV.627-35.

->SAPHPHAIOR<-
->BAELKOTA KIKATOUTARA EKENNK LIX<-
->The great <term>daimon</term><note>A supernatural spirit or power, not necessarily evil in this context</note> and the inexorable one,<-
->...IPSENTANCHOUCHEOUCH<-

---

->DOOU SHAMAI ARABENNAK ANTRAPHEU BALE<-
->SITENGI ARTEN BENTEN AKRAB ENTH OUANTH<-
->BALA SHOUPLA SRAHENNE DEHENNE KALASHOU<-
->CHATEMMOK BASHNE BALA SHAMAI<-
<note>The preceding lines are "voces magicae"—ritualistic strings of secret sounds and names intended to command the attention of the gods</note>

->On the day of Zeus <note>original: "Dios"; referring to Thursday</note>, at the first hour,<-
->But on the [day] of deliverance, at the fifth hour,<-
->A cat <note>Likely referring to a ritual appearance of the spirit or the form the deity takes at this time</note><-
->At the eighth, a cat<-

->Hail, Osiris, king of the underworld,<-
->Lord of embalming,<-
->Who is south of Thinis <note>An ancient city in Upper Egypt, often associated with the earliest Pharaohs</note>,<-
->Who gives answer in Abydos <note>A major cult center for Osiris where he was believed to be buried; the "answer" refers to his oracular powers</note>,<-
->Who is under the <term>noubs tree</term><note>A species of jujube or Ziziphus tree, considered sacred in ancient Egyptian tradition</note> in Meroe <note>The capital city of the Kingdom of Kush, located in modern-day Sudan</note>,<-
->Whose glory is in Pashalom <note>Likely a geographical location or a corrupt form of a sacred name</note>.<-

<summary>This page contains a syncretic ritual invocation to Osiris, utilizing magical names of power and specific planetary timings to seek a divine vision or revelation.</summary>

Hail, Althabot <note>original: "Althabot"; a name likely derived from Gnostic or Jewish mystical traditions</note>,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Bring Sabaoth <note>original: "Sabaoth"; a Hebrew divine title meaning "Lord of Hosts," often personified in these spells as a powerful entity to be summoned</note> unto me.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;You are upon your mountain <note>An ancient Egyptian epithet for Anubis ("tepy-dju-ef"), referring to his role as a jackal-god guarding the desert hills of the necropolis</note>.
Hail, goddesses,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Thoth <note>The Egyptian god of wisdom and writing; though usually male, the text here addresses a collective or uses a flexible divine category</note> the great, the great, the wise <note>A Greek rendering of the Egyptian title for Thoth, which later became "Hermes Trismegistus" or "Thrice-Great Hermes"</note>.

For Akshha Shha is my name,
Sabashha is my true name
Shlot Shlot very valiant is my name <note>The magician identifies themselves with these secret, powerful names to assume a divine authority that the spirits must obey</note>
So let the one who is in the underworld
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Join the one who is in the air.
Let them arise, enter, and give answer to me
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Concerning the matter about which I ask them.

<meta>This page continues the rituals within PGM IV (the Great Magical Papyrus of Paris). It demonstrates the high degree of religious syncretism in 4th-century Egypt, blending Jewish patriarchs, the Christian figure of Jesus, and traditional Greek and Egyptian magical concepts to command and expel malevolent spirits.</meta>

# Greek Magical Papyri: IV.1227–64

*This text gives instruction for driving out a demon. After the opening sentence, the text indicates the words to be uttered, and the words to be addressed to the divine (lines 1231-39) are given in Coptic <note>A late Egyptian language written with the Greek alphabet, often used by early Egyptian Christians</note>. These Coptic words are the only Christian element in the text. The reference to "the seven" probably indicates the seven spheres of the sky <note>The seven "planets" of the ancient world: Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note>. Next, the text describes the procedures to bind and defeat the demonic force (lines 1248-52). Lastly, there are instructions for the preparation of an amulet. The utterances directed toward the amulet include permutations and verbal transformations based upon BOR and PHOR <note>original: "P-Hor"; likely the name of the Egyptian god Horus</note>. Other utterances refer to the Egyptian god Bes <note>A dwarf deity who protected the household and frightened away evil spirits</note> and the Greek word for favor <note>original: "charis"</note>.*

## Excellent spell for driving out demons:

Formula to be spoken over his head: Place the olive branches before him and stand behind him and say,

> "Hail <note>original: "chaire"; a standard greeting used to address gods in ritual</note>, God of Abraham; hail, God of Jacob; Jesus the upright <note>original: "Iesous chrestos"; though "Christos" means anointed, "Chrestos" means "good" or "upright," a common wordplay in early Christian era papyri</note>, the holy spirit, the son of the father, who is below the seven, who is within the seven. Bring Yao <note>original: "Yao"; a Greek rendering of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton, the name of God</note> Sabaoth <note>original: "Sabaoth"; from the Hebrew "Lord of Hosts"</note>; may your power issue forth from N. <note>A placeholder standing for the name of the person being healed</note>, until you drive away this unclean demon Satan, who is in him. I adjure you <note>original: "exorkizo"; the technical term for "I command by oath," the root of the word exorcism</note>, demon, whoever you are, by this god Sabarbarbathioth Sabarbarathiouth Sababarbathioneht Sabarbarbaphai <note>These are "voces magicae" or "words of power." They are rhythmic, non-translatable sounds believed to compel spirits by their vibration and secret meaning</note>. Come out, demon, whoever you are, and stay away from N., hurry, hurry, now, now! Come out, demon, since I bind you with unbreakable adamantine <note>In mythology, "adamant" was an indestructible metal or diamond-like substance used to create inescapable bonds</note> fetters, and I deliver you into the black chaos in perdition."

<summary>This page contains a syncretic exorcism ritual that invokes Jewish, Christian, and magical names to drive a demon from a possessed person. It features one of the few explicit mentions of Jesus and Satan within the Greek Magical Papyri collection.</summary>

<keywords>exorcism, Jesus, demon, Satan, Coptic, Yao Sabaoth, adamantine, Abraham, Jacob, Papyri Graecae Magicae</keywords>

It demonstrates the high degree of religious syncretism in 4th-century Egypt, blending Jewish patriarchs, the Christian figure of Jesus, and traditional Greek and Egyptian magical concepts to command and expel malevolent spirits.</meta>

Procedure: Take seven olive branches. For six of them, tie the two ends of each one together to form loops, but for the remaining one, use it as a whip as you utter the adjuration <note>A formal solemn oath or incantation used to command a spirit</note>. Keep it secret; it is proven <note>A common ancient Greek phrase "dokimon," indicating that the practitioner has found the spell to be effective</note>.

After driving out the demon, hang an amulet around the neck of "N." <note>A placeholder for the name of the person being treated</note>, which the patient puts on after the expulsion of the demon. Write these things on a tin metal leaf: 

->**BOR PHOR PHORBA PHOR PHORBA BES FAVOR <note>original: "charin"</note> BAUBA TE PHOR BORPHORBA PHORBABOR BAPHORBA PHABRAIE PHORBA PHARBA PHORPHOR PHORBA BOBORBORBA PAMPHORBA PHORPHOR PHORBA**<-

Protect "N."

# Greek Magical Papyri: IV.1716–1870

**The Sword of Dardanos:** A ritual known as the "Sword," which is unsurpassed in its power; it immediately subdues and attracts the soul <note>original: "psyche"</note> of whomever you desire. As you recite the incantation, also say:

> "I am bending to my will the soul of this person, NN <note>A placeholder for "Name of the Name," where the practitioner would speak the name of their target</note>."

Take a magnetic stone <term>lodestone: a naturally magnetized mineral used in ancient magic to "attract" people just as it attracts iron</term> which is "breathing" <note>This likely refers to a high-quality or "living" stone believed to possess its own spirit</note> and engrave an image of Aphrodite <note>The Greek goddess of love</note> sitting astride Psyche <note>The personification of the human soul</note>, holding Psyche’s curled hair with her left hand. Above her head, engrave: "ACHMAGE RARPEPSEI" <note>Magical words of power, or "voces magicae," which were believed to have inherent potency beyond their literal meaning</note>. Below the figures of Aphrodite and Psyche, engrave Eros <note>The god of desire, often depicted as a winged child</note> standing upon the vault of heaven, holding a blazing torch and burning the soul, Psyche. Below Eros, engrave these names: "ACHAPA, MY LORD <note>original: "Adonaie," a Hebrew title for God</note>, BASMA CHARAKO, JACOB <note>The biblical patriarch, often invoked in syncretic magic</note>, YAHWEH <note>original: "Iao," the Greek transcription of the Hebrew divine name</note>, E, PHARPHAREI."

On the reverse side of the stone, engrave Psyche and Eros embracing one another. Beneath Eros’s feet, engrave these letters: "SSSSSSSS," and beneath Psyche’s feet: "EEEEEEEEE" <note>Repetitive vowels and sibilants were used to represent the harmonious sounds of the cosmos or the Seven Planets</note>. Once the stone has been engraved and consecrated, use it in this manner: place it beneath your tongue, turn it toward the person you desire, and recite this spell:

> "I call upon you, the author of all creation, who spread your own wings over the entire world. You are the unapproachable and immeasurable one who breathes life-giving reason into every soul, you who fitted all things together by your power. You are the firstborn, the founder of the universe, the golden-winged one <note>A reference to Eros as a primordial creative force</note> whose light is darkness <note>A paradoxical description of the overwhelming and blinding nature of divine desire</note>, who shrouds rational thoughts and breathes forth a dark frenzy. You are the hidden one who secretly inhabits every soul. You create an unseen fire as you seize every living thing, never tiring of its torment; rather, you have delighted in pain from the moment the world began. Come also now and bring pain, you who are..."

<summary>This page describes the "Sword of Dardanos," a powerful attraction ritual involving a carved lodestone intended to bind a person's soul through the invocation of Aphrodite, Eros, and various Hebrew-derived names of power.</summary>

# Greek Magical Papyri: IV.1716–1870 (continued)

> "…sometimes reasonable, sometimes irrational, because of whom men dare beyond what is fitting and take refuge in your light which is darkness. Most headstrong, lawless, implacable, inexorable, invisible, bodiless, generator of frenzy, archer, torch-carrier, master of all living sensation and of everything clandestine, dispenser of forgetfulness, creator of silence, through whom the light and to whom the light travels, infantile when you have been engendered within the heart, wisest when you have succeeded; I call upon you, unmoved by prayer, by your great name:

->**AZARACHTHARAZA LATHA IATHAL Y Y Y LATHAI ATHA LLALAPH IOIOIO AI AI AI OUERIEU OIAI LEGETA RAMAI AMA RATAGEL** <note>These are "voces magicae" or magical words of power. They are often palindromic or rhythmic sounds believed to command cosmic forces</note><-

first-shining, night-shining, night-rejoicing, night-engendering, witness, **EREKISITHPHE ARARACHARARA EPHTHISIKERE IABEZEBYTH IT**, you in the depth, **BERIAMBO BERIAMBEBO**, you in the sea, **MERMERGO U**, clandestine and wisest, **ACHAPA** MY LORD <note>original: "Adonaie," a Greek transcription of the Hebrew word for "My Lord"</note> **MASMA CHARAKO** JACOB <note>The Hebrew patriarch</note> **IAO** <note>The Greek rendering of the Tetragrammaton, the name of the Hebrew God</note> **CHAROUER AROUER LAILAM SEMESILAM SOUMARTA MARBA KARBA MENABOTH EIIA**. Turn the ‘soul’ <note>original: "psyche"</note> of her, NN <note>A placeholder for the practitioner to insert the "Name of the Name" of the person they desire</note>, to me, NN <note>The practitioner's own name</note>, so that she may love me, so that she may feel passion for me, so that she may give me what is in her power. Let her say to me what is in her soul because I have called upon your great name."

And on a golden leaf <term>lamella: a thin sheet of metal used for writing spells or prayers to be buried or worn as amulets</term> inscribe this sword <note>The term "sword" refers to the name of this specific ritual and perhaps the pointed, piercing nature of the spell</note>: "One **THOURIEL MICHAEL GABRIEL OURIEL MISAEL IRRAEL ISTRAEL** <note>A list of archangels and divine names derived from Jewish tradition: Turiel, Michael, Gabriel, Uriel, Mishael, and Israel</note>: May it be a propitious day for this name and for me who know it and am wearing it. I summon the immortal and infallible strength of God. Grant me the submission of every soul for which I have called upon you." Give the leaf to a partridge to gulp down and kill it. Then pick...

# Greek Magical Papyri: IV.1833–1870 (continued)

...it up and wear it around your neck after inserting into the strip the herb called <term>boy-love<note>original: "philopais," likely referring to the herb *Potentilla* or "cinquefoil," which was used in ancient Greco-Roman magic for its supposed aphrodisiac properties</note></term>.

**The burnt offering** which endows Eros and the whole procedure with <term>soul<note>original: "psyche." In this context, the incense is believed to provide the "vital breath" or animating force that brings the ritual and the crafted objects to life</note></term> is this: <term>manna<note>A dried sap or resin used as incense</note></term>, 4 <term>drams<note>original: "drachmai," a unit of weight roughly equivalent to 4.3 grams</note></term>; <term>storax<note>A fragrant resin from the *Styrax* tree</note></term>, 4 drams; opium, 4 drams; myrrh, 4 drams; frankincense, <term>saffron bdella<note>A mixture of saffron and bdellium, a semi-transparent oleo-gum resin</note></term>, one-half dram each. Mix in a rich dried fig and blend everything in equal parts with fragrant wine, and use it for the performance. In the performance first make a burnt offering and use it in this way.

And there is also ***a rite for acquiring an assistant*** <note>original: "parhedros," a supernatural helper or familiar spirit bound to the magician to perform tasks</note>, who is made out of wood from a mulberry tree. He is made as a winged Eros wearing a cloak, with his right foot lifted for a stride and with a hollow back. Into the hollow put a gold leaf after writing with a <term>cold-forged copper stylus<note>A tool made by hammering copper without heat; the "cold" nature of the metal was often specified to maintain its ritual purity</note></term> so-and-so's name <note>The name of the intended target</note> [and]:

->**MARSABOUTARTHE** <note>A magical name of power or "vox magica" used to identify and command the spirit</note> — be my assistant and supporter and sender of dreams.<-

Go late at night to the house [of the woman] you want, knock on her door with the Eros figure and say: "Lo, she NN <note>A placeholder for "Name of the Name," where the practitioner speaks the woman's name</note> resides here; wherefore stand beside her and, after assuming the likeness of the god or <term>daimon<note>original: "daimon," a divine or semi-divine spirit that acts as an intermediary; not necessarily "demonic" in the modern sense</note></term> whom she worships, say what I propose." And go to your home, set the table, spread a pure linen cloth and seasonal flowers, and set the figure upon it. Then make a burnt offering to it and continuously say the spell of invocation. And send him, and he will act without fail. And whenever you bend her to your will with the stone, on that night it sends dreams, for on a different night it is busy with different matters.

...forged bronze <term>stylus<note>original: "stoma," literally "mouth," referring here to the sharp point or edge of an engraving tool</note></term> the names and the <term>character<note>original: "charaktēr," a magical sigil or non-linguistic symbol believed to contain inherent divine power</note></term>. # Greek Magical Papyri: VII.429–458

**A restraining <term>rite<note>original: "katádesmos," a binding spell or curse intended to "tie up" or restrict the actions of an opponent or an object.</note></term> for anything**, which even works against chariots <note>In the Roman and late-antique world, chariot racing was the most popular sport; fans and drivers frequently used lead "curse tablets" to hex opposing teams.</note>. It also causes enmity and sickness, cuts down, destroys, and overturns, for whatever you wish. This <term>spell<note>original: "logos," the spoken formula as opposed to the "praxis" or physical action.</note></term>, when recited, conjures <term>daimons<note>original: "daimones," intermediary spirits that were neither inherently good nor evil, but could be commanded by a skilled magician.</note></term> out and forces them to enter [objects or people].

Engrave your desired outcome onto a plate made of lead taken from a cold-water channel <note>Lead was the standard material for curses because it is heavy, "cold," and comes from deep within the earth, linking it to the powers of the underworld.</note>. When you have consecrated it with bitter aromatics such as myrrh, bdellium, <term>styrax<note>a fragrant resin from the Snowbell tree, used as incense.</note></term>, aloes, and thyme, along with river mud, perform the ritual late in the evening or in the middle of the night.

Find a place where there is a stream or the drain of a bath; having tied a cord to the plate, throw it into the stream—or into the sea—and let it be carried along. Use the cord so that, when you wish, you can undo the spell. If you wish to break the enchantment, simply untie the plate. Say the formula seven times and you will see something wonderful. Then go away without turning back or answering anyone <note>The "not turning back" rule was a common ritual taboo intended to prevent the summoned spirits from following the practitioner home.</note>. After you have washed and immersed yourself, go up to your own room and rest, eating only vegetable food. Write the spell with a headless bronze needle.

***The text to be written is:***

->“I <term>conjure<note>original: "horkizō," to bind an entity by the power of a divine name or oath.</note></term> you, lord Osiris, by your holy names: **OUCHIOCH OUSENARATH**, Osiris, **OUSERANNOUPHTHI OSORNOUPHE** <note>original: "Osor-Nofer," meaning "Osiris the Good Being."</note>, **Osiris-Mnevis** <note>Mnevis was the sacred bull of Heliopolis, considered the living incarnation of the god Osiris.</note>, **OUSERSETEMENTH AMARA MACHI CHOMASO EMMAI SERBONI EMER** Isis, **ARATOPHI ERACHAX ESEO IOTH ARBIOTHI AMEN CHNOUM** <note>Chnoum (Khnum) is the ram-headed Egyptian creator god who fashioned humans on a potter's wheel.</note> **MONMONT OUZATHI PER OUNNEPHER** <note>original: "Wennefer," an epithet of Osiris meaning "The One who remains perfect/good."</note> **EN OOO**. I give over to you, lord Osiris, and I deposit with you this matter:**”**

*[At this point, the practitioner would write the specific effect they wanted the spell to have and the name of the person it should target.]*

<summary>This ritual describes the creation of a binding curse using an engraved lead plate submerged in water to restrict or destroy an opponent. It invokes Osiris and other Egyptian-derived deities through powerful "magical names" to execute the practitioner's command.</summary>

<keywords>binding spell, katadesmos, Osiris, lead plate, chariot racing, ancient magic, PGM VII, ritual purity, voces magicae</keywords>

# Greek Magical Papyri: VII.429–458 (Continued)

But if you arrange for the plate to be buried or sunk into a river, the earth, the sea, a stream, a coffin, or even a well, write the secret Orphic words, saying: "ASKEI KAI TASKEI" <note>original: "ΑΣΚΕΙ ΚΑΙ ΤΑΣΚΕΙ"—these are 'voces magicae,' or powerful syllables believed to hold cosmic authority; they are part of the 'Ephesian Letters,' a famous set of protective magical words.</note>. 

Then, take a black thread and tie three hundred and sixty-five knots <note>The number 365 corresponds to the days of the solar year, intended to bind the target for every single day.</note> and wrap the thread around the outside of the plate. While doing so, repeat the same formula again and add: "Hold fast the one who is held" (or "bound"), or whatever specific command suits your purpose. 

In this manner, the plate is deposited. For Selene <note>The personification of the Moon, who acted as a witness and a judge in ancient magic.</note>, when she travels through the underworld, typically breaks any spell she discovers. However, when this specific ritual has been performed, the enchantment remains unbroken so long as you recite the formula daily at the spot where the plate is hidden. 

Do not share this information carelessly with anyone, for you will only find its equal through great labor.

<summary>This text describes the final steps of a binding ritual, involving knot-tying and lead-plate deposition to ensure a spell's permanence against the moon's purifying power.</summary>

# Selections from the Magical Papyri <note>original Latin: "Ecloga Ex Papyris Magicis"</note>: Book I

->by Johannes Opsopoeus<-

## Book I <note>original Latin: "Liber I"</note>

---

## Book I

> Shall we write about the things not to be spoken of?
> Shall we divulge the things not to be divulged?
> Shall we pronounce the things not to be pronounced?

->— Julian <note>Flavius Claudius Iulianus, the last pagan Roman Emperor (r. 361–363 CE), known as "the Apostate" for his attempt to revive traditional polytheism.</note>, Hymn to the Mother of the Gods<-

## Introduction

### *Origin of the Magical Papyri*

Although most of the magical papyri <note>The "Greek Magical Papyri" (Papyri Graecae Magicae, or PGM) are a collection of scrolls from Graeco-Roman Egypt containing spells, rituals, and hymns.</note> were discovered in Egypt in the nineteenth century and brought together as part of the Anastasi Collection <note>Giovanni Anastasi (1780–1860) was a merchant and diplomat who acquired a massive cache of papyri, likely from a single find in Thebes, which he later sold to major European museums.</note>, they were not completely published until 1925.

It is quite likely that many of the papyri come from a single source, perhaps a tomb or temple library, and it is commonly supposed that they were collected by a Theban Magician <note>A hypothetical figure—likely a scholar-priest—believed to have owned this private library of ritual power before it was buried for safekeeping.</note>. In any case, they are one of the best sources of Greco-Egyptian magic and religion, comparable to the Qumran scrolls <note>Also known as the Dead Sea Scrolls, which provided a massive leap in our understanding of ancient Judaism.</note> for Judaism and the Nag Hammadi library <note>A collection of early Christian and Gnostic texts discovered in Upper Egypt in 1945.</note> for Gnosticism. We are extremely lucky that they have survived, since magical books and scrolls were often systematically burned (Acts 19:19 <note>A New Testament passage describing early Christian converts in Ephesus burning their expensive "curious arts" books.</note>; but not just by the Christians: Augustus <note>The first Roman Emperor, who ordered the burning of over 2,000 prophetic and magical books to consolidate religious authority.</note> ordered 2,000 to be burned).

<term>Ecloga Ex Papyris Magicis</term> <note>Latin for "Selections from the Magical Papyri."</note>
<term>Johannes Opsopoeus</term> <note>The author/editor's name in Latin; John Opsopaus is a contemporary scholar of Hellenic magic.</note>
<term>Gnosticism</term> <note>A diverse group of ancient religious movements that emphasized "gnosis" (personal spiritual knowledge) over orthodox dogma.</note>

<summary>This page introduces a collection of ancient magical texts, explaining their 19th-century discovery in Egypt and comparing their historical importance to the Dead Sea Scrolls.</summary>
<keywords>Greek Magical Papyri, PGM, Anastasi Collection, Theban Magician, Emperor Julian, book burning, Greco-Egyptian magic, history of archeology</keywords>

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the historical background of the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM) to a practical guide for the reader, acting as a table of contents and a pronunciation guide for the spells that follow. It establishes the editorial methodology used by Johannes Opsopoeus, citing the landmark 1986 translation by Hans Dieter Betz.</meta>

# Contents

Here are the translations of a few spells, prayers, and other rituals that I thought might be useful or interesting. The information in [brackets] following each entry indicates the specific collection:
* **Greek Magical Papyri** <note>original: "PGM" or "Papyri Graecae Magicae"</note>
* **Demotic Magical Papyri** <note>original: "PDM" or "P. Demoticae M."</note>

Following these abbreviations, you will find the papyrus number and the specific lines where the spell is located. The primary source for these English translations is the work edited by Hans Dieter Betz, *The Greek Magical Papyri in Translation Including the Demotic Spells*.

The spells, prayers, and rituals are organized by category:

I. Protection
II. Divination and Visions
III. Self-Improvement
IV. Health and Healing
V. Craft
VI. Miscellaneous

# Pronunciation of Magical Names <note>original Latin: "voces magicae"</note>

In general, most of the <term>magical names</term> <note>original Latin: "voces magicae"; these are strings of syllables, secret names of deities, or "barbarous words" believed to possess inherent power when spoken aloud.</note> are written in Old Coptic <note>An early stage of the Coptic language, representing the Egyptian language written using the Greek alphabet along with several additional characters derived from Demotic.</note>, which used Greek letters, so you will do best if you think in terms of Greek phonetics. 

Thus, the letter **Y** <note>the Greek letter "Upsilon"</note> sounds like something between the English "u" and "y", similar to the German "u" with an umlaut (ü). Pronounce **CH** <note>the Greek letter "Chi"</note> as in the German "ach" or the Scottish "loch". The symbol **E'** represents the letter **eta** <note>the Greek letter Η</note>, so it should be pronounced like a long "a" (as in "gate"). The symbol **O'** represents the letter **omega** <note>the Greek letter Ω</note>, so it should be pronounced like a long "o" (as in "note"). 

The vowel pair (diphthong) **"OU"** is pronounced like the English "oo" (as in "moon"). The symbols **PH** <note>the Greek letter "Phi"</note> and **TH** <note>the Greek letter "Theta"</note> probably should be pronounced as aspirated "p" and "t" sounds, but they may have evolved to sound like the English "f" and "th" by the time these papyri were originally written. The symbol **"NN"** in a spell means

<summary>This page serves as a preface and instructional guide, categorizing the upcoming spells and providing a phonetic key for pronouncing the secret magical names (voces magicae) found in the Greco-Egyptian papyri.</summary>

"fill in the blank," generally with the name of the one on whose behalf the spell is cast (thus, "NN" or "the NN man"), or with the question or problem to which the spell is addressed (thus, "the NN matter").

# *I. PROTECTION*

## *i. Protective Spell*

Taking Sulfur and Seed of Nile Rushes, burn as Incense to the Moon and say, "I call on You, Lady Isis, whom the <term>Good Spirit</term> <note>original Greek: "Agathos Daimon"; a benevolent guardian divinity often depicted as a serpent.</note> permitted to rule in the entire Black Land <note>original: "Chemia"; the ancient name for Egypt, referring to the dark, fertile soil of the Nile valley.</note>. Your name is LOU LOULOU BATHARTHAR THARE'SIBATH ATHERNEKLE'SICH ATHERNEBOUNI E'ICHOMO' CHOMO'THI Isis Sothis <note>Isis identified with the star Sirius, whose rising heralded the Nile's flood.</note>, SOUE'RI, Boubastis <note>The cat-headed goddess Bastet.</note>, EURELIBAT CHAMARI NEBOUTOS OUE'RI AIE' E'OA O'AI. Protect me, Great and Marvelous Names of the God (add the usual [i.e., the protection you seek]); for I am the One Established in Pelusium <note>A major frontier city and port in the eastern Nile Delta.</note>, SERPHOUTH MOUISRO' STROMMO' MOLO'TH MOLONTHE'R PHON Thoth. Protect me, Great and Marvelous Names of the Great God! (add the usual) "ASO' EIO' NISAO'TH. Lady Isis, <term>Retribution</term> <note>original Greek: "Nemesis"; the goddess of divine vengeance and justice.</note>, <term>Inescapable Destiny</term> <note>original Greek: "Adrasteia"; a goddess associated with Nemesis who represents the unavoidable.</note>, Many-named, Many-formed, glorify me, as I have glorified the Name of Your Son Horus! (add the usual)" [PGM VII.490-504]

If you want someone to cease being angry with you, write with myrrh-based ink this magical name of anger: "CHNEO'M" <note>original: "CHNEO'M"; likely a Greek rendering of Khnum, the ancient Egyptian creator god often associated with water and pottery, here used as a name of power to 'mold' or suppress emotion.</note>. Hold the writing in your left hand and say:

[PGM XII.179-81]

Immediately, immediately! Quickly, quickly!" <-

### v. Protection Against a Punishing Spirit

If He <note>Referring to a <term>daimon</term>, a spirit or supernatural entity that carries out divine punishment.</note> comes forth, say to Him: 

> "I am Ereschigal <note>original: "Ereschigal"; the Greek version of Ereshkigal, the ancient Mesopotamian Queen of the Underworld.</note>, the One holding Her Thumbs, and not even one Evil can befall Her!" 

If, however, He comes close to you, take hold of your Right Heel and recite the following: 

> "Ereschigal, Virgin, Bitch, Serpent, Wreath, Key, Herald's Wand, Golden Sandal of the Lady of Tartaros <note>The deepest abyss of the underworld in Greek mythology, used here as the domain of the goddess.</note>!" 

And you will avert Him. 

> "ASKEI KATASKEI ERO'N OREO'N IO'R MEGA SAMNYE'R BAUI <note>These are the "Ephesian Letters," a famous set of ancient "words of power" often inscribed on amulets for protection.</note> (3 times) PHOBANTIA SEMNE' <note>original: "Phobantia Semne"; can be translated as "Revered Terrible One" or "Awe-inspiring One."</note>, I have been initiated, and I went down into the Underground Chamber of the Dactyls <note>The <term>Dactyls</term> were mythical archaic smiths and magicians associated with the Mother Goddess and secret initiations.</note>, and I saw the Other Things Down Below, Virgin, Bitch, and all the rest!" 

Say It at the Crossroad <note>Crossroads were considered thin places where the physical and spirit worlds met, sacred to goddesses of the night and underworld.</note>, and turn around and flee, because it is at those Places that She appears.

Saying It Late at Night, about what you wish, It will reveal it in your Sleep; and if you are led away to Death <note>Likely referring to a legal sentence or a mortal threat.</note>, say It while scattering Seeds of Sesame, and It will save you. [PGM LXX.4-19]

### vi. Indispensable Invisibility Spell

Take Fat or an Eye of a Nightowl and a Ball of Dung rolled by a Beetle and Oil of an Unripe Olive and grind them all together until smooth, and smear your Whole Body with it and say to Helios <note>The personification of the Sun.</note>: 

> "I adjure You by Your Great Name, BORKE' PHOIOUR IO' ZIZIA APARXEOUCH THYTHE LAILAM AAAAAA IIIII OOOO IEO' IEO' IEO' IEO' IEO' IEO' IEO' NAUNAX AI AI AEO' AEO' E'AO'!" <note>These strings of vowels and syllables are <term>voces magicae</term>, intended to resonate with the divine frequencies of the cosmos.</note> 

And moisten It and say in addition: 

> "Make me Invisible, Lord Helios, AEO' O'AE' EIE' E'AO', in the Presence of Any Man until Sunset, IO' IO' O' PHRIXRIZO' EO'A!" 

[PGM I.222-31]

### vii. Direct Vision Spell

"EEIM TO EIM ALALE'P BARBARIATH MENEBREIO ARBATHIAO'TH IOUE'L IAE'L OUE'NE'IIE MESOMMIAS <note>These are *voces magicae* (magic words); strings of syllables and secret names of power intended to compel spirits or deities. Many are derived from distorted Hebrew or Egyptian names for God.</note>, let the God who prophesies to me come and let Him not go away until I dismiss Him, OURNAOUR SOUL ZASOUL OUGOT NOOUMBIAOU THABRAT BERIAOU ACHTHIRI MARAI ELPHEO'N TABAO'TH KIRASINA LAMPSOURE' IABOE ABLAMATHANALBA <note>A famous magical palindrome meaning "Thou art our Father."</note> AKRAMMACHAMAREI!"

In a Bronze Cup over Oil. <note>A practice known as <term>lecanomancy</term>, where the practitioner gazes into a liquid—usually oil floating on water—to see visions.</note> Anoint your Right Eye with Water from a Shipwreck and the Left with Coptic Eyepaint <note>original: "stibi"; a dark mineral powder (kohl) used in Egypt as makeup and medicine.</note>, with the same Water. If you cannot find Water from a Shipwreck, then from a Sunken Skiff. [PGM V.54-69]

### viii. Request for a Dream Oracle

Take a Strip of Clean Linen and write on it the following Name. Roll it up to make a Wick, pour Pure Olive Oil over it and light it.

The Formula to be written is this: "HARMIOUTH LAILAM CHO'OUCH ARSENOPHRE' PHRE'U PHTHA HARCHENTECHTHA." <note>These names incorporate the Egyptian gods Phre (the Sun) and Ptah (the Creator).</note>

In the Evening then, when you are about to go to Sleep, being Pure in every respect, do this: Go to the Lamp, say 7 times the following Formula, extinguish the Light and go to Sleep.

The Formula to be spoken is as follows: "SACHMOUNE <note>original: "Sachmoune"; the Greek rendering of Sakhmet, the lion-headed Egyptian goddess of war and healing.</note> PAE'MALIGOTE'RE'E'NCH, the One who Shakes, who Thunders, who has Swallowed the Serpent, Surrounds the Moon, and at all times rouses the Soul in the Spirit of the Abyss..."

### ix. Spell for Revelation

[Addressed to the constellation of **Ursa Major** (the Great Bear)]: "KOMPHTHO KOMASITH KOMNOUN <note>Magical chants like these were believed to resonate with the cosmos; they are untranslatable strings of power-sounds.</note> You who shook and shake the world, you who have swallowed the ever-living serpent and daily raise the disk of the sun and of the moon, you whose name is **ITHIOO' E'I ARBATHIAO' E'**, send up to me, [Name], <note>The original text uses "NN" (deina) as a placeholder where the practitioner would insert their own name.</note> at night the <term>Daimon</term> <note>In this context, a "daimon" is a guardian spirit or supernatural intermediary, not an evil "demon" in the modern sense.</note> of this night to reveal to me concerning the [specific matter] <note>The text uses "NN" here again as a placeholder for the user's question or desired information.</note> I seek." [PGM IV.1323-30]

### x. Saucer Divination of Aphrodite

<note>This practice, known as lecanomancy, involves gazing into a liquid—here, a mixture of water and oil—to induce a trance or see visions of a deity.</note>

Having kept oneself pure for 7 days, take a white saucer, fill it with water and olive oil, having previously written on its base with **myrrh ink** <note>A ritual ink made from the resin of the myrrh tree, often mixed with burnt vine wood or soot.</note>: "E'IOCH CHIPHA ELAMPSE'R ZE'L A E E' I O Y O'" (25 letters); and beneath the base, on the outside: "Swift God of the Earth, Grasp!" <note>original: "TACHIEL CHTHONIE DRAXO". TACHIEL translates to "Swift God," CHTHONIE to "of the underworld/earth," and DRAXO to "Grasp."</note> (18 letters). Wax over the writing with white wax. On the outside of the rim at the top, write: "IERMI, I love... 6... Long-haired one, I see... I ask" <note>original: "IERMI PHILO 6 ERIKOMA DERKO MALOK GAULE APHRIEL." Portions of this appear to be Greek wordplay: "philo" means love, "erikoma" means long-haired, and "derko" means to see or watch.</note> (say it 3 times). Let the saucer rest on the floor and, looking intently at it, say: 

"I call upon you, the Mother and Mistress of Nymphs, **ILAOCH OBRIE' LOUCH TLOR**; come in, holy light, and give answer, showing your lovely shape!"

Then look intently at the bowl. When you see her, welcome her and say, "Hail, very glorious goddess, **ILARA OUCH**. And if you give me a response, extend your hand."

Put the Drinking Cup on your Knees and speak over it the Stele mentioned above, and the Goddess will appear to you and will reveal concerning what Things you wish. [PGM IV.3209-54]

### *III. SELF-IMPROVEMENT*

xi. Memory Spell

Take Hieratic Papyrus <note>A high-quality grade of papyrus used by priests for sacred texts.</note> and write the Prescribed Names with Hermaic Myrrh Ink <note>A ritual ink dedicated to Hermes, the god of language, writing, and hidden knowledge.</note>. ### xii. Another Memory Spell

Take a silver tablet and engrave it with the <term>Eye of Horus</term> <note>original: "Uzait Horu." The *wedjat* or Eye of Horus is an ancient Egyptian symbol representing healing, protection, and wholeness.</note> after <term>Helios</term> <note>The personification of the Sun.</note> sets. Take cow's milk and pour it <gloss>or perhaps heat it</gloss>. Put it into a clean vessel and place the tablet under it; add barley meal, mix, and form bread: twelve rolls in the shape of female figures. Say the formula three times, eat the rolls on an empty stomach, and you will know the power.

> AAAAAAAA IIIY E'I AI O'O'O'O'O'O'O' MOUMOU O'YIO' NAK
> NAK NAX LAINLIMM LAILAM AEDA . . . LAILAM AE'O O'AE'
> O'AE' E'OA' AO'E' E'OA' O'E'A, enter, Master, into my Mind, and
> grant me Memory, MMM E'E'E' MTHPH!" <note>These strings of rhythmic or repetitive syllables are known as *voces magicae* (magical words). They were not meant to be translated but rather vibrated or chanted as the secret languages of gods and spirits.</note>

Do this monthly, facing the Moon, on the First Day of the month. Prostrate yourself before the Goddess <note>original: "Thea"</note> [that is, <term>Selene<note>The personification of the Moon in Greek mythology, often called upon in rituals involving the night, cycles, and memory.</note></term>], and wear the Tablet as an Amulet. [PGM III.410-23]

### xiii. Spell for Strength

> "PHNOUNEBEE' (2 times), give me Your Strength, IO' <term>ABRASAX<note>A powerful solar deity frequently invoked in ancient magic; the letters of his name in Greek add up to 365, representing the days of the year.</note></term>, give me Your Strength, for I am <term>ABRASAX</term>!"

Say it 7 times while holding your two Thumbs. [PGM LXIX.1-3]

### xiv. Your Great Name, for Favor

> "Everyone fears Your Great Might. Grant me the Good Things: The Strength of AKRYSKYLOS, the Speech of EUO'NOS, the Eyes of Solomon <note>A reference to King Solomon, who in the ancient world was regarded as the ultimate master of magic and wisdom who could command spirits.</note>, the Voice of <term>ABRASAX</term>, the Grace of ADO'NIOS <... [PGM XXXVI.211-30]

### xv. Ritual for Business Success

<note>This text concludes a ritual involving a small statue of the god Hermes.</note> ...in his right hand a small Bag. Write on <term>Hieratic Papyrus<note>A high-quality paper used by Egyptian priests for sacred texts.</note></term> these Names, and you will see Continuous Business: "CHAIO'CHEN OUTIBILMEMNOUO'TH ATRAUICH. Give Income and Business to this place, because Psentebeth lives here." Put the Papyrus inside the Figure and fill in the hole with the same Beeswax. Then deposit in a wall, at an inconspicuous place, and crown Him on the outside, and sacrifice to Him a cock, and make a Drink Offering of Egyptian Wine, and light for Him a Lamp that is not colored Red. [PGM IV.2359-72]

### xvi. Spell for Assertiveness

> "Greetings, Lord, You who are the Means to obtain Favor for the Universe and for the Inhabited World. Heaven has become a Dancing Place for You, ARSENOPHRE', O King of the Heavenly Gods, ABLANATHANALBA <note>A famous magical palindrome meaning 'Thou art our Father'.</note>, You who possess Righteousness, AKRAMMACHAMAREI, Gracious God, SANKANTHARA, Ruler of Nature, SATRAPERKME'PH, Origin of the Heavenly World, ATHTHANNOU ATHTHANNOU ASTRAPHAI IASTRAPHAI PAKEPTO'TH PA . . . E'RINTASKLIOUTH E'PHIO' MARMARAO'TH! "Let my Outspokenness <note>original: "parrhesia," meaning the freedom or courage to speak one's mind boldly before authority.</note> not leave me. But let every Tongue and Language listen to me, because I am PERTAO' [ME'CH CHACH] MNE'CH SAKME'PH IAO'OYEE' O'E'O' O'E'O' IEOYO'E'IE'IAE'A IE'O'YOEI, Give me graciously whatever You want." [PGM XII.182-189]

### xix. Spell for Migraine Headache

Take Oil in your Hands and utter the Spell: 
> "Zeus sowed a Grape Seed: it parts the Soil; He does not sow it; it does not sprout."
<note>This is a "sympathetic magic" formula. By stating a series of impossible or negated agricultural events—that Zeus didn't actually sow the seed and it didn't grow—the magician commands the headache to likewise cease its "growth" and vanish from the head.</note>
[PGM VII.199-201]

### xx. Spell for Scorpion Sting

> "LIGHT LIGHT BEARER BEARER <note>original: "OR OR PHOR PHOR". These are roots derived from Greek and Egyptian terms for light and the act of carrying/bringing forth.</note> LORD OF HOSTS <note>original: "SABAO'TH". A Hebrew title for God frequently used in Greek magic to invoke supreme authority.</note> MY LORD <note>original: "ADO'NE". From the Hebrew "Adonai."</note> PEACE <note>original: "SALAMA". Likely related to the Semitic root for peace or safety, "Shalom."</note> TARCHEI <note>A magical name of power, possibly referring to a specific celestial protector.</note> ABRASAX <note>A powerful mystical name in late antiquity; its Greek letters add up to 365, representing the days of the year and the totality of the heavens.</note>, I bind you, Scorpion of <term>Artemisia<note>Likely referring to the goddess Artemis in her role as a protector against wild creatures, or perhaps the plant of the same name used in the remedy.</note></term>, three-hundred and fifteen times, on the fifteenth day of <term>Pachon<note>The ninth month of the Egyptian calendar, roughly corresponding to late April and May, a time when scorpions become more active as the weather warms.</note></term> . . ." [PGM XXVIIIa.1-7]

### xxi. A Contraceptive, the Only One in the World

Take as many Bittervetch <note>A type of legume (Vicia ervilia) often used in ancient medicine and livestock feed.</note> Seeds as you want for the Number of Years you wish to remain Sterile. Steep them in the Menses of a Menstruating Woman. Let them steep in her own Genitals. And take a Frog that is alive and throw the Bittervetch Seeds into its Mouth so that the Frog swallows them, and release the Frog alive at the place where you captured him. 

And take a Seed of Henbane <note>A highly toxic plant in the nightshade family used in ancient times for its sedative and hallucinogenic properties.</note>, steep it in Mare's Milk; and take the Nasal Mucus of a Cow, with Grains of Barley, put these into a Leather Skin made from a Fawn and on the outside bind it up with <term>Mulehide Skin<note>The use of a mule is highly symbolic in "sympathetic magic." Because a mule is the sterile offspring of a horse and a donkey, ancient magicians believed that parts of the animal—or even its earwax—could magically transfer that sterility to a human.</note></term>, and attach it as an Amulet during the Waning of the Moon in a Female Sign of the Zodiac on a Day of <term>Kronos or Hermes<note>Known today as Saturn or Mercury. Saturn was associated with restriction and boundaries, making it "ideal" for a spell intended to stop a natural process like conception.</note></term>. Mix in also, with the Barley Grains, <term>Cerumen<note>Earwax.</note></term> from the Ear of a Mule. [PGM XXXVI.320-32]

<summary>This page lists diverse magical remedies for physical ailments including coughs, migraines, and scorpion stings, alongside an elaborate ritual for long-term contraception. These spells utilize sympathetic magic, such as using sterile animals (mules) to induce sterility in humans, and the invocation of syncretic deities like Abrasax.</summary>

### xxii. A Prescription to Stop Blood

Juice of the "Great-Nile" plant <note>The identity of this plant is debated; it may refer to a species of heliotrope or a specific marsh plant used in Egyptian medicine.</note> mixed together with beer; you should make the woman drink it at dawn before she has eaten. It stops the bleeding. 
[<term>PDM<note>Abbreviation for *Papyri Demoticae Magicae*, the Demotic Magical Papyri.</note></term> xiv.953-5]

### xxiii. A Way to Know if a Woman will be Pregnant

You should make the woman urinate on this plant, the one mentioned above <note>The "Great-Nile" plant.</note>, at night. When morning comes, if you find the plant scorched, she will not conceive. If you find it green, she will conceive. 
[PDM xiv.956-60]

## V. *CRAFT*

### xxiv. Spell for Picking a Plant

Use it before sunrise. The spell to be spoken:

> "I am picking you, such and such a plant, with my five-fingered hand, I, <term>NN<note>An abbreviation for the Latin *Nomen Nominandum*, meaning "the name to be named." The practitioner would insert their own name here.</note></term>, and I am bringing you home so that you may work for me for a certain purpose. I adjure you by the undefiled name of the God: if you pay no heed to me, the earth which produced you will no longer be watered as far as you are concerned—ever in life again, if I fail in this operation: MOUTHABAR NACH BARNACHO'CHA BRAEO' MENDA LAUBRAASSE PHASPHA BENDEO' <note>original: "MOUTHABAR...". These are *Voces Magicae* (Magic Words). They are strings of untranslatable syllables believed to be the secret language of gods or spirits, used here to bind the plant’s power to the magician's will.</note>; fulfil for me the perfect charm!" 
[<term>PGM<note>Abbreviation for *Papyri Graecae Magicae*, the Greek Magical Papyri.</note></term> IV.286-95]

### xxv. Procedure for Obtaining Herbs

Among the Egyptians, herbs are always obtained like this: the herbalist first purifies his own body, then sprinkles with <term>natron<note>A naturally occurring salt (sodium carbonate decahydrate) harvested from dry lake beds in Egypt, used extensively for ritual cleansing and mummification.</note></term>...

<summary>This page describes a gynecological remedy and a pregnancy test using the "Great-Nile" plant, followed by a ritual incantation for the sacred harvesting of magical herbs. It emphasizes the necessity of ritual purity and the use of powerful, non-human language to command the natural world.</summary>

<keywords>gynecology, pregnancy test, Great-Nile plant, herbalism, Voces Magicae, ritual purity, natron, Egyptian magic</keywords>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Spell for Picking a Plant" (Section xxiv) from the Greek Magical Papyri. Having already addressed the initial address to the plant and the use of magic words (Voces Magicae), the text now describes the physical ritual of fumigation and libation, followed by a secondary, more elaborate invocation that identifies the plant with the entire Egyptian and Greek pantheon.</meta>

...and fumigates the Herb with Resin from a Pine Tree after carrying it around the Place 3 times. Then, after burning Kyphi <note>A famous and complex incense used in Egyptian temples, typically made from sixteen ingredients including honey, wine, raisins, myrrh, and juniper berries.</note> and pouring the Libation of Milk as he prays, he pulls up the Plant while invoking by Name the Daimon <note>original: "daimon." In this context, a mediating spirit or divine power that governs the plant’s efficacy.</note> to whom the Herb is being dedicated and calling upon Him to be more effective for the Use for which it is being acquired.

The Invocation for him, which he speaks over any Herb, generally at the Moment of Picking, is as follows:

> "You were sown by Kronos <note>The god of time and agriculture.</note>, you were conceived by Hera <note>Queen of the gods.</note>, you were maintained by Ammon <note>The Egyptian creator god Amun.</note>, you were given birth by Isis <note>The Egyptian goddess of magic and motherhood.</note>, you were nourished by Zeus the God of Rain, you were given growth by Helios <note>The Sun.</note> and Dew. You are the Dew of all the Gods, you are the Heart of Hermes <note>The messenger god and patron of magic.</note>, you are the Seed of the Primordial Gods, you are the Eye of Helios, you are the Light of Selene <note>The Moon.</note>, you are the Zeal of Osiris <note>The Egyptian god of the afterlife.</note>, you are the Beauty and Glory of Ouranos <note>The Sky.</note>, you are the Soul of Osiris' Spirit which revels in Every Place, you are the Spirit of Ammon. As you have exalted Osiris, so exalt yourself and rise just as Helios rises each day. Your size is equal to the Zenith of Helios, your Roots come from the Depths, but your Powers are in the Heart of Hermes, your Fibers are the Bones of Mnevis <note>original: "Mr-wr." The sacred bull of Heliopolis, regarded as an oracle and an avatar of the sun god.</note>, and your Flowers are the Eye of Horus, your Seed is Pan's Seed. I am washing you in Resin as I also wash the Gods <note>A reference to the ritual cleaning and anointing of cult statues in temples.</note> even as I do this for my own Health. You also be cleaned by Prayer and give us Power as Ares and Athena do. I am Hermes! <note>The practitioner uses the "Identity Formula," claiming to be the god himself to command the plant's power.</note> I am acquiring you with Good Fortune and Good Spirit both at a Propitious Hour and on a Propitious Day that is effective for all things."

<summary>This text details the ritual protocols for harvesting a magical plant, including fumigation with pine resin and a poetic invocation that links the plant's biology to various Greek and Egyptian deities. The practitioner concludes by ritually identifying as the god Hermes to ensure the herb's potency.</summary>

<keywords>herbal magic, ritual harvesting, Kyphi, Mnevis, Hermes, Osiris, botanical invocation, Papyri Graecae Magicae</keywords>

--- Page 37 ---
<meta>This page concludes the ritual instructions for picking a magical herb and introduces a significant lexicographical list. It bridges the transition from physical ritual actions—such as the symbolic "payment" to the earth for the harvested plant—to the secret "Decknamen" or code names used by Egyptian temple scribes to hide the true nature of their ingredients from the uninitiated.</meta>

After saying this, he rolls the Harvested Stalk in a **Pure Linen Cloth** (but into the place of its Roots they threw seven Seeds of Wheat and an equal number of Barley, after mixing them with Honey <note>This act serves as a ritual "replacement" or payment to the Earth for taking the life of the plant.</note>), and after pouring in the Ground which has been dug up, he departs. [PGM IV.2967-3006]

## xxvi. Interpretations of Herbs and Other Ingredients

Which the **Temple Scribes** employed, from the Holy Writings, in translation. Because of the Curiosity of the Masses they <insert>the scribes</insert> inscribed the Names of the Herbs and Other Things which they employed on the Statues of the Gods, so that they <insert>the masses</insert>, since they do not take Precaution, might not practice Magic, <note>The scribes intentionally used "frightening" or bizarre names—like "Lion Semen"—to scare off casual practitioners and prevent dangerous mistakes by those without proper training.</note> [being prevented] by the Consequence of their Misunderstanding. But we have collected the explanations from many Copies, all of them **Secret**.

Here they are:

| Secret Name | True Ingredient |
| :--- | :--- |
| A Snake's Head | A Leech |
| A Snake's Ball of Thread | This means **Soapstone** |
| Blood of a Snake | **Hematite** <note>A reddish-black iron ore; the name derives from the Greek word for "blood."</note> |
| A Bone of an Ibis | This is **Buckthorn** |
| Blood of a Hyrax | Truly of a Hyrax <note>A small, furry mammal (Procavia capensis) native to Africa and the Middle East, often associated with rocky landscapes.</note> |
| Tears <gloss>Sleep Sand</gloss> of a **Hamadryas Baboon** | Dill Juice <note>The Hamadryas baboon was sacred to Thoth, the Egyptian god of wisdom and magic.</note> |
| Crocodile Dung | **Ethiopian Soil** |
| Blood of a Hamadryas Baboon | Blood of a **Spotted Gecko** |
| Lion Semen | **Human Semen** |
| Blood of Hephaistos | **Wormwood** <note>Hephaistos was the Greek god of fire and the forge; Wormwood is a bitter herb used both medicinally and in ritual fumigation.</note> |
| Hairs of a Hamadryas Baboon | **Dill Seed** |

<keywords>ritual sacrifice, temple scribes, Decknamen, ingredient substitutions, Hamadryas baboon, botanical magic, PGM IV, ancient cryptography</keywords>

| Spice of Kronos | Piglet's Milk <note>Likely another milky plant sap rather than animal milk.</note> |
| A Lion’s Hairs | Tongue of a Turnip <note>A descriptive term for the long, slender leaves of the turnip plant.</note> |
| Blood of Kronos | . . . of Cedar <note>The text is damaged here, likely referring to cedar resin or oil.</note> |

| From the Belly | Earth-apple <note>Likely referring to a tuber, such as a cyclamen bulb.</note> |
| From the Foot | Houseleek |

<summary>This page provides a Greek-Egyptian glossary of "Decknamen," or code names, used to disguise botanical and mineral ingredients in magical papyri. It reveals that dramatic terms like "Blood of a Titan" or "Seed of Hermes" refer to common items like wild lettuce and dill.</summary>

## VI. *Miscellaneous*

### xxvii. Prayer to Selene for Any Spell

[Since several aspects of this ritual are contrary to modern Pagan and Wiccan ethics and practice, I had some misgivings about including it in this collection, but decided to do so, because the hymn is so beautiful, so moving and so empowering. It has been discussed by K. Kerenyi, "The Goddess Nature," <note>original: "Die Goettin Natur"</note> *Eranos-Yearbook* 14 (1947), 39-86.]

> "Come to me, O Beloved Mistress, Three-faced
> Selene <note>The Greek personification of the Moon, often depicted with three heads or forms in magical papyri to show her power over heaven, earth, and the underworld</note>; kindly hear my Sacred Chants;
> Night's Ornament, young, bringing Light to Mortals,
> O Child of Morn who ride upon the Fierce Bulls <note>Ancient poets often described the Moon's chariot as being pulled by bulls, whose horns mirrored the crescent moon</note>,
> O Queen who drive Your Car <note>A poetic term for a chariot</note> on Equal Course
> With Helios <note>The Sun god</note>, who with the Triple Forms
> Of Triple Graces <note>The Charites: goddesses of charm, beauty, and human creativity</note> dance in Revel with
> The Stars. You're Justice and the Moira's <note>Fate</note> Threads:
> Klotho and Lachesis and Atropos <note>The three Fates who spin, measure, and cut the thread of human life</note>
> Three-headed, You're Persephone <note>Queen of the Dead</note>, Megaira,
> Allekto <note>Two of the Furies or Erinyes, spirits of divine vengeance and social order</note>, Many-Formed, who arm Your Hands
> With Dreaded, Murky Lamps, who shake Your Locks

## VI. *Miscellaneous*

### xxvii. Prayer to Selene for Any Spell

[Since several aspects of this ritual are contrary to modern Pagan and Wiccan ethics and practice, I had some misgivings about including it in this collection, but decided to do so, because the hymn is so beautiful, so moving and so empowering. It has been discussed by Karl Kerényi in "The Goddess Nature" <note>original: "Die Goettin Natur"</note>, *Eranos-Yearbook* 14 (1947), 39-86.]

> "Come to me, O Beloved Mistress, Three-faced
> Selene <note>The Greek personification of the Moon, often depicted with three heads or forms in magical papyri to show her power over heaven, earth, and the underworld</note>; kindly hear my Sacred Chants;
> Night's Ornament, young, bringing Light to Mortals,
> O Child of Morn who ride upon the Fierce Bulls <note>Ancient poets often described the Moon's chariot as being pulled by bulls, whose horns mirrored the crescent moon</note>,
> O Queen who drive Your Car <note>A poetic term for a chariot</note> on Equal Course
> With Helios <note>The Sun god</note>, who with the Triple Forms
> Of fearful Serpents on Your Brow, who sound
> The Roar of Bulls out from Your Mouths, whose Womb
> Is decked out with the Scales of Creeping Things,
> With Pois'nous Rows of Serpents down the Back,
> Bound down Your Backs with Horrifying Chains
> Night-Crier, Bull-faced, loving Solitude,
> Bull-headed, You have Eyes of Bulls, the Voice
> Of Dogs; You hide Your Forms in Shanks of Lions <note>This describes the goddess’s "theriomorphic" or animal-shaped aspects, common in ancient magic where the divine was seen in the wild power of beasts</note>,
> Your Ankle is Wolf-shaped, Fierce Dogs are dear
> To You, wherefore they call You Hekate <note>A goddess of boundaries, ghosts, and the "triple-ways" or crossroads</note>,
> Many-named, Mene <note>An ancient name for the Moon as the measurer of time</note>, cleaving Air just like
> Dart-shooter Artemis, Persephone,
> Shooter of Deer, night shining, triple-sounding,
> Triple-headed, triple-voiced Selene
> Triple-pointed, triple-faced, triple-necked,
> And Goddess of the Triple Ways, who hold
> Untiring Flaming Fire in Triple Baskets,
> And You who oft frequent the Triple Way
> And rule the Triple Decades <note>A reference to the thirty days of the lunar month</note>, unto me
> Who'm calling You be gracious and with Kindness
> Give Heed, You who protect the Spacious World
> At night, before whom <term>Daimons</term> <note>Intermediate spirits that act as messengers or agents between gods and humans; they were not necessarily evil in the ancient world</note> quake in Fear
> And Gods Immortal tremble, Goddess who
> Exalt Men, You of Many Names, who bear
> Fair Offspring, Bull-eyed, Horned, Mother of Gods
> And Men, and Nature, Mother of All Things,
> For You frequent Olympos, and the broad
> And boundless Chasm <note>The abyss or the Underworld</note> You traverse. Beginning
> And End are You, and You Alone rule All."

<summary>This page concludes a powerful liturgical hymn to the Moon goddess, invoking her through various animalistic and "triple" forms to demonstrate her absolute sovereignty over all realms. It serves as a universal prayer intended to empower any magical operation.</summary>
