<meta>This page transitions from the "Prayer to Selene" (PGM IV.2785-2890) into the practical ritual instructions—the "Praxis"—required to complete the rite. The previous page ended mid-sentence during a description of the goddess’s power over the underworld.</meta>

...and You keep **<term>Kerberos</term>** <note>The three-headed watchdog of the underworld</note> in check with heavy, unbreakable chains.

### Offering for The Rite
For doing **Good**, offer **<term>Storax</term>** <note>A fragrant resin collected from trees, commonly used as incense in religious and magical rituals</note>, Myrrh, Sage, Frankincense, and a Fruit Pit. But for doing **Harm**, offer the magical material of a **Dog** and a **Dappled Goat** (or, in a similar way, material from a **<term>Virgin Untimely Dead</term>** <note>In ancient magic, the spirits of those who died young or violently—the *aoroi*—were believed to remain near the physical world and could be more easily commanded by a sorcerer</note>).

### Protective Charm for The Rite
Take a **<term>Lodestone</term>** <note>A naturally magnetized piece of mineral magnetite, believed to possess a 'living' spirit capable of attracting or binding</note> and have a **Three-faced Hekate** carved upon it. Let the **Middle Face** be that of a **Maiden wearing Horns**, the **Left Face** that of a **Dog**, and the **One on the Right** that of a **Goat**. After the carving is finished, clean the stone with **<term>Natron</term>** <note>A natural salt used extensively in purification rites and mummification</note> and Water, and dip it in the **Blood of One who has died a Violent Death**. Then make a Food Offering to it and recite the same spell at the time of the ritual.

## xxviii. Love Spell
The name of **Aphrodite**, which is quickly made known to no one, is **NEPHERIE’RI** <note>original: "Νεφεριήρι". This is a Greek transcription of the Egyptian phrase *Nfr-iry.t*, meaning "The Beautiful Eye," a title for the goddess Hathor, whom the Greeks equated with Aphrodite</note>—this is the Name. If you wish to win a woman who is beautiful, remain **Pure** <note>This refers to ritual purity, usually requiring abstinence from sex and certain foods like meat or garlic</note> for 3 days, make an offering of **Frankincense**, and call this Name over it. You should approach the woman and say it **seven times** in your Soul <note>meaning silently, within one's mind</note> as you gaze at her, and in this way it will succeed. But do this for 7 days.

## xxix. To be Able to Eat Garlic and Not Stink
Bake **Beetroots** and eat them.

## xxx. For Those Who Have Difficulty Intermingling
<note>This heading likely refers to a spell for social grace or sexual potency, though the full recipe is not provided on this fragment.</note>

# Socializing

### xxx. To Present Yourself Well
<note>This refers to improving one's social standing or attractiveness in public gatherings</note>
Take **Gum** <note>Likely gum arabic, a resin from acacia trees commonly used in ancient cosmetics</note> mixed with **Wine** and **Honey** and smear it on your face.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.179-80</note>

### xxxi. To be Able to Drink a Lot and Not Get Drunk
<note>Excessive drinking was a staple of ancient social life, and remaining sober while others succumbed to wine was seen as a mark of self-control and superiority</note>
Eat a baked **Pig's Lung**.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.181</note>

### xxxii. To be Able to Copulate a Lot
Grind up fifty **Tiny Pinecones** <note>Pine seeds and cones were frequently associated with fertility and the god Dionysus</note> with 2 ounces of **Sweet Wine** and two **Pepper Grains** and drink it.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.184-5</note>

### xxxiii. To Get an Erection When You Want
Grind up a **Pepper** with some **Honey** and coat your **<term>Phallus</term>** <note>The male reproductive organ, considered in ancient magic to be a focal point of generative power and vitality</note>.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.186</note>

### xxxiv. Love Salve
. . . <note>The ellipsis indicates where the original papyrus is damaged or illegible</note> **Hawk's Dung**; **Salt**, **Reed**, **Bele Plant** <note>original: "Bele". This likely refers to Henbane (*Hyoscyamus niger*), a powerful psychoactive plant used in ancient medicine and sorcery</note>. Pound these ingredients together. Anoint your **<term>Phallus</term>** with the mixture and lie with the woman. If the mixture is dry, you should pound a little of it with **Wine**, anoint your **<term>Phallus</term>** with it, and lie with the woman. This is very good.
<note>original reference: PDM xiv.1155-62</note>

->*** FINIS ***<-
<note>Latin for "The End"</note>

# Selections from the Magical Papyri: Book II <note>original Latin: "Ecloga Ex Papyris Magicis: Liber II"</note>

->by John Opsopaus<- <note>original Latin: "a Johanne Opsopoeo"</note>

## Book II

> 'Tis true : There's Magicke in the web of it:
> A Sybill, that had numbred in the world
> The Sun to course, two hundred compasses,
> In her Prophetticke furie sow'd the Worke:
> The Wormes were hallowed, that did breede the Silke,
> And it was dyde in Mummey, which the Skilfull
> Conserv'd of Maidens hearts.

-- Shakespeare, Othello, III.iv.70

### Introduction

*Contents*

This is the second book of spells, charms, prayers, rituals etc. from the Greek magical papyri and Demotic spells. I have selected items that seem interesting and useful. Unfortunately, many of the papyri are very fragmentary and incomplete, and others make use of complicated magical symbols and figures that are hard to represent so I have had to omit some otherwise worthwhile spells.

<note>original vocabulary: Selections from the Magical Papyri, John Opsopaus, Othello, Greek magical papyri, Demotic spells, magical symbols</note>

# Socializing

### xxx. To Present Yourself Well
<note>This refers to improving one's social standing or attractiveness in public gatherings, essentially a "favor" spell to ensure a positive reception from others</note>

<note>original reference: PGM VII.179-80</note>

### xxxi. To be Able to Drink a Lot and Not Get Drunk
<note>Excessive drinking was a staple of ancient social life and symposia; remaining sober while others succumbed to wine was seen as a mark of self-control and intellectual superiority</note>
Eat a baked **Pig's Lung**.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.181</note>

### xxxii. To be Able to Copulate a Lot
Grind up fifty **Tiny Pinecones** <note>Pine seeds and cones were frequently associated with fertility and the god Dionysus, representing generative power</note> with 2 ounces of **Sweet Wine** and two **Pepper Grains** and drink it.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.184-5</note>

### xxxiii. To Get an Erection When You Want
Grind up a **Pepper** with some **Honey** and coat your **<term>Phallus</term>** <note>The male reproductive organ, considered in ancient magic to be a focal point of generative power and vitality</note>.
<note>original reference: PGM VII.186</note>

### xxxiv. Love Salve

This likely refers to Henbane (Hyoscyamus niger), a powerful and toxic psychoactive plant used in ancient medicine and sorcery to induce altered states</note>. Pound these ingredients together. Anoint your **<term>Phallus</term>** with the mixture and lie with the woman. If ...

and charms. For these I suggest consulting Betz (below), which contains nearly 600 spells, charms, prayers, invocations, etc.

The information in [brackets] following each selection indicates the collection (PGM = Greek Magical Papyri <note>original Latin: Papyri Graecae Magicae</note>, PDM = Demotic Magical Papyri <note>original Latin/Greek: P. Demoticae M.</note>), the papyrus number and the lines where the spell can be found. The source for these translations is Hans Dieter Betz (ed.), *The Greek Magical Papyri in Translation Including the Demotic Spells*, Chicago: The University of Chicago Press, 1986.

The spells, prayers, etc. are organized by category:

I. Protection
II. Divination and Visions
III. Self-Improvement
IV. Health and Healing
V. Craft
VI. Miscellaneous

### *Pronunciation of Magical Names*

In general, most of the "magical words" <note>original Latin: "voces magicae"; these are strings of syllables believed to hold power precisely because they were untranslatable or foreign</note> (magical names) are written in Old Coptic, which used Greek letters, so you will do best if you think Greek. Thus, Y sounds between English "u" and "y", something like German umlauted "u". Pronounce CH as in German "ach" or Scotch "loch". The symbol E' represents eta, so pronounce like a long "a"; O' represents omega, so pronounce like a long "o". The symbols PH (phi) and TH (theta) probably should be aspirated "p" and "t", but may have been pronounced like English "f" and "th" by the time the papyri were written. The symbol "NN" in a spell means "fill in the blank," generally with the name of the person being addressed or the practitioner themselves.

...on whose behalf the spell is cast (thus, "NN" or "the NN man"), or with the question or problem to which the spell is addressed (thus, "the NN matter").

## *I. PROTECTION*

### i. Charm to Break Enchantment

Taking a Three-Cornered Sherd <note>a piece of broken pottery, commonly used as a writing surface for temporary or low-cost documents in antiquity</note> from a Fork in the Road — pick it up with your Left Hand — inscribe it with Myrrhed Ink <note>ink made with soot or gall nuts and scented or consecrated with myrrh resin</note> and hide it. [Write:]

> "ASSTRAELOS CHRAELOS <note>these are *voces magicae*, or "magical words"—strings of syllables believed to carry divine power through their specific sound even if their literal meaning is obscure</note>, dissolve every Enchantment against me, NN, for I conjure You by the Great and Terrible Names which the Winds fear and the Rocks split when they hear it."

[There are seven Symbols <note>these "charakteres" are occult signs believed to be the secret signatures of spiritual beings; they were common in the Late Antique magical tradition</note> to be written on the Sherd; they look like this: (1) a Greek capital upsilon (like a Y with sagging arms), with small circles at all three ends; (2) an X with small circles on the ends; (3) a squared-off U with small circles at the ends of the arms; (4) a line inclined to the right, with a small right-angle bend to the right at the end, small circles at both ends; (5) a line inclined to the right, small circles on ends, but slightly to the left; (6) arc or rounded L from upper left to lower right, circles on ends; (7) An A with circles on its feet. The whole sign looks something like this: Y X U / \ ' ) L A] [PGM XXXVI.256-64 <note>original Latin/Greek: Papyri Graecae Magicae, a standard modern reference for this collection of ancient spells</note>]

### ii. Charm to Restrain Anger and Charm for Success

(No Charm is Greater, and it is to be performed by means of Words Alone.) Hold your Thumbs and repeat the Spell 7 times:

> "ERMALLO'TH ARCHIMALLO'TH stop the Mouths that speak against me, because I glorify Your Sacred and Honored Names which are in Heaven."

<term>PGM</term>: <note>Greek Magical Papyri; a collection of spells, hymns, and rituals from Graeco-Roman Egypt.</note>
<term>Enchantment</term>: <note>A magical influence or curse cast by another person.</note>
<term>Sherd</term>: <note>A broken fragment of pottery used as a ritual tool.</note>
<term>ASSTRAELOS / CHRAELOS / ERMALLO'TH / ARCHIMALLO'TH</term>: <note>Traditional magical names or "voces magicae" used to invoke specific protective forces.</note>
<term>Protection charm</term>: <note>A ritual intended to guard an individual from spiritual or social harm.</note>

<summary>This page provides two ancient protection rituals: one using inscribed pottery to break existing curses and another using spoken names to silence critics and ensure favor.</summary>
<keywords>protection magic, Greek Magical Papyri, voces magicae, folk rituals, enchantment, ancient spells, ritual tools</keywords>

### ii. To Increase the Power of Words
Take a piece of Papyrus <note>an early form of paper made from the pith of the papyrus plant</note> and write as follows:

> "I am Khepri <note>original: "CHPHYRIS"; the Egyptian creator god often depicted as a scarab beetle, representing transformation and the rising sun</note>. I must be successful! Michael, Raphael, Roubel, Nariel, Kattiel, Roumbourthiel, Azariel, Joel, Jouel, Ezriel, Souriel, Nariel, Metmouriel, Azael, Aziel, Saoumiel, Roubouthiel, Rabiel, Rabiel, Rabchlou, Enaezrael—Angels, protect me from every bad situation that comes upon me!" <note>This list combines traditional archangels with "voces magicae," or secret magical names intended to compel divine attention.</note>

### iii. Spell to Catch a Thief

[Although this spell is coercive <note>meaning it seeks to force someone's will through magic</note>, I have included it because it is fundamentally defensive.] Take the *Chelkbei* plant <note>an unidentified medicinal herb mentioned in several ancient papyri</note> and Bugloss <note>a plant of the borage family, historically used in medicine and dyes</note>, strain them, and burn the remains. Mix the ashes well with the juice and use it to write "*CHOO*" <note>a word of power</note> on a wall. Take wood from a gallows <note>wood from a place of execution was believed to hold residual "restless" energy, making it potent for aggressive spells</note> and carve a hammer from it. With the hammer, strike the Eye [depicted below] while reciting the following formula:

> "I conjure You by the Holy Names! Hand over the thief who made off with my property! *CHALCHAK CHALKOUM CHIAM CHARCHROUM ZBAR BERI ZBARKOM CHRE KARIOB PHARIBOU*, and by the names that cause trembling: A EE EEE IIII OOOOO YYYYYY OOOOOOO!" <note>The seven vowels of the Greek alphabet—A, E, H (long E), I, O, Y, and Ω (long O)—were thought to represent the seven planets and the fundamental sounds of the universe.</note>

[This is the figure to strike:]

<image-desc>A magical vowel diagram arranged around a central instruction. The letter "A" is centered at the top. Below it, two columns of vowel sequences flank the text "[draw the Uzat-Eye, the Eye of Horus, here]". The left column reads downward: O, YY, IIII, EEEEE, EEEEEE, AAAAAAA. The right column mirrors this structure.</image-desc>

->O [draw the E E<-
->YY Udjat-Eye, EEE<-
->IIII the Eye of IIII<-
->EEEEE Horus, here] EEEEE<-
->EEEEEE EEEEEE<-
->AAAAAAA AAAAAAA<-
<note>The Udjat-Eye, or Eye of Horus, was a primary symbol of protection and "making whole" in Egyptian tradition; here it is used to focus the spell's power to "see" and identify the criminal.</note>

## *II. DIVINATION AND VISIONS*

iv. Inquiry of Bowl Divination <note>a method of fortune-telling by gazing into a liquid, also known as lecanomancy</note> and Necromancy <note>communication with the spirits of the dead</note> Nephotes to Psammetichos <note>Psammetichos was the name of several historical Pharaohs; here he is addressed as a legendary patron of the occult</note>, Immortal King of Egypt. You should prepare your eyes with green eye-paint and black eye-paint <note>In ancient Egyptian ritual, green malachite and black galena paints were used not only for beauty but to protect the eyes and grant the practitioner "true sight" to see divine beings.</note>. Listen to these things which I have said! Walk in accordance with the words of my mouth! Reveal yourself to me, THAN THANA THANATHA <note>original: θαν θανα θαναθα</note>, this is my correct name!"

Say this nine times until she <note>The text shifts gender to feminine, reflecting the Greek influence where the Moon is the goddess Selene.</note> reveals herself to you. <note>This citation refers to a known Demotic/Greek papyrus (PDM xiv.695-700) containing similar lunar instructions.</note>

### **vi. Method of the 29 Letters for Receiving an Omen**

> "Great is the Lady Isis!" This is a copy of a Holy Book found in the Archives of Hermes: The method concerns the 29 letters <note>This refers to the Coptic alphabet, which consists of the 24 Greek letters plus additional characters derived from Egyptian Demotic; the alphabet was seen as a divine tool representing the components of the universe.</note>, through which letters Hermes <note>The Greek name for Thoth, the god of writing and magic.</note> and Isis, who was seeking her brother and husband Osiris, finally found him. Call upon Helios <note>The Sun God.</note> and all the gods in the Deep <note>The primordial abyss or the underworld.</note> concerning those things for which you want to receive an omen. Take 29 leaves of a male date palm and write the names of the gods on each of the leaves. Pray and then pick them up two by two. Read the last remaining leaf and you will find your omen, how things truly are, and you will be answered clearly. <note>This ritual is a form of cleromancy, or divination by lot, found in the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM XXIVa.1-25).</note>

### viii. Spell for Revelation

Keep yourself Pure for 7 days before the Moon becomes Full by abstaining from Meat and Uncooked Food, by leaving behind during the prescribed days exactly Half of your Food in a **Turquoise Vessel** <note>original: "Turquoise Vessel"; in the ancient world, this likely refers to Egyptian faience, a blue-green glazed earthenware that was far more common than actual turquoise gemstone.</note>, over which you are also to Eat, and by abstaining from Wine. When the Moon is Full, go by yourself to the Eastern Section of your City, Village, or House and throw out on the Ground the Left-Over Morsels. Then return Very Quickly to your Quarters and shut yourself in before **He** <note>The spirit or deity being summoned.</note> can get there, because He will shut you out if He gets there before you. 

But before you throw out the Morsels, fix in the Ground at a Slight Angle a **Verdant Reed** that is about Two Cubits <note>Approximately 3 feet or 90 centimeters.</note> long, tie some Hairs from a **Stallion** about the Mid-Section of a **Horned Dung Beetle** <note>The scarab beetle represented the sun god Khepri; here it is used as a sympathetic link to compel the deity.</note>, and suspend the Beetle from the Reed by them. Then light a **Lamp** that has not been used before and place it under the Beetle in a new Earthen-Ware Dish, so that the Heat from the Lamp barely reaches the Beetle. Stay Calm after you have thrown out the Morsels, gone to your Quarters, and shut yourself in; for the One you have summoned will stand there and, by threatening you with Weapons, will try to force you to release the Beetle. But remain Calm, and do not release it until He gives you a Response; then release it right...

(None is Greater): Say this to the Sun <note>original: "Helios"</note> 7 times, then anoint your Hand with Oil and wipe it on your Head and Face.

> "Rejoice with me, You who are set over the East Wind and the World, for whom all the Gods serve as Body-Guards at Your Good Hour and on Your Good Day, You who are the Good Spirit <note>original: "Daimon"</note> of the World, the Crown of the Inhabited World, You who arise from the Abyss, You who Each Day rise a Young Man and set an Old Man, HARPENKNOUPHI BRINTANTE'NO'PHRI BRISSKYLMAS AROURZORBOROBA MESINTRIPHI NIPTOUMI CHMOUMMAO'PHI <note>These are "voces magicae," powerful non-translatable magical names used to invoke divine energy.</note>. ## xi. Prayer

> "I call upon You who have All Forms and Many Names, Double-Horned Goddess <note>original: "dikeros"; a reference to the crescent moon, which resembles horns.</note>, Moon <note>original: "Mene"</note>, whose Form no one knows except Him who made the entire World, IAO <note>A Greek transliteration of the Hebrew name for God, frequently used in these papyri as a supreme creative deity.</note>, the One who shaped You into Twenty-Eight Shapes of the World <note>This refers to the twenty-eight days of the lunar month, with each day representing a different "shape" or phase.</note> so that they might complete every Figure and distribute Breath to every Animal and Plant, that it might Flourish, You who grow from Obscurity into Light and leave Light for Darkness" (beginning to leave by Waning).

> "And the First Companion of Your Name is Silence, the Second a Popping Sound, the Third Groaning, the Fourth Hissing, the Fifth a Cry of Joy, the Sixth Moaning, the Seventh Barking, the Eighth Bellowing, the Ninth Neighing, the Tenth a Musical Sound, the Eleventh a Sounding Wind, the Twelfth a Wind-Creating Sound, the Thirteenth a Coercive Sound, the Fourteenth a Coercive Emanation from Perfection <note>These fourteen sounds represent the waxing half of the lunar cycle; they are ritual vocalizations the practitioner would likely perform.</note>.

> "Ox, Vulture, Bull, Beetle, Falcon, Crab, Dog, Wolf, Serpent, Horse, She-Goat, Asp, Goat, He-Goat, Baboon, Cat, Lion, Leopard, Field-Mouse, Deer, Multi-Form, Virgin, Torch, Lightning, Garland, a Herald's Wand, Child, Key. <note>This list of twenty-eight animals and objects corresponds to the twenty-eight "shapes" or manifestations of the Goddess mentioned earlier, one for each day of the moon.</note>

> "I have said Your Signs and Symbols of Your Name so that You might hear me, because I pray to You, Mistress of the Whole World. Hear me, You, the Stable One, the Mighty One! **APHEIBOE'O' MINTE'R OCHAO' PIZEPHYDO'R CHANTHAR CHADE'ROZO MOCHTHION EOTNEU PHE'RZON AINDE'S LACHABOO' PITTO' RIPHTHAMER ZMOMOCHO'LEIE TIE'DRANTEIA OISOZOCHABE'DO'PHRA** <note>These are "voces magicae" or "words of power." They were believed to be the secret, true names of the gods or the language of spirits, and were intended to be spoken exactly as written to trigger the spell's effect.</note>"

# *IV. HEALTH AND HEALING*

## xii. Phylactery for Fever, Phantoms, Daimons, etc.
<note>A phylactery (from the Greek "phylakterion") is a protective charm or amulet, often written on papyrus or metal, worn on the body to ward off disease or malevolent spirits.</note>

> "I, Abrasax <note>A powerful name of power in ancient magic, often represented as a creature with a rooster's head and snakes for legs; his name’s Greek letters add up to 365, representing the days of the year.</note>, shall deliver. Abrasax am I! ABRASAX ABRASICHO'OU, help little Sophia-Priskilla. Get hold of and do away with what comes to little Sophia-Priskilla, whether it is a Shivering Fit <note>Likely referring to the recurring chills associated with malaria, which was a common affliction.</note> — get hold of it! Whether a Phantom — get hold of it! Whether a Daimon <note>original: "daimon"; in antiquity, this referred to a spirit or supernatural being that could be good or evil, often blamed for sudden illnesses.</note> — get hold of it! I, Abrasax, shall deliver. Abrasax am I! ABRASAX ABRASICHO'OU. Get hold of, get hold of and do away with... what comes to little Sophia-Priskilla on This Very Day, whether it is a Shivering Fit — do away with it! Whether a Daimon — do away with it!" <note>PGM refers to the "Papyri Graecae Magicae," a collection of ancient Greek magical texts.</note>

## xiii. Spell for Dog Bite

To be said to the Bite of the Dog: 
> "My Mouth being full of Blood of a Black Dog, I spitting out the Redness of a Dog, I come forth from Alkhah <note>original: "rq-hh"; a sacred area at Abydos, Egypt, famed as the burial site of the god Osiris.</note>. O this Dog who is among the Ten Dogs which belong to Anubis <note>The jackal-headed Egyptian god of embalming and the protector of the dead.</note>, the Son of His Body, extract your Venom, remove your Saliva from me also! If you do not extract your Venom and remove your Saliva, I shall take you up to the Fore-Court of the Temple of Osiris, my Watch-Tower. I will do for you according to the Voice of Isis, the Magician, the Lady of Magic, who Bewitches everything, who is Never Bewitched in her Name of Isis, the Magician."

You pound Garlic with Gum, put it on the Wound of the Dog Bite, and speak to it Daily until it is Well. <note>PDM refers to the "Papyri Demoticae Magicae," ancient magical texts written in Demotic Egyptian.</note>

<summary>This page contains practical ritual instructions for healing, featuring a protective amulet against fevers and spirits, and a spoken charm combined with a garlic poultice to treat dog bites by invoking Egyptian deities.</summary>
<keywords>Abrasax, Sophia-Priskilla, Phylactery, Fever, Phantoms, Daimons, Dog Bite, Alkhah, Abydos, Osiris, Anubis, Isis, Ancient Medicine, Amulets</keywords>

# V. CRAFT

### xiv. This is the Consecration for All Purposes: Spell to Helios

> "I invoke You, the Greatest God, Eternal Lord, World Ruler, who are over the World and under the World, Mighty Ruler of the Sea, rising at Dawn, shining from the East for the Whole World, setting in the West. Come to me, Thou who risest from the Four Winds, benevolent and lucky <term>Noble Spirit</term> <note>original: "Agathos Daimon"; a benevolent personal deity or "good spirit" often associated with luck and protection.</note>, for whom Heaven has become the Processional Way. I call upon Your Holy and Great and Hidden Names which You rejoice to hear. The Earth flourished when You shone forth, and the Plants became fruitful when you laughed; the Animals begat their Young when You permitted. Give Glory and Honor and Favor and Fortune and Power to this <note>The scribe used "NN" (Greek: "deina") as a placeholder where the practitioner would speak the name of the person or the object.</note> Stone which I consecrate today (or to the <term>protective charm</term> <note>original: "phylactery"; an item worn to protect the user from harm or disease.</note> being consecrated) for [or in relation to] NN. 

> I invoke You, the greatest in Heaven, E'I LANCHYCH AKARE'N BAL MISTHRE'N MARTA MATHATH LAILAM MOUSOUTHI SIETHO' BATHABATHI IATMO'N ALEI IABATH ABAO'TH SABAO'TH ADO'NAI <note>These include the Hebrew titles "Sabaoth" (Lord of Hosts) and "Adonai" (Lord), which were frequently used by Greek magicians as potent names of power.</note>, the Great God, ORSENOPHRE' ORGEATE'S TOTHORNATE'SA KRITHI BIO'THI IADMO' IATMO'MI METHIE'I LONCHOO' AKARE' BAL MINTHRE' BANE BAI(N)CHYCHYCH OUPHRI NOTHEOUSI THRAI ARSIOUTH ERO'NERTHER, the Shining Helios, giving Light throughout the Whole World. 

> You are the Great Serpent, Leader of all the Gods, who control the Beginning of Egypt and the End of the Whole Inhabited World, who mate in the Ocean, PSOI PHNOUTHI NINTHE'R. You are He who becomes Visible each Day and Sets in the Northwest of Heaven, and Rises in the Southeast."

# Selections from the Magical Papyri: Book III <note>original Latin: "Ecloga Ex Papyris Magicis: Liber III"</note>

## Introduction

*Contents*

This is the book of love spells, charms, prayers, rituals, and more, drawn from the Greek magical papyri and Demotic spells. These materials are not intended for general public consumption.

The information in [brackets] following each selection indicates the collection (PGM = Greek Magical Papyri <note>original Latin: "Papyri Graecae Magicae"</note>, PDM = Demotic Magical Papyri <note>original Latin: "P. Demoticae M."</note>), the specific papyrus number, and the line numbers where the spell can be found.

*Pronunciation of Magical Names* <note>original: "voces magicae"; these are strings of powerful, often untranslatable sounds or secret divine names intended to resonate with spiritual forces</note>

In general, most of the "magical names" are written in Old Coptic <note>the final stage of the Egyptian language, which utilized the Greek alphabet for its script</note>, which used Greek letters, so you will do best if you think in Greek. Thus, Y <term>upsilon</term> sounds between the English "u" and "y," something like a German "u" with an umlaut (ü). Pronounce CH <term>chi</term> as in the German "ach" or the Scottish "loch." The symbol E' represents eta, so pronounce it like a long "a"; O' represents omega, so pronounce it like a long "o." The symbols PH <term>phi</term> and TH <term>theta</term> probably should be aspirated <note>pronounced with a distinct puff of air, like "p-h" in "top-hat" or "t-h" in "hot-head," rather than the modern soft "f" or "th" sounds</note>.

<summary>This title page introduces a collection of ancient love spells and rituals, providing a guide to the abbreviations for the Greek and Demotic Magical Papyri and instructions on the Greek-based pronunciation of ritual power-words.</summary>

<keywords>Greek Magical Papyri, PGM, PDM, love spells, voces magicae, Old Coptic, pronunciation guide, Johannes Opsopoeus</keywords>

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the magical papyri, specifically finishing the pronunciation guide before transitioning into the first set of love spells from the Great Magical Papyrus in Paris (PGM IV).</meta>

"p" and "t", but may have been pronounced like English "f" and "th" by the time the papyri were written.

The symbol "NN" in a spell means "fill in the blank," generally with the name of the one on whose behalf the spell is cast (thus, "NN" or "the NN man"), or with the question or problem to which the spell is addressed (thus, "the NN matter"). <note>NN stands for the Latin "nomen nominandum," meaning "the name to be named." In these spells, the practitioner would substitute the generic placeholder for a specific person's name, often including their mother's name for greater spiritual accuracy.</note>

# SPELLS

### i. Love Spell

Aphrodite's Name, which becomes known to No One quickly, is NEPHERIE'RI <note>original Egyptian: "Nfr-iry.t", meaning "the beautiful eye," a traditional title for the goddess Hathor, who was identified with the Greek Aphrodite</note> — this is the Name. If you wish to win a Woman who is beautiful, be Pure for 3 days, make an offering of Frankincense <term>Frankincense</term> <note>a costly aromatic resin used in religious and magical offerings to attract divine favor</note>, and call this Name over it. You approach the Woman and say it seven times in your Soul as you gaze at her, and in this way it will succeed. But do this for 7 days. [PGM IV.1265-74]

### ii. Love Spell Which Acts in the Same Hour

# The "Mithras" Liturgy

## **Preface**

This work provides the reader with a brief introduction, Greek text, and English translation of the fascinating though difficult document known as the Mithras Liturgy.

## **Introduction**

The so-called <term>Mithras Liturgy <note>Though named after the Persian god Mithras, scholars debate whether this text represents the actual secret "Mysteries of Mithras" or is a magical ritual using the god's name to achieve a vision of the divine.</note></term> is included in this series of texts and translations for several reasons. The Liturgy is frequently mentioned in secondary literature, and has been partially translated into English upon several occasions, but it has not previously been available in its entirety in English translation. 

Furthermore, the Mithras Liturgy deserves the attention of students of early Christian literature and the history of religions, particularly <term>Graeco-Roman religion <note>The diverse religious landscape of the Roman Empire, where traditional Greek and Roman gods existed alongside "Oriental" deities and local traditions.</note></term>: the Liturgy reflects an important religious tendency of its day, a <term>syncretistic <note>Refers to the blending or merging of different religious beliefs and practices into a new, unified system.</note></term> piety utilizing astrology and magic and emphasizing the <term>ecstatic ascent <note>A ritual process intended to allow the soul of the living practitioner to leave the body and travel through the heavens to stand before a god.</note></term> of the individual soul. Of additional interest is the relationship of the Mithras Liturgy to the previously known <term>Hermetic literature <note>A collection of philosophical and magical writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, blending Greek and Egyptian wisdom.</note></term> and the recently discovered tractates from <term>Nag Hammadi <note>A site in Egypt where a collection of early Christian "Gnostic" texts was discovered in 1945, providing insight into alternative early Christian beliefs.</note></term> (cf., for example, page 3, note b, below).

The Mithras Liturgy is part of the great magical codex of Paris (<term>Papyrus 574 <note>A famous and extensive papyrus scroll containing a wide variety of spells and rituals; it is one of the most important sources for our understanding of ancient magic.</note></term> of the <note>original: "Bibliotheque Nationale," the National Library of France in Paris.</note>). Presumably compiled in the early fourth century C.E., this codex contains a variety of tractates, hymns, recipes, and prescriptions, which were apparently collected for use in the working library of an Egyptian

<summary>This preface introduces the "Mithras Liturgy," a complex ritual text from a 4th-century Egyptian magical codex that describes the soul's ascent to the divine. It contextualizes the document within the broader landscape of Graeco-Roman religion, magic, and early Christian-era philosophy.</summary>
<keywords>Mithras Liturgy, Paris Papyrus 574, syncretism, ecstatic ascent, Hermeticism, Nag Hammadi, Graeco-Roman religion, Egyptian magic</keywords>

--- Page 73 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the "Mithras Liturgy," picking up from the discussion of its relationship to the previous magical practices mentioned in the codex. It transitions from the general context of the manuscript to the specific scholarly debate surrounding the origin and nature of this ritual text.</meta>

magician. Lines 475–834 of this codex constitute the Mithras Liturgy; these boundaries for the Liturgy are suggested by the continuity of thought within the Liturgy, by the punctuation utilized by the scribe, and by the apparent transition to a different section (lines 835–849: astrological calculations). Interestingly, lines 467–474 parallel lines 821–823 and 830–834: thus the Mithras Liturgy is placed between two closely related versions of spells utilizing lines from <term>Homer <note>The legendary Greek poet; in this era, verses from his epics like the Iliad and Odyssey were often repurposed as powerful "words of power" in magic rituals.</note></term>.

The Mithras Liturgy received its name and fame from <term>A. Dieterich <note>Albrecht Dieterich (1866–1908), a pioneering German scholar who first argued that this magical text was actually a lost ritual of the Mithraic Mysteries.</note></term>. In 1903 Dieterich published his valuable book, *A Mithras Liturgy* <note>Original title: *Eine MithrasLiturgie*</note>, in which he proposed that the text in question contains an official liturgy of the Mithras cult, a Mithraic ritual for the ascent and immortalization of the soul. Although the Mithras Liturgy had been later adopted and somewhat adapted by Egyptian magicians, Dieterich concluded, the text still preserves the highest sacrament in which the <note>Likely a typographical error for "Mithraic"</note> initiate could participate. However, since the publication of Dieterich's book, <term>F. Cumont <note>Franz Cumont, the premier historian of Mithraism in the early 20th century, who strongly disagreed with Dieterich and viewed the text as purely magical rather than a formal religious liturgy.</note></term>, <term>R. Reitzenstein <note>Richard Reitzenstein, a scholar of the "History of Religions School" who sought the roots of such rituals in broader Hellenistic and Iranian influences.</note></term>, and others have expressed skepticism concerning the Mithraic origin of the Liturgy. Thus, such scholars have suggested that more significant parallels to the Liturgy can be found in the <term>hermetic writings <note>A collection of philosophical and religious texts attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, focusing on the soul's journey to the divine.</note></term>, or in individualistic and private mysteries, or in <term>Graeco-Egyptian syncretism <note>The blending of Greek and Egyptian religious traditions, which was very common in the culture of Roman-era Egypt.</note></term>, magic, and <term>solar piety <note>Devotion centered on the sun as a supreme deity or a symbol of divine power and enlightenment.</note></term>. Yet the evidence amassed by Dieterich cannot be easily dismissed, for he highlights important echoes of Mithraism in the Mithras Liturgy: particularly striking are the mention of "the great god Helios Mithras" (line 482), the invocation of the elements (lines 487–537), the description of the fire-breathing god <term>Aion <note>A deity representing "Eternal Time," often associated with the cosmic cycles and depicted in mystery religions as a powerful, lion-headed figure.</note></term> (lines 587–616), and the portraits of Helios (lines 635–637) and the highest God (lines

<summary>This page details the structural placement of the Mithras Liturgy within the manuscript and explores the academic debate regarding its origins, specifically Albrecht Dieterich's theory that it is a genuine Mithraic ritual.</summary>
<keywords>Mithras Liturgy, Albrecht Dieterich, Franz Cumont, Richard Reitzenstein, Mithraism, Graeco-Egyptian syncretism, Aion, Helios Mithras, ritual ascent</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues the scholarly assessment of the "Mithras Liturgy," moving from the debate between scholars like Dieterich and Cumont toward a compromise view that acknowledges the text's unique Egyptian-magical character while maintaining its connection to Mithraic themes.</meta>

693-704). Furthermore, the accounts of <term>Celsus <note>A 2nd-century Greek philosopher and opponent of early Christianity whose views are preserved in the writings of Origen.</note></term> (in <term>Origen <note>An influential early Christian theologian.</note></term>, *Against Celsus* <note>original: "Contra Celsum"</note>, 6. 21-22) and <term>Porphyry <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher interested in the symbolic meaning of religious rituals.</note></term> (*On the Cave of the Nymphs* <note>original: "De antro nympharum"</note>, 5-6 etc.) on Mithraism verify the fact that such a liturgy for the soul's ascent as the Mithras Liturgy could be quite compatible with at least some expressions of Mithraism.

Consequently, it is advisable to conclude that the Mithras Liturgy may indeed represent some variety of Mithraism—though not, to be sure, Mithraism as it is usually presented. If there is Mithraism in this papyrus, it is a Mithraism on the fringe, a Mithraism preoccupied with individualism, **syncretism** <note>the blending or merging of different religions, cultures, or schools of thought</note>, and magic. The Mithras Liturgy may thus illustrate a direction taken by those carrying on the Mithras tradition in Egypt.

The text of the Mithras Liturgy is composed of two main parts: a liturgical mystery of ascent (lines 475-750), and a set of instructions (lines 750-834) for the use of the mystery. After the brief introduction (lines 475- 485), the mystery of ascent presents the seven liturgical stages for the soul's ecstatic journey:

1. The soul thus encounters the **four elements** (lines 485-537), in their generative and regenerative aspects; 
2. The **lower powers of the air** (lines 537-585), including the winds, bolts of thunder and lightning, and meteors; 
3. <term>Aion <note>A deity representing eternal or unbounded time.</note></term> and the Aionic powers (lines 585-628), as planetary guardians of the heavenly doors; 
4. **Helios** (lines 628-657), young and fiery; 
5. The **seven Fates** (lines 657-672) and, next, 
6. The **seven Pole-Lords** (lines 673-692), both groups from the region of the fixed stars, and both depicted in Egyptian fashion; 
7. And finally the **highest God** (lines 692-724), portrayed like Mithras himself.

After the conclusion (lines 724-750) to the mystery of ascent, the instructions for the use of the mystery present a **scarab ceremony**...

<summary>This page concludes that the "Mithras Liturgy" represents a "fringe" version of Mithraism influenced by Egyptian magic and outlines the seven stages of the soul's ascent described in the text.</summary>
<keywords>Celsus, Origen, Porphyry, Mithraism, Syncretism, Aion, Helios, Seven Fates, Pole-Lords, Soul's Ascent</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the scholarly introduction to the Mithras Liturgy, shifting focus from its historical context to the specific magical mechanics used in the ritual. ...of the sun (lines 750-798) provide instructions for the obtaining of the **kentritis herb** <note>An unidentified plant often associated with the "centaury" and used in ancient solar rituals.</note> and the fashioning of amulets (lines 798-830) and append two additional spells (lines 831-834). The predominant place of magic within the Mithras Liturgy deserves special mention. The entire text of the Liturgy is permeated with magic, including breathing techniques (cf. lines 537-538: drawing in breath from the rays), special recipes (cf. lines 750-755: preparing the cake for the scarab beetle), magical rituals (cf. lines 767-769: burying the scarab beetle) amulets (cf. lines 659-660: kissing the amulets), and magical formulae. 

The magical formulae themselves are diverse in character: some seem **onomatopoetic** <note>words that imitate the natural sound of a thing</note> (cf. line 488, the popping sound <note>original: "PPP"</note>: making a popping sound, possibly like thunder), symbolic (cf. line 487, the vowel sequence <note>original: "AEEIOYO"</note>: using the seven Greek vowels in a series to represent the seven planets), or perhaps **glossolalic** <note>uttering sounds that resemble a spiritual or "angelic" language, often called "speaking in tongues."</note> (cf. line 492, the sounds <note>original: "EY EIA EE"</note>); some seem derived from or imitative of Greek (cf. line 562, **Primal Brightener** <note>original: "PROPHREGGE"</note>), Egyptian (cf. line 672, **Horus of the two horizons** <note>original: "ARRACHES"</note>; and line 717, **Re, the Sun** <note>original: "PHRE"</note>), or Semitic words (cf. line 591, **Eternal sun** <note>original: "SEMESILAM"</note>; and line 593, **Yahweh** <note>original: "IAO"</note>).

## Actual Text

(475) Be gracious to me, O <term>Providence <note>The divine foresight or care that governs the universe.</note></term> and <term>Psyche <note>The personified soul.</note></term>, as I write these mysteries handed down not for profit but for instruction; and for an only child <note>Ancient magical traditions often restricted the passing of knowledge to a single chosen heir or spiritual descendant.</note> I request **immortality**, O initiates of this our power. 

(Furthermore, it is necessary for you, O daughter, to take (480) the juices of herbs and spices, which will be provided to you at the end of my holy treatise), which the great god **Helios Mithras** <note>A solar deity resulting from the blending of the Greek Sun god (Helios) and the Persian god Mithras.</note> ordered to be revealed to me by his **archangel** <note>In this context, a powerful spirit-messenger of the highest order in the cosmic hierarchy.</note>, so that I alone may ascend into heaven as an inquirer (485) and behold the universe.

<keywords>Mithras Liturgy, magical formulae, celestial ascent, Helios Mithras, breathing techniques, amulets, ritual ingredients, immortality, providence, soul</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the transition from the general mechanics of the Mithras Liturgy to the actual spoken ritual. It marks the beginning of the "Invocation," the formal prayer used by the practitioner to seek a spiritual transformation—a process the text calls "immortal birth."</meta>

# The Invocation of the Ceremony

This is the spoken invocation of the ceremony:

> "First origin of my origin <note>original: 'archē tēs emēs arches'</note>, **AEEIOYO** <note>A sequence of the seven Greek vowels, which ancient magicians believed represented the sounds of the seven planets and the harmony of the universe.</note>, first beginning of my beginning, **PPP SSS PHR** <note>These are onomatopoeic sounds intended to mimic the rushing of wind or the crackle of divine fire.</note> spirit of spirit, the first of the spirit in me (490), **MMM**, fire given by God to my mixture of the mixtures in me, the first of the fire in me, **EY EIA EE**, water of water, the first of the water in me, **OOO AAA EEE**, earthy substance, the first of the earthy substance in me, (495) **YE YOE**, my complete body—

> (I, <insert>your name</insert>, whose mother is <insert>your mother's name</insert> <note>In ancient magic, practitioners were often identified by their mother’s name rather than their father’s to ensure absolute genealogical certainty in the eyes of the gods.</note>), 

> which was formed by a noble arm and an incorruptible right hand in a world without light and yet radiant, without soul and yet alive with soul, **YEI AYI EYOIE**: now if it be your will, **METERTA** (500) **PHOTH** (**METHARTHA PHERIE**, as written in another version) <note>These names likely derive from Egyptian or Semitic titles for the divine; 'Photh' is often associated with the god Thoth.</note> **YEREZATH**, give me over to **immortal birth** <note>original: 'palingenesia,' referring to a spiritual rebirth or recreation of the self into a divine state.</note> and, following that, to my underlying nature, so that, after the present need which is pressing me exceedingly, I may gaze upon the immortal (505) beginning with the immortal spirit, **ANCHREPHRENESOYPHIRIGCH**, with the immortal water, **ERONOYI PARAKOYNETH**, with the most steadfast air, **EIOAE PSENABOTH**; that I may be born again in thought, **KRAOCHRAX R OIM ENARCHOMAI** <note>original: 'enarchomai' means 'I begin.'</note>, (510) and the sacred spirit may breathe in me, **NECHTHEN APOTOY NECHTHIN ARPI ETH**; so that I may wonder at the sacred fire, **KYPHE**; that I may gaze upon the unfathomable, awesome water of the dawn, **NYO THESO ECHO OYCHIECHOA**, and the vivifying (515), and encircling **aether** <note>The 'aether' was considered the 'fifth element' in antiquity—the pure, upper air breathed by the gods.</note> may hear me, **ARNOMETHPH**; 

> for today I am about to behold, with immortal eyes—I, born mortal from a mortal womb, but transformed by tremendous power and an incorruptible right hand (520)!—and with immortal spirit, the immortal **Aion** <note>Aion is the personification of eternal time and the infinite cycle of the cosmos.</note> and...

<summary>This page begins the primary invocation of the Mithras Liturgy, where the practitioner identifies their physical elements (fire, water, earth, spirit) and petitions for a spiritual "immortal birth" to transcend their mortal origins.</summary>

...master of the fiery diadems—

I, <insert>name</insert> whose mother is <insert>mother's name</insert> according to the **immutable decree** of god, **EYE YIA EEI AO EIAY IYA IEO**! <note>A series of ritual vowel sounds intended to resonate with the celestial spheres.</note> Since it is impossible for me, born (530) **mortal**, to rise with the golden brightnesses of the **immortal brilliance**, **OEY AEO EYA EOE YAE 5IAE**, stand, O perishable nature of mortals, and at once [restore] me safe and sound after the inexorable and pressing (535) need. For I am the son **PSYCHON DEMOY PROCHO PROA**, I am **MACHARPHON MOY PROPSYCHON PROE**! <note>These phrases are likely liturgical names or secret identifications meant to prove the practitioner's divine lineage to the guardians of the heavens.</note>

Draw in breath from the rays, drawing up three times as much as you can, and you will see yourself being lifted up and (540) ascending to the height, so that you seem to be in mid-air. You will hear nothing either of man or of any other living thing, nor in that hour will you see anything of mortal affairs on earth, but rather you will see all immortal things. For in that day (545) and hour you will see the divine order of the skies: the **presiding gods** <gloss>8. These are the celestial spirits or planetary deities who govern the movements of the stars.</gloss> rising into heaven, and others setting. Now the course of the visible gods will appear through the **disk of god**, my father; and in similar fashion the so-called "pipe" (550), the origin of the **ministering wind**. <note>The 'pipe' (aulos) refers to a famous cosmological feature in this text—a conduit or tube extending from the sun that was believed to be the source of the world's winds.</note> For you will see it hanging from the sun's disk like a pipe. You will see the outflow of this object toward the regions westward, boundless as an east wind, if it be assigned to the regions of the East—and the other (namely, the west wind), similarly, toward its own (555) regions. <gloss>9. This refers to the secondary wind channel moving in the opposite direction, maintaining the cosmic balance.</gloss>

And you will see the gods staring intently at you and rushing at you. So at once put your right finger on your mouth and say:

> "Silence! Silence! Silence! Symbol of the living, incorruptible god! (560) Guard me, Silence, **NECHTHEIR THANMELOY**!" <note>original: 'NECHTHEIR THANMELOY.' > "Silence! Silence! (the prayer) I am a star, wandering about with you, and shining forth out of (575) the deep, **OXY O XERTHEYTH**." <note>'OXY' likely relates to the Greek 'oxys,' meaning sharp or bright. 'XERTHEYTH' is a secret name for the divine power guiding the initiate.</note>

Silence!"

### The Apparition of the Seven Fates

...Make a long, bellowing sound like a horn, releasing all your breath and straining your sides; and kiss (660) the amulets <note>original: *phylaktēria*. These were protective charms, likely inscribed on metal or papyrus, worn by the practitioner to ensure safety during the encounter with divine forces.</note> and say, first toward the right: "Protect me, **PROSYMERI**!"

After saying this, you will see the doors thrown open, and seven virgins coming from deep within, dressed in linen garments and with the faces of asps. They are called the Fates (665) of heaven, and they wield golden wands. When you see them, greet them in this manner:

> "Hail, O seven Fates of heaven, O noble and good virgins, O sacred ones and companions of **MINIMIRROPHOR**, O most holy guardians of the four pillars! <note>Ancient cosmologies often depicted the sky as a physical structure supported by four pillars located at the cardinal points of the world.</note>
> 
> (670) Hail to you, the first, **CHREPSENTHAES**!
> Hail to you, the second, **MENESCHEES**!
> Hail to you, the third, **MECHRAN**!
> Hail to you, the fourth, **ARARMACHES**!
> Hail to you, the fifth, **ECHOMMIE**!
> Hail to you, the sixth, **TICHNONDAES**!
> Hail to you, the seventh, **EROY ROMBRIES**!

### The Apparition of the Pole-Lords

There also come forth another seven gods, who have the faces of black bulls, dressed in linen (675) loin-cloths and wearing seven golden crowns. These are the so-called Pole-Lords <note>original: *polokratōres*. These deities represent the power of the Great Bear constellation (the "Big Dipper"), which was believed to control the rotation of the heavens around the celestial pole.</note> of heaven, whom you must greet in the same manner, each of them with his own name:

> "Hail, O guardians of the pivot <note>The "pivot" refers to the North Pole of the celestial sphere, the fixed point around which the stars seem to revolve.</note>, O sacred and brave youths, who turn (680) at one command the revolving axis of the vault of heaven; you who send out thunder and lightning, the jolts of earthquakes, and thunderbolts against the nations of impious people! But to me, who am pious and god-fearing, send health, soundness of body (685), and acuteness of hearing and seeing..."

<summary>This passage describes the arrival of the celestial guardians—the seven asp-faced Fates and the seven bull-faced Pole-Lords—who oversee the mechanics of the universe and the judgment of humanity.</summary>

### The Descent of the Great God

Now when they take their place, here and there, in order, look in the air and you will see lightning-bolts going down, and lights flashing (695), and the earth shaking, and a god descending. This god is immensely great, having a bright, youthful appearance, golden-haired, wearing a white tunic and a golden crown and trousers <note>original: *anaxyrides*. These were distinctive long trousers worn by Persians and other Eastern peoples, which in the Roman world became a hallmark of the god Mithras's "oriental" attire.</note>, and holding in his right hand a golden (700) shoulder of a young bull: this is the Bear <note>original: *arktos*. In ancient Egyptian and some Greek magical traditions, the constellation we call the "Big Dipper" or "Great Bear" was visualized as the thigh or shoulder of a bull that turns the heavens.</note> which moves and turns heaven around, moving upward and downward in accordance with the hour. 

Then you will see lightning-bolts leaping from his eyes and stars from his body. And at once (705) produce a long bellowing sound <note>original: *mykēma*. This refers to a deep, resonant ritual vocalization intended to mimic the sound of a bull, used here to vibrate the practitioner's body and "awaken" the senses.</note>, straining your belly, that you may excite the five senses: bellow long until the conclusion, and again kiss the amulets, and say: 

"**MOKRIMO PHERIMOPHERERI**, life of me, __________" <note>The blank line indicates where the practitioner would insert their own name.</note> stay!

> Dwell in (710) my soul! Do not abandon me, for one entreats you, **ENTHO PHENEN THROPIOTH**."

And gaze upon the god while bellowing long; and greet him in this manner:

### Instruction for the Ritual:

Take a sun-scarab <note>The scarab beetle, associated with the Egyptian god Khepri, symbolized the sun and the process of rebirth.</note> which has twelve rays, and make it fall into a deep, turquoise cup, at the time when the moon is invisible; put in together with it the seed of the **lotometra** <note>original: *lotometra*. Likely the seed pod of the white lotus (Nymphaea lotus).</note>, (755) and honey; and, after grinding it, prepare a cake. And at once you will see it (the scarab) moving forward and eating; and when it has consumed it, it immediately dies. Pick it up and throw it into a glass vessel of excellent rose oil, as much as you wish; and (760) spreading sacred sand in a pure manner, set the vessel on it, and say the formula over the vessel for seven days, while the sun is in mid-heaven:

> "I have consecrated you, that your essence may be useful to me, to [Name] <note>The practitioner would insert their own name or the name of the initiate here.</note> alone, **IE IA E EE OY EIA** <note>Magic words consisting of vowel permutations intended to resonate with the divine.</note>, that you may prove useful to me (765) alone. For I am **PHOR PHORA PHOS PHOTIZAAS** (others: **PHOR PHOR OPHOTHEI XAAS**)." <note>These *voces magicae* contain the Greek root *phos* (light), invoking the solar power of the deity.</note>

On the seventh day pick up the scarab, and bury it with Myrrh and wine from **Mendes** <note>An ancient Egyptian city famous in antiquity for its high-quality perfumes and ritual wines.</note> and fine linen; and put it away in a flourishing bean-field. (770) Then, after you have entertained and feasted together, put away, in a pure manner, the ointment for the immortalization. If you want to show this to someone else, take the juice of the herb called **kentritis**, and smear it, along with rose oil, over the eyes of the one you wish; (775) and he will see so clearly...

...that he will amaze you. I have not found a greater spell than this in the world. Ask the god for what you want, and he will give it to you.

Now, the presentation <note>original: *systasis*. This refers to the formal introduction or encounter between the practitioner and the deity.</note> before the great god is as follows: obtain the herb (780) <term>kentritis <note>A specific magical plant, possibly a type of verbena or centaury, though its exact botanical identity remains debated by scholars.</note></term> at the conjunction <note>The moment when the sun and moon align in the sky.</note> occurring in the **Lion** <note>The zodiac sign of Leo, a sign associated with the sun and divine power.</note>. (785) Lick off the leaf while you show it to the Sun, and then he <note>The sun god, specifically Helios or Mithras in this context.</note> will listen to you attentively. ->“I EE oo IAI.”<-
<note>original: "Ι ΕΕ οο ΙΑΙ." These are magical vocalizations or "voces magicae." The use of specific vowels was thought to resonate with the celestial spheres.</note>

Lick this up so that you may be protected; and rolling up the leaf (790), throw it into the rose oil. Many times have I used this spell and have wondered greatly at its power. But the god said to me: "Use the ointment no longer; instead, after casting it into the river, perform the consultation while wearing the great <term>mystery <note>In this context, the 'mystery' refers to a physical amulet or ritual object.</note></term> (795) of the scarab. This scarab is revitalized through the twenty-five living birds <note>This likely refers to a complex symbolic ritual involving the "spirit" of birds, representing the twenty-five days of the Egyptian lunar month.</note>. Consult once a month at the full moon, instead of only three times a year." 

The <term>kentritis</term> plant grows starting from the month of **Payni** <note>The tenth month of the Egyptian calendar, roughly June or July.</note> in the regions of the (800) black earth <note>The fertile silt-lands of the Nile Valley.</note>, and it looks similar to the upright verbena. This is how you may recognize it: dip an ibis wing at its black tip into the plant's juice, and the feathers will fall off when touched. (805) After the Lord pointed this out, the herb was discovered in **Menelaitis** at **Phalagry** <note>Regions within the Nile Delta of Egypt.</note> on the river banks, near the **Besas** plant <note>Likely the Peganum harmala, or Syrian Rue, often associated with the protector god Bes.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the detailed instructions for the "immortalization" ritual (often called the Mithras Liturgy). The plant consists of a single stem and is reddish down to the root. Its leaves are somewhat crinkled, and it bears fruit (810) resembling the tip of wild asparagus. It is similar to the plant called <term>talapes <note>An unidentified botanical species, likely a variety of wild beet or a similar leafy green mentioned in ancient herbals.</note></term>, resembling the wild beet.

Now, the amulets require this procedure: transcribe the formula for the right side onto the skin (815) of a black sheep using ink mixed with myrrh. After tying it with the sinews of that same animal, wear it. Then, transcribe the formula for the left side onto the skin of a white sheep and follow the same procedure. The left amulet is filled with <term>PROSTHYMERI <note>This likely refers to "The Gods who are set before [the days]," a specific category of protective or presiding spiritual entities in the magical papyri.</note></term> (820) and contains this text:

"So speaking, he drove the single-hoofed horses through the trench." (*Iliad* 10.564) "And men were gasping amid the grievous slaughter." (*Iliad* 10.521) "And they washed away their heavy sweat in the sea." (*Iliad* 10.572) "You will dare to lift your mighty spear against Zeus himself." (*Iliad* 8.424) (825)

Zeus ascended the mountain with a golden bullock and a silver dagger. Upon all he bestowed a share, but to Amara <note>Perhaps a personification or a specific figure within this local mythic variant; her identity remains obscure to modern scholars.</note> alone he gave nothing. Instead, he said: "Relinquish what you hold, and then you shall receive: **PSINOTHER NOPSITHER THERNOPSI**" (and continue the chant as you wish). (830)

"So Ares suffered when Otos and mighty Epialtes <note>Twin giants (the Aloadae) who, in Greek mythology, famously imprisoned Ares, the god of war, in a bronze jar for thirteen months.</note> [bound] him." (*Iliad* 5.385)

A spell for restraining anger: "You will dare to lift your mighty spear against Zeus." (*Iliad* 8.424). For friends: "Let [the enemy] seize [nothing], lest we become a source of joy for our enemies." (*Iliad* 10.193)

<meta>This page transitions from the specific mythological and biblical comparisons of the previous section into the formal introduction and "Preamble" of G.R.S. Mead’s study on the Mithraic Ritual, also known as the Mithras Liturgy.</meta>

# A Mithraic Ritual

## G.R.S. Mead

### Preamble

The last little volume gave the reader a brief outline of what is known of the cult of Mithra <note>Mithra (or Mithras) was a Persian deity whose mystery cult became one of the most significant religious rivals to early Christianity in the Roman Empire.</note> and the spread of the Mithraic Mysteries in the Western world. We have now to deal with a Mithraic Ritual of the most instructive and intensely interesting character, which introduces us to the innermost rite of the carefully guarded secrets of the Mithriaca <note>original: "Mithriaca," a term referring to the collection of rites and teachings associated with Mithra.</note>.

This Ritual is all the more precious in that our knowledge of the Liturgies of the ancient Pagan cults of the West is of the scantiest nature. A few fragments only remain, mostly in the form of hymns; whereas the Ritual before us is complete, and the only complete one so far discovered. Dieterich <note>Albrecht Dieterich (1866–1908), a German philologist who first published this text under the title "Eine Mithrasliturgie."</note> calls it a {9} <note>This bracketed number indicates the page break in the original printed edition.</note> Liturgy; but a Liturgy is a service in which several take part, whereas it is plain that our Ritual was a secret and solemn inner rite for one person only.

The credit of unearthing it from the obscurity in which it was buried, and of conclusively demonstrating its parentage, is due to Dieterich; for though Cumont <note>Franz Cumont (1868–1947), the foundational scholar of Mithraic studies, who famously disagreed with Dieterich, arguing the ritual was Egyptian magic rather than pure Mithraism.</note> in his great work quotes several passages from the unrevised text, he does so only to reject it as a genuine Mithraic document.

It is dug out of the chaos of the great Paris Magic Papyrus 574 (*Greek Supplement of the National Library* <note>original: "Supplement grec de la Bibliotheque nationale." This refers to the Great Magical Papyrus of Paris, PGM IV.</note>), the date of which is fixed with every probability as the earliest years of the fourth century A.D.. The original text of the Ritual has, however, been

<summary>G.R.S. Mead introduces a rare, complete ritual text from the Paris Magic Papyrus, identifying it as a secret rite of the Mithraic mysteries despite scholarly debates over its authenticity.</summary>

<keywords>G.R.S. Mead, Mithraic Ritual, Mithras Liturgy, Albrecht Dieterich, Franz Cumont, Paris Magic Papyrus, PGM IV, Mystery Religions</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the origins of the Mithraic Ritual, focusing on the linguistic layers added by Egyptian magicians and the potential Persian or Chaldean roots of its "words of power."</meta>

plainly worked over by a school of Egyptian magicians, who inserted most of the now unintelligible words and names (<note>original: "ashma onomata," referring to "meaningless" or "unutterable" names</note>, <note>original: "nomina barbara," a term for foreign, non-Greek names used in magic</note>, <note>original: "nomina arcana," Latin for "secret names"</note>), and vowel-combinations and permutations (<note>original: "voces {10} mysticæ," Latin for "mystic voices" or sounds</note>), of their theurgic <note>Theurgy is a form of "high magic" or ritual practice intended to invoke the presence of a god or to achieve union with the divine.</note> language, which were known in Egypt as "words of power."

The subject is naturally one of the most obscure that is known to scholarship, and so far no one has thrown any real light on it. That, however, there was once in Egypt and Chaldæa <note>Chaldæa was a region in ancient Mesopotamia (modern-day Iraq) famous in antiquity for its advanced astrology and occult sciences.</note> a science of this "nature language," or "tongue of the gods," which subsequently passed into the superstition of a purely mechanical tradition, is highly probable; and one means towards a recovery of the understanding of its nature is a study of the still living tradition of the science of mantras (<note>original: "mantra-vidya"</note>), or the science of mystic utterances and invocations (<note>original: "mantrah"</note>), in India of to-day.

When these evidently later insertions are removed, there still remains a certain number of secret names (<note>original: "nomina arcana"</note>) and mystic voices (<note>original: "mystica voces"</note>) which cannot be removed without doing violence to the text. It, therefore, follows that these stand as part of the Ritual. Did they, however, form part {11} of the *original* Ritual? The original Ritual must have contained, one would have imagined, Persian names. But the distinguished scholar Bartholomae <note>Christian Bartholomae (1855–1925) was a renowned linguist specializing in Iranian languages and the Zoroastrian Avesta.</note>, whom Dieterich has called in to his assistance, declares that nothing Persian can be made out of them without violent changes of the letters. But why, it might be asked, should not the original Persian Ritual have contained secret names (<note>original: "nomina arcane"</note>) taken over from Chaldæa? However this may be, our Greek Ritual evidently contained certain names and words "of power," before it reached the hands of the Egyptian

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the analysis of the text's layers, specifically addressing how a "magical school" added complex chants to an older, core ritual intended for the highest level of spiritual initiation.</meta>

magical school who inserted the majority of the mantric formulas <note>original: "mantric formulæ"; referring to repeated sacred sounds or chants used in meditation and ritual.</note> in our present text.

The latter are, of course, entirely eliminated from the translation, while the former are marked by <term>obeli <note>Plural of obelus (†); symbols used by ancient scholars to mark passages in a manuscript that were suspected of being added by a later hand or were otherwise questionable.</note></term>.

On the whole the most likely supposition is that we have before us (when the latter insertions are removed) a Ritual translated or paraphrased into Greek, and adapted for use in Egypt, {12} and that, too, for picked members of the most esoteric circles. For our Ritual is not for the initiation of a neophyte of the lower grades, but for a candidate who is to self-initiate himself in the solitary mystery of <term>apotheosis <note>The process of a human being being elevated to divine status or becoming a god.</note></term>, whereby he became a true "Father" of the inmost rites, one possessing face to face knowledge and <term>gnosis <note>A Greek word for "knowledge," specifically direct, experiential spiritual knowledge rather than intellectual understanding.</note></term>.

Dieterich <note>Albrecht Dieterich (1866–1908), a German classical scholar who was the first to identify and publish this text as a "Mithraic Liturgy."</note> thinks that this Greek ritual was first made in Egypt about 100-150 A.D., and was used in the Mysteries until 200 A.D. It was then that it got into the hands of the magical school, and was included, together with many other pieces, some of them similarly treated, in a collection which was copied on the papyrus which we now possess, about 300 A.D.

It is exceedingly probable, therefore, that we have in this Ritual of initiation certain <term>theurgic <note>Theurgy refers to "divine work" or high magic—rituals intended to invite the presence of a god or to achieve union with the divine.</note></term> practices of Egyptian tradition combined with the traditional <term>Mithraic <note>Refers to the cult of Mithras, a mystery religion centered on the god Mithras that was popular throughout the Roman Empire.</note></term> invocations done into Greek.

As to the chanting of the vowels, it is {13} of interest to learn from Demetrius, *On Interpretation*, chapter 71 (page 20 of the Radermacher edition), that: 

> "In Egypt the priests hymn the Gods by means of the seven vowels, chanting them in order; instead of the pipe and lute the musical chanting of these letters is heard. So that if you were to take away

<summary>This page explores the origins and structure of the Greek Ritual, arguing it is a self-initiation rite for advanced practitioners seeking divinization through a blend of Egyptian theurgy and Mithraic tradition. It also highlights the ancient practice of chanting vowels as a form of divine musical praise used by Egyptian priests.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of ritual sounds, moving from the general influence of magical schools to the specific theories of the second-century mystic Nicomachus of Gerasa regarding "unarticulated" sacred sounds.</meta>

this accompaniment you would simply remove the whole melody and music of the <term>Word <note>original: "logos"; here referring to the divine utterance or the reasoned structure of the ritual.</note></term>."

The statement of Nicomachus of Gerasa the "musician" and mystic (second century A.D.), is still clearer; for he not only tells us about the vowels and consonants, but also of certain other "unarticulated" sounds which were used by the <term>theurgists <note>Ancient ritualists who performed "god-working" to communicate with or invoke divine beings.</note></term>, and which are directed to be used in the rubrics of our Ritual. In speaking of the vowels or "sounding letters"—each of the seven spheres being said to give forth a different vowel or nature-tone—Nicomachus (c. 6) informs us that these root-sounds in nature are combined with certain material elements, as they are in spoken speech with the {14} consonants; but "just as the soul with the body, and music with the lyre-strings, the one produces living creatures and the other musical modes and tunes, so do those root-sounds give birth to certain energic and initiatory powers of divine operations. It is because of this that whenever theurgists are awe-struck in any such operation, they make invocation symbolically by means of "hissings" and "poppings" and un-articulated and discordant sounds.

The exact translation of the Greek terms, <term>hissing <note>original: "surigmoj" (syringmos).</note></term> and <term>clicking <note>original: "poppusmoj" (poppysmos); a sound made by smacking or clucking the lips or tongue.</note></term> is somewhat of a difficulty. The first denotes a shrill piping sound or hissing, the Latin <term>harsh grating <note>original: "stridor".</note></term>. It is used of such different sounds as the rattling of ropes, the trumpeting of elephants and a singing in the ears. The second is used of a clicking or clucking with the lips and tongue, and of the whistling, cheeping, chirruping, warbling or trilling of birds. It is used of the smack of a loud kiss and also of the cry "hush." Both Aristophanes and Pliny {15} tell us that it was

It highlights the belief that these sounds correspond to the seven celestial spheres and possess initiatory energy.</summary>
