--- Page 94 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of ritual sounds (hissings and clicking) mentioned by Nicomachus of Gerasa. It explains their function as naturalistic greetings to divine forces like lightning and compares these Western techniques to the Indian system of yoga.</meta>

used as a protection against, or rather a reverent greeting of,
lightning; and the latter adds that this was a universal custom.

The English "pop" perhaps represents the idea of the Greek <note>original: "poppysmos," a clucking or smacking sound made with the lips.</note> most
nearly. In the Ritual, however, I have rendered it by "puff" as it is
connected with breath. It is evident that we have here to do with
certain nature-sounds, which have disappeared from articulate
speech, except in some primitive languages such as the "clicking"
of the Zulus. It pertains to the art of *<term>word-making <note>original: "onomatopiia".</note></term>* or
*<term>word-creation <note>original: "onomatopoiesis".</note></term>*, or the forming of words expressive of natural
sounds. The root-idea seems to be that in mystic operations
designed to bring man in touch with the hidden powers of nature,
the language of nature must be employed.

As we have said, the Ritual before us is not of the nature of a
church or temple service; on the contrary, it contains directions for
a solitary sacrament, in which the whole effort of the celebrant {16}
is to stir into activity, and bring into conscious operation, his own
hidden nature or the root-substance of his being. It is a <term>union-rite <note>original: "yoga-rite".</note></term>
(<term>mystical union <note>original: "unio mystica".</note></term>), or act for union, in which the physical breath, the
etheric currents, and the psychic radiations, or <term>life-breaths <note>original: "pranas"; the vital life-force in Indian philosophy.</note></term>
work together with the inbreathing of the Great Breath, or Holy
Spirit, or <term>Energy of the Self <note>original: "Atmic Energy"; from Atman, the Sanskrit term for the supreme soul or self.</note></term>.

It should therefore prove of very great interest to many who have
of late heard much concerning union-practices <note>original: "yoga".</note>, both in its higher
contemplative modes, and also in its modes of deep and psychic
breathing (<term>physical yoga <note>original: "hatha-yoga".</note></term>); for it may be news to many that in the
ancient West, especially in **Egypt**, there was a high art of this
selfsame <term>spiritual union <note>original: "yoga".</note></term> which has been developed so elaborately in India.

<summary>This page explains that the "popping" and "hissing" sounds in the ritual are "nature-sounds" intended to connect the practitioner with natural forces. It defines the ritual as a solitary practice of mystical union, drawing direct parallels between ancient Egyptian/Western traditions and Indian Yoga.</summary>

<keywords>ritual sounds, onomatopoeia, mystical union, breathing exercises, Egypt, yoga, prana, Atman, hatha-yoga</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the theoretical discussion of sacred sounds and "yoga-like" breath-work into the actual text of the Mithraic Ritual. It begins the formal liturgy with the Father’s Prayer and the first section of the invocatory utterance, focusing on the purification and recognition of the elemental components of the self.</meta>

We will now give a translation of the Ritual and then proceed to comment on it. The prayers and utterances are printed in italics, and the rubrics or instructions in Roman type.

# The Ritual

->**I.**<-

### [THE FATHER'S PRAYER.]

> *O Providence, O Fortune, bestow on me Thy Grace — imparting these the Mysteries a <term>Father <note>In the Mithraic mysteries, "Father" (Pater) was the title for the seventh and highest grade of initiation.</note></term> only may hand on, and that, too, to a Son alone — his Immortality — [a Son] initiate, worthy of this our Craft, with which Sun Mithras, the Great God, commanded me to be endowed by His Archangel; so that I, Eagle [as I am, by mine own self] alone, may soar to Heaven, and contemplate all things.*

---

->**II.**<-

### THE INVOCATORY UTTERANCE (<term>Sacred Word <note>original: "Logos".</note></term>).

> 1. *O Primal Origin of my origination; Thou Primal Substance of my substance; First Breath of breath, the breath that is in me; First Fire, God-given for the Blending of the blendings in me, [First Fire] of fire in me; First Water of [my] water, the water in me; Primal Earth-essence of the earthy essence in me; Thou Perfect Body of me — <note>The letters "N. N." serve as placeholders where the practitioner would insert their own name and the names of their parents.</note> N. N. son of N. N., son of N.N. (fem.) — fashioned by Honoured Arm and Incorruptible Right Hand, in World that's lightless, yet radiant with Light, [in World] that's soulless, yet filled full of Soul!*

<summary>This page introduces the "Mithras Liturgy," beginning with a prayer for divine grace and a profound invocation where the practitioner calls upon the primal elements—fire, water, air, and earth—that compose their physical and spiritual being.</summary>
<keywords>Mithras, Ritual, Father's Prayer, Logos, Invocation, Elements, Initiation, Alchemy of the Soul</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Invocatory Utterance" (the Sacred Word) of the Mithraic Ritual. The practitioner, having acknowledged their elemental composition, now petitions for a spiritual transformation and a vision of the divine realm, moving from the physical state toward a state of immortality.</meta>

> 2. *If, indeed, it seems good to you, transport me—now held by my lower nature—into the <term>Birth that is free from Death <note>original: "palingenesia". This refers to a spiritual rebirth or a return to a divine state of being.</note></term>; so that, beyond the pressing Need that weighs on me, I may have a vision of the Deathless Source. Let this be by the power of the Deathless Spirit, the Deathless Water, the Deathless Earth, and the Deathless Air; so that I may be born again in the <term>Higher Mind <note>original: "Nous". In ancient philosophy, this is the highest part of the soul, capable of perceiving divine truth.</note></term>; so that I may become an initiate and the Holy Breath may breathe within me; so that I may marvel at the Holy Fire; that I may see the Deep of the New Dawn, the Water that makes the soul tremble; and that the life-giving <term>Ether <note>original: "Aether". The "fifth element" believed to fill the space of the heavens beyond the earthly sphere.</note></term> which surrounds all things may grant me hearing.*

> 3. *For today I am to look with immortal eyes—I, a mortal born of a mortal womb, but now transformed by the power of a Mighty Strength, yes, by the Incorruptible Right Hand of God. By the power of the Deathless Spirit, I am to see today the <term>Eternal Age <note>original: "Aeon". Here it refers to the personification of infinite time and the master of the celestial spheres.</note></term>, the lord of the Crowns of Fire. I have been purified with sacred purities, and my human soul-power remains pure within me for a little while. This power I shall receive back again after it is passed to me beyond the bitter, pressing weight of <term>Necessity <note>original: "Ananke". The personification of fate or the inescapable laws of the physical universe that demand "debts" (death and decay) be paid.</note></term>, whose debts can never go unpaid. I— <note>The letters "N. N." serve as placeholders where the practitioner would speak their own name and their mother's name.</note> N. N., son of N. N. (mother)—act according to the Decree of God that can never be changed.*

> 4. *For it is beyond my own reach, born as I am beneath the power of Death, to soar into the Height by myself, along with the golden light of the Splendor that knows no death.*

<vocab>Birth free from death, Deathless Source, Deathless Spirit, Eternal Age, Crowns of Fire, Decree of God</vocab>

<summary>The initiate prays for a spiritual rebirth into immortality, asking to transcend the laws of Fate and physical decay to perceive the divine elements and the Eternal Age. The text emphasizes that such an ascent to the celestial heights is impossible for a mortal without divine intervention and purification.</summary>
<keywords>rebirth, immortality, Aeon, Pneuma, Aether, Fate, divine vision, Mithraic mysteries</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the narrative of the practitioner’s ascent. The previous page concluded with the initiate’s soul being purified and prepared to face the "Eternal Age." This section begins with a final command to the mortal self before the first practical ritual instruction for the vision begins.</meta>

5. *Stay still, O <term>nature <note>original: "physis". Referring here to the biological, physical state of human existence which is prone to decay.</note></term> doomed to Perish, [nature] of men subject to Death! And straightway let me pass beyond the <term>implacable Necessity <note>original: "ananke". This is the cosmic force of fate that binds all mortals to their destiny and the cycle of birth and death.</note></term> that presses on me; for that I am His Son; I breathe; I am!*

---

## III.

### [THE FIRST INSTRUCTION.]

1. Take from the [Sun-]rays breath, inhaling thrice [as deeply] as thou canst; and thou shalt see thyself being raised aloft, and soaring towards the Height, so that thou seem'st to be in midst of Air.

2. Thou shalt hear naught, nor man nor beast; nor shalt thou see aught of the sights upon the earth, in that same hour; but all things thou shalt see will be immortal.

3. For thou shalt see, in that same day and hour, the <term>Divine Ordering <note>original: "diakosmesis". This refers to the sacred arrangement or "disposition" of the gods within the celestial spheres.</note></term> of the Gods – the Ruling Gods ascending heavenwards, the other ones descending. And through his Disk - the God's, my Father's - there shall be seen the <term>Pathway <note>original: "hodos". Literally a "way" or "road," describing the visible trajectory the gods take through the heavens.</note></term> of the Gods accessible to sight.

4. And in like fashion also [shall be seen] the **Pipe**, as it is called, whence comes the Wind in service [for the day]. For thou shalt see as though it were a Pipe depending from His Disk; and toward the regions Westward, as though it were an infinite East Wind. But if the other Wind, toward the regions of the East should be in service, in the like fashion shalt thou see, toward the regions of that [side,] the converse of the sight. <note>This "Pipe" (aulos) is a famous and unique feature of the Mithras Liturgy, describing a celestial tube through which the winds are directed by the sun.</note>

<summary>This page describes the beginning of the initiate's ritual ascent, providing breathing instructions to achieve a state of levitation and a vision of the celestial hierarchy and the solar "Pipe" that controls the winds.</summary>
<keywords>ascent, Sun-rays, Necessity (Ananke), Divine Ordering (Diakosmesis), Solar Disk, Celestial Pipe, Winds, Immortality</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructional narrative of the initiate’s ascent through the celestial spheres. Having just declared their divine nature as the "Son" of the God, the practitioner now encounters the visual presence of the deities. This section provides the specific ritual gestures and sacred "utterances" (formulae) needed to safely navigate this encounter.</meta>

5. And you will see the Gods staring intensely at you and rushing toward you. Immediately place your <term>right finger <note>original: "dexios". In ancient ritual practice, the right hand was considered the side of power and auspiciousness.</note></term> on your lips and say:

## **IV.**

### [THE FIRST UTTERANCE.]

*Silence! Silence! Silence!*

The <term>Symbol <note>original: "symbolon". A sacred password or token used to recognize a member of a mystery cult or to gain passage through divine realms.</note></term> of the Living God, who is beyond all corruption.

*Protect me, Silence!* [Sacred sign]!

Next, make a long "hissing" sound: *Sss! Sss!*

Then "puff" your breath, saying: [Sacred name]!

And at that moment, you will see the Gods looking kindly upon you, no longer rushing toward you, but continuing on with the <term>orderly arrangement <note>original: "epi ten idian taxin". This refers to the strict hierarchy and movement of the stars and gods in the heavens.</note></term> of their own divine tasks.

## **V.**

### [THE SECOND INSTRUCTION.]

When you see the <term>Upper Cosmos <note>original: "hyperkosmos". The region of the universe beyond the visible stars, considered the dwelling place of the highest divinity.</note></term> clean and clear, with none of the Gods or <term>Angels <note>original: "angeloi". Here used to describe divine messengers or lesser deities who serve the higher powers.</note></term> rushing at you, prepare yourself to hear a massive thunder-clap, loud enough to startle you.

Then say again:

### THE [SECOND] <term>UTTERANCE <note>original: "Logos". In this ritual context, a Logos is more than just a word; it is a sacred formula or "Word of Power" that has the ability to affect the divine realm.</note></term>.

1. *O Silence! Silence!*

<summary>This page details the ritual gestures and vocalizations—silence, hissing, and puffing—required to appease the gods during the initiate's celestial ascent. It warns the practitioner of a coming thunder-clap once the "Upper Cosmos" is reached.</summary>
<keywords>ritual silence, divine vision, sacred utterance, Upper Cosmos, celestial hierarchy, Logos, protective gesture</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the initiate's ritual ascent. Having just completed the second utterance and performed the ritual sounds (hissing and puffing), the practitioner is now witnessing the visual transformation of the divine sun-disk and preparing to address the deity with a powerful litany of names.</meta>

*I am a Star, whose course is the same as your course, shining anew from out of the depth <note>original: "bythos". In this context, it refers to the celestial abyss or the primordial depths from which the sun rises.</note>.*

Upon your saying this, the divine disk will immediately start expanding.

2. And after you have said the second <term>Utterance <note>original: "Logos". A sacred formula or ritual speech-act believed to carry divine power.</note></term>—namely, saying "Silence" twice and the rest of the formula—"hiss" twice and "puff" twice. Immediately, you will see a mighty host of five-pointed stars emerging from His Disk and filling all the air.

3. Then say again:

> *O Silence! Silence!*

And when His Disk has opened fully, you will behold an infinite encircling and Doors of Fire that are fast closed.

Immediately then, start the utterance that follows while closing your eyes:

### THE THIRD UTTERANCE (LOGOS).

> 1. *Hear me, give ear to me—<note>The text here uses "N.N. son of N.N.," a standard placeholder. The practitioner would insert their own name and their mother's name here to identify themselves to the gods.</note>—O Lord, who with Your Breath have closed the <term>Fiery Bars of Heaven <note>This refers to the celestial boundaries that separate the human world from the divine realm of pure fire.</note></term>; <term>Twin-bodied <note>original: "dimorphos". This may refer to the sun's dual nature as it transitions between the worlds or its perceived male and female aspects.</note></term>; Ruler of the Fire; Creator of the Light; O Holder of the Keys; Inbreather of the Fire; Fire-hearted One, whose Breath gives Light; You who find joy in Fire; Beautiful Light; O Lord of Light, whose Body is made of Fire; Light-giver and <term>Fire-sower <note>A poetic title suggesting the deity scatters light and heat like a farmer scatters seeds.</note></term>; Fire-loosener, whose Life is in the Light; Fire-whirler, who sets the Light in motion; You <term>Thunder-rouser <note>One who awakens the power of the storm.</note></term>; O You Light-glory, Light-increaser; Controller of the <term>Light Empyrean <note>The Empyrean is the highest, most refined level of heaven, consisting of pure fire or light.</note></term>; O You <term>Star-tamer <note>original: "astrodamite". One who subdues or governs the movements and influences of the stars.</note></term>!*

<summary>This page describes the visual climax of the ritual where the sun-disk expands and releases a host of stars. The initiate is then provided with a "Third Utterance," a litany of solar and fire-based titles used to address the supreme deity of the Empyrean.</summary>

<keywords>ritual ascent, sun-disk, sacred silence, fire deity, logos, star-tamer, empyrean, liturgical titles</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the initiate's ritual ascent. In the previous section, the practitioner witnessed the divine sun-disk expanding and stars emerging. They have just been instructed to call out for "Silence" as they stand before the closed "Doors of Fire" at the edge of the heavens, preparing for the next stage of their transformation.</meta>

2. *Oh! Open unto me! For on account of this—the bitter and unavoidable <term>Necessity <note>original: "Ananke". In ancient Greek thought, Necessity was a personified force of fate or cosmic compulsion that governed the movements of the stars and the destiny of all beings.</note></term> that presses upon me—I do invoke Your <term>Immortal Names <note>original: "Onomata athanata". These are the sacred, hidden names of the gods that were believed to hold the literal power of the deities themselves.</note></term>, which are innate with Life, most worshipful, and which have not yet descended into the nature of mortals, nor have been made clear by human tongue, or the cry or tone of man:*

· ieö · ëeö · oëeeoë · ëeö · euö · oë · eiö · ëö · öë · öë · öë · ee · ooouiöë!
<note>These strings of vowels are "voces magicae" (magical words). Ancient ritualists believed that these specific sounds, often associated with the seven planets, could resonate with the divine realm in a way that normal human language could not.</note>

3. Utter all these with <term>Fire and Spirit <note>This is a ritual instruction to recite the names with intense focus and internal energy, as if the breath itself is catching fire.</note></term> once until the end; and then begin again a second time, until you have completed [invoking] the <term>Seven Immortal Gods of the Cosmos <note>These are the seven planetary deities (Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn) who were thought to guard the celestial spheres.</note></term>.

When you have uttered them, thunders and crashings shall you hear in the <term>Surround <note>original: "periechon". This refers to the celestial vault or the atmosphere encompassing the practitioner.</note></term>, and you will feel yourself shaking with every crash. Then once more utter *Silence!* [and then] the utterance [following it].

4. Thereon open your eyes; and you shall see the Doors thrown open, and the <term>Cosmos of the Gods <note>original: "Kosmos ton theon". The ordered, divine realm that exists beyond the physical sky, visible only to the enlightened soul.</note></term> that is within the Doors; so that for the joy and rapture of the sight, your <term>Spirit <note>original: "pneuma". The practitioner's vital breath or soul-essence.</note></term> runs to meet it, and soars up.

Therefore, hold yourself steady, and, gazing steadily into yourself, draw breath from the <term>Divine <note>original: "theion". The sacred, celestial air that sustains the gods.</note></term>.

When, then, your <term>Soul <note>original: "psyche".</note></term> shall be restored, say:

### **VIII.**

### **[THE FOURTH UTTERANCE.]**

<summary>The initiate recites a powerful string of ritual sounds to invoke the seven planetary gods, causing the celestial doors to burst open amidst thunder. The practitioner is then instructed to breathe in the divine essence to steady their soul before the next prayer.</summary>
<keywords>Necessity, Ananke, Immortal Names, Seven Immortal Gods, Surround, periechon, Spirit, pneuma, Divine, theion, Fourth Utterance, voces magicae</keywords>

1. > *Draw nigh, O Lord!*

Upon this utterance, His rays shall be turned toward you, and you shall stand in the very midst of them.

2. When you have done this, you will behold a God, in the prime of youth, of the most exquisite beauty, with <term>Locks of Flame <note>original: "chaitas pyrinos". This vivid description identifies the deity as a solar figure, likely Helios-Mithras, whose hair is depicted as literal rays of light or fire.</note></term>, dressed in a white tunic and a scarlet mantle, and wearing a crown of fire. Immediately greet Him with the Salutation of the Fire:

## **IX.**

### [THE FIFTH UTTERANCE.]

1. > *Hail, Lord! O You of mighty power; O King of mighty sway; Greatest of Gods; O Sun; You Lord of Heaven and Earth; O God of Gods! Strong is Your breath; strong is Your might!*
>
> *O Lord, if it seems good to You, announce my arrival to the <term>God Most High <note>original: "Theos Hypsistos". A common title in the Greco-Roman world for the supreme deity, often used by both pagans and Jews to describe the ultimate source of divinity.</note></term>, who has begotten and created You!*

2. > *For a man—<note>The original text uses "N.N.", a placeholder for the initiate's name (Nomen Nominandum).</note> [Name], son of [Mother's Name], born of a mortal womb and of <term>spermatic ichor <note>original: "spermatikou ichoros". In this context, it refers to the biological fluids of human conception, emphasizing the initiate's humble, earthly beginnings compared to their impending divine state.</note></term>—yes, of this very essence which at Your hands today has undergone the <term>transmutation of rebirth <note>original: "palingenesia". This is the spiritual "re-birth" or "regeneration" required for a mortal to enter the divine realm.</note></term>—one man among tens of thousands, transformed to <term>immortality <note>original: "athanasia". The central goal of this ritual is to transcend the death-bound nature of humanity.</note></term> in this very hour by the good-pleasure of God, the Transcendent Good—[this man, I say,] presumes to worship You and prays with whatever power a mortal possesses.*

3. Upon this utterance, He shall come to the <term>Pole <note>The celestial pole or the axis of the universe, which was believed to be the dwelling place of the highest gods and the "pivot" around which the heavens turned.</note></term>, and you shall see Him moving along a path as if on a circuit.

<summary>This page describes the appearance of the solar deity (Helios-Mithras) and provides the prayer the initiate must recite to claim their new, immortal status. The ritual emphasizes the practitioner's transformation from a biological, mortal human into a being worthy of standing before the supreme God.</summary>

Then gaze intently, and send forth a prolonged <term>bellowing <note>original: "mykēma". Kiss your <term>amulets <note>In the Greek Magical Papyri, these were usually inscribed gemstones or small metal scrolls (lamellae) worn to protect the practitioner from the overwhelming power of the gods.</note></term>, and speak first to the one on your right side:

## X.

### [THE SIXTH UTTERANCE.]

> *Protect me! <note>The symbol (†) in the original indicates the use of "voces magicae"—sacred, untranslatable magical names or strings of vowels that the practitioner would chant here.</note>!*

When you have uttered this, you shall behold the Doors thrown open. Issuing from the Depth, seven virgins will appear, dressed in <term>byssus robes <note>Byssus was an exceptionally fine and costly linen used in antiquity, often associated with priestly garments and royal burials.</note></term>, with the faces of serpents and golden sceptres in their hands. These are the ones known as the **Fortunes of Heaven** <note>original: "Tychai". In Greek thought, Tyche was the personification of luck and destiny. Here, they appear as a group of seven celestial guardians.</note>.

When you see these things, offer your salutation in this way:

2. Following these, others come forth—Seven Gods, possessing the faces of black bulls, dressed in linen loincloths and wearing seven golden fillets <note>original: "diademata". ## XII.

### [THE EIGHTH UTTERANCE.]

1. > *Hail, ye Guardians of the Pivot, sacred and sturdy Youths! Ye who all at once revolve the spinning Axis of Heaven's Circle; ye who let loose the thunder and the lightning, and the shocks of earthquakes and thunder-bolts against the companies of the impious; but [who grant] to me—who am pious and a worshipper of God—health and the wholeness of my body in every part, and the proper keenness of my hearing and my sight, and a state of calm in the present good hours of this day, O mighty Ruling Lords and Gods of mine!*

> *Hail to you, the Second, †!*

> *Hail to you, the Third, †!*

> *Hail to you, the Fourth, †!*

> *Hail to you, the Fifth, †!*

2. Now, when all these deities are present in their proper order, look intently into the air. You will see flashes of lightning, quivering lights, and the earth will begin to shake. Then, you will see a God descending—a being of immense, transcending presence, with a radiant appearance and golden hair. He is in the flower of his youth, dressed in a robe of brilliant light, wearing a golden crown, and clad in trousers <note>original: "anaxyrides". These were traditional Persian-style garments, signaling the god's eastern or Mithraic origins.</note>. In his right hand, he holds the golden shoulder of a young bull.

This shoulder is the constellation of the Great Bear <note>In ancient Egyptian and Greek magic, the "Thigh" or "Shoulder" of the bull represented the Great Bear (Ursa Major), the constellation that appears to pivot the entire sky around the North Star.</note> which moves the dome of the heavens, changing its direction up or down according to the hour.

Then you will see lightning leaping from his eyes and stars springing forth from his body.

3. Immediately let out a long "bellowing" <note>original: "mookhthismos". This was a ritual vocalization mimicking the sound of a bull, performed by tensing the abdominal muscles to "start the senses going" and align the practitioner's breath with the divine vibration.</note> using the pressure of your belly to activate all your senses at once. Sustain this sound until the very end, then kiss your amulets <note>These are the protective charms or inscribed stones the practitioner prepared before starting the rite.</note> and say:

## XIII.

### [THE NINTH UTTERANCE]

> †, [O Lord] of me—<term>N. N.</term> <note>A placeholder for "Nomen Nominandum," where the practitioner speaks their own name.</note>—stay with me in my soul! Do not leave me! For † commands you to remain.

And look fixedly at the God, while bellowing once more, and salute him in this way:

### [THE TENTH UTTERANCE]

<summary>The practitioner witnesses the descent of a radiant, golden-haired God holding the "Shoulder of the Calf" (the Great Bear), marking the climax of the celestial ascent. The ritual requires the practitioner to perform a deep, abdominal "bellowing" to harmonize with the deity's power.</summary>

--- Page 105 ---
<meta>This text continues the ritual of the "Mithras Liturgy" from the Great Magical Papyrus of Paris. Following the appearance of the solar deity and the ritual "bellowing" performed by the initiate, the practitioner now addresses the god directly to confirm their transformation from a mortal being into an immortal one.</meta>

->*Hail, Lord, Master of the Water! Hail, Creator of the Earth!*<-
->*Hail, Ruler of the Breath of Life!*<-

*O Lord, as I am born again, I pass away while being made Great; and, having been made Great, I die.* <note>This paradoxical language describes the process of "apotheosis" or deification. The initiate's mortal self must "die" or be eclipsed so that their soul can expand to a divine, "Great" scale.</note>

*Having been born from the cycle of birth and death <note>original: "palingenesia". While often translated as "rebirth," in this context, it refers to the exhausting cycle of mortal reincarnation from which the initiate seeks escape.</note> that produces mortal life, I am now set free. I pass into the state that transcends birth, just as You have established it, and according to the Mystery <term>Mystery: a secret religious rite or knowledge revealed only to initiated followers, intended to bring them into a personal relationship with a deity.</term> You have ordained and performed.*

<summary>The initiate recites a solemn prayer of transformation, declaring their transition from a mortal existence governed by the cycle of birth and death into a permanent, divine state of being.</summary>
<keywords>rebirth, deification, immortality, Mystery, elements, transcendence</keywords>

# The Demotic Magical Papyrus of London and Leiden

## COLUMN I.

(1) [A ritual for vessel-divination <term>vessel-divination: also known as lecanomancy, this is a method of prophecy where a practitioner or a medium gazes into a bowl of liquid—usually oil or water—to see visions of gods or the future.</term> which a physician?] of the district of Pemze <note>original: "Pemze," the Egyptian name for the city of Oxyrhynchus, famous for its vast archaeological finds.</note> [gave to me]. The procedure is as follows: (2) ‘[O god, Name of God]... the edges of whose belt (?) rest in Peremoun <note>original: "Peremoun," likely referring to the city of Pelusium located at the eastern edge of the Nile Delta.</note>, whose face shines like a spark (3) ... of a fierce (?) cat, whose toes are like a rising cobra <term>cobra: a "uraeus," the sacred rearing serpent found on the crowns of Egyptian gods and royalty, symbolizing protection and divine authority.</term> (4) ... come quickly; grant light and clarity within my vessel (5) ... Open the earth for me, open the Underworld for me, open the abyss for me, (6) ... the great... made of bronze from Alkhah, you gods who dwell in heaven, you who are exalted, come forth (7) ... [grant] light and clarity within my vessel, (8) ... [this] boy, whose face is looking into this vessel of oil; make this ritual succeed (9) ... for this divination is the very ritual used by the goddess Isis when she went searching <note>A reference to Isis searching for the body of her husband, Osiris. By invoking this myth, the practitioner gives the ritual cosmic authority.</note> (10) ... come to me, O my compelling spirit (?), for everything (11) ...’ and cause the eyes of this child to be opened to them all, (12) ... for I am the Pharaoh, the Lion-Ram; my name is Ram-Lion-Lotus (13) ... to you here today, for I am Sit-ta-ko, my name is Setem, Setem (14) [is my true name, and so on]. Hrenoute, Lapptotha, Laxantha, Sa-ij-risa, [and so on] ... Bolbouel (twice), Louteri, Klo-Kasantra, Iaho <note>original: "Iaho," a Greek-Egyptian rendering of the Hebrew name for God, Yahweh, frequently used in magical spells as a word of power.</note> (16) [is my name, and so on, Balkam the] terrifying one of heaven, Ablanathanalba <note>original: "Ablanathanalba," a famous magical palindrome—reading the same forward and backward—common in ancient protective amulets.</note>, the gryphon (17) [of the temple of God, and so on].’

You whisper these words down into his head seven times; when you utter this, then (19) his ears speak. <note>In the context of ancient mediumship, "ears speaking" likely refers to the boy experiencing a buzzing, ringing, or a clear auditory sensation that signals the presence of the spirits.</note> If both of his ears speak, he is very good; if it be his right ear, (20) he is good; if it be his left ear, he is bad.

### Ritual Preparations for the Vessel

**Prescription for enchanting the vessel quickly so that the gods enter and tell (21) you the answer truthfully.**

You put the shell of a crocodile's egg, or that which is inside it, on the flame; it will be enchanted instantly. <note>The crocodile was sacred to the god Sobek, but its eggs were also potent symbols of solar birth and protection in Egyptian magic.</note>

**Prescription to make them (22) speak:** you put a frog's head on the brazier <term>brazier: A portable metal container for hot coals, used here to burn ritual ingredients and create smoke (fumigation).</term>, then they speak.

**Prescription for bringing the gods in by force:** you put the bile (23) of a crocodile with pounded frankincense on the brazier. <note>Magical "force" was often used when a deity was perceived as reluctant; pungent or sacred substances like bile and frankincense were thought to compel their appearance.</note>

If you wish to make them come in quickly again, you put stalks (?) of anise (?) on the brazier together with the (24) egg-shell as above, then the charm works at once.

### Summoning Other Entities

If you wish to bring in a living man, you put sulphate of copper on the brazier, then he comes in.

(25) If you wish to bring in a spirit, you put *sa-wr*-stone <term>sa-wr-stone: Likely a reference to a specific mineral such as lapis lazuli or a similar dark, "great" stone used in Egyptian ritual practice.</term> with stone of *ilkh* <term>ilkh-stone: An unidentified ritual mineral, possibly related to the "stone of the moon" or a specific pebble used in divination.</term> on the brazier, then the spirit comes in. You put the heart (26) of a hyaena or a hare, excellent (*bis*). <note>original: "bis" (meaning "twice" or "repeat")</note>

If you wish to bring in a drowned man <note>The spirits of those who died by drowning (the *hesy*) were considered particularly powerful and divinely touched in ancient Egypt, as they had met their end in the sacred Nile.</note>, you put sea-*karab*-stone (?) on the brazier.

### **COLUMN VI.**

#### **(1) An inquiry of the lamp.**

You must go to a clean, dark chamber without light and dig a new hole in a wall facing the east (2) and take a white lamp that has not been treated with <term>minium</term> <note>original: "minium" — a red lead pigment often used for decoration or sealing; its absence here ensures the lamp's ritual purity.</note> or <term>gum water</term> <note>A binder made from plant resins.</note>. Its wick must be clean. Fill it with pure, genuine <term>Oasis oil</term> <note>High-quality oil produced in the Egyptian oases, frequently specified in magical papyri for its superior quality.</note>, (3) and recite the prayers of praise for <term>Ra</term> <note>The Egyptian sun god, representing the supreme source of light and revelation.</note> at dawn during his rising. Bring the lit lamp and hold it opposite the sun, reciting the spells provided below four times. (4) Then, take the lamp into the chamber. You must be ritually pure, as must the boy who assists you. Recite the spells to the boy while his eyes are (5) closed, repeating them seven times. Place pure frankincense upon the <term>brazier</term> <note>A portable metal stand or pot used for burning charcoal and incense.</note>. Place your finger on the boy's head while his eyes remain closed. (6) When you have finished, have him open his eyes toward the lamp; he will then see the shadow of the god surrounding the lamp, and he will ask on your behalf (7) regarding whatever you desire to know.

If you are seeking an inquiry from a <term>spirit damned</term> <note>original: "spirit damned" — likely referring to a *nekydaimon*, the spirit of one who died prematurely or violently, often used in Greco-Egyptian magic for their proximity to the physical world.</note>, you must perform this at midday in a place without light. A wick made of <term>sail-cloth</term> (8) is what you should place in the lamp, and you must fill it with clean butter.

If the ritual is for any other business, use a clean wick with pure, genuine oil. (9) If you are performing the ritual to bring a woman to a man, you must put an ointment of roses into the lamp. You...

### **The Procedure**

### The Rite of the Vessel

(18) ...Take care that the sun does not reach the water; you must finish its surface with fine oil. You place the vessel upon **three new bricks**, their lower sides having been sprinkled (19) with sand; you then place four other bricks under the child medium; you make the child lie down upon his stomach; (20) you cause him to place his chin on the brick supporting the vessel; you make him look into the oil, while he has a cloth spread over his head. (21) There must be a lighted lamp on his right, and a censer with fire on his left; you put a leaf of the **Anubis-plant** <note>original: "Anubis-plant". This likely refers to a specific herb, such as the *penthonon* or perhaps a type of lettuce, which was sacred to Anubis in magical traditions.</note> on the lamp, and you put this incense on the fire; you recite these spells, which are written above, to the vessel seven times.

### The Sacred Incense

The incense which you put (23) on the fire:
*   Frankincense
*   Wax
*   Styrax <note>A fragrant, vanilla-scented resin used extensively in ancient Mediterranean rituals.</note>
*   Turpentine resin
*   Date-stones

Grind these ingredients with wine; (24) you make them into a ball and put them on the fire.

### The Vision and Inquiry

When you have finished, you make the child open his eyes, and you ask him, saying, "Is the god coming in?" If he says (25) "The god has come in," you recite this formula before him:

->**The Formula**<-
(25) "O Bull Mao! <note>Possibly a reference to a solar or generative power.</note> Hail, Anubis! This soldier, this Kam, (26) this Kem... Pisreithi, Pisreithi, Sreithi, Sreithi, **Abrithi** is your name, by your true name!" <note>These "true names" or *voces magicae* were believed to be the secret, essential names of the deity that compelled them to act.</note>

(27) You ask him concerning that which you desire; when you have finished the inquiry which you are asking about, you call to him seven times; you dismiss the god to his home.

### The Dismissal

His dismissal formula: (28) "Farewell, farewell Anubis, the good ox-herd; Anubis, Anubis, the son of a jackal and a dog... (Another manuscript says: the child of (29) **Isis** and a dog), **Nabrishoth**, the <term>Cherub</term><note>A term for a guardian spirit, here showing the syncretism between Egyptian and Near Eastern angelic concepts.</note> of <term>Amenti</term><note>The "West," the ancient Egyptian realm of the dead.</note>, king of those of..." 

Say this seven times.

## COLUMN XV

### A Potion for Attraction

(1) **A potion.** You take a small shaving from the head of a man who has died a violent death <note>The spirits of those who died prematurely or violently were believed to be particularly powerful and restless, making them ideal for magical tasks.</note>, (2) along with seven grains of barley that have been buried in the grave of a dead man. You pound these together with ten <term>oipe<gloss>an ancient Egyptian unit of dry measure</gloss></term>—(3) or perhaps nine—of apple-seeds. You then add the blood of a black dog’s worm <note>Possibly a parasite or a specific type of insect associated with dogs.</note> to them, along with a little (4) blood from your second finger (the one associated with the heart) of your left hand, and your own semen. (5) You pound all these together and place them into a cup of wine. Add to this three <term>uteh<gloss>a liquid measure or specific type of sacrificial vessel</gloss></term> of (6) the first-fruits of the grape harvest—ensuring you have not yet tasted the wine and none has been poured out from it yet. 

You must pronounce this invocation over the mixture seven times (7) and then have the woman drink it. Finally, tie the skin of the aforementioned parasite with a band of <term>byssus<gloss>fine, high-quality linen used for priestly garments and mummies</gloss></term> (8) and bind it to your left arm.

## COL. XVII.

(18) Here is another method for this ritual. Rise from your bed early in the morning on the day you intend to perform the rite, or indeed on any day, (19) so that everything you undertake will succeed in your hands—provided you remain pure from all ritual impurities <note>The practitioner was expected to abstain from certain foods and sexual activity to remain "pure" for the gods.</note>. Recite this invocation before **the Sun God** <note>original: 'Phre'—the Egyptian Sun god, Ra.</note> three or seven times:

> (20) "**Io, Tabao, Sokhom-moa, Okh-okh-khan-bouzanau, An-** (21) **iesi, Ekomphtho, Ketho, Sethouri, Thmila, Alouapokhri**, let everything (22) that I set my hand to here today, let it be fulfilled."

**The Procedure:** Take a new lamp that has not been decorated with **red lead pigment** <note>original: 'minium'—a reddish mineral used as a dye or paint; its absence ensured the lamp's ritual purity.</note> and (23) place a clean wick inside it. Fill it with pure, genuine oil and set it in a secluded place that has been purified with **natron water** <note>term: 'natron'—a naturally occurring salt used in ancient Egypt for cleaning, soap-making, and mummification.</note>. (24) Set the lamp upon a new brick. Then, take a young boy and seat him upon another new brick, ensuring his face is (25) turned toward the lamp. Close his eyes and recite the words written above directly into the boy's head seven times. Then, command him to (26) open his eyes and ask him: "Do you see the light?"

When he answers you, "I see the light in the flame of the lamp," you must shout at that exact moment, (27) "**Heoue**" <note>A sacred ritual cry or divine name.</note> nine times. You may then ask him about anything you wish to know, after having first recited the invocation (28) that you performed earlier before **the Sun God** <note>original: 'Phre'</note> in the morning.

Perform this in a room where the entrance opens toward the East. Position the face of the lamp toward the [blank] <note>The scribe left a space here, likely intending to specify a direction like East or West later.</note>. Place the boy (29) so he is facing the lamp, while you stand at his left hand. Cry the spell down into his head and strike his head with your second finger—the [blank] finger <note>This likely refers to the index or middle finger.</note>—of your (30) right hand.

When they are seated, you say to Anubis: "Bring a wine-jar in and some cakes; let them eat, let them drink." <note>This "divine banquet" is a common motif in Egyptian ritual. By offering hospitality to the spirits, the practitioner secures their favor and ensures they are willing to provide truthful answers.</note> (31) While he is making them eat and making them drink, you say to Anubis: "Will they inquire for me today?" If he says "Yes" again, you say to him, (32) "The god who will ask for me, let him put forth his hand to me and let him tell me his name." When he tells you his name, you ask him as to that which you desire. When you have ceased asking him as to that which you desire, you send them away.

### Spell Spoken for a Dog’s Bite

(1) [Spell] spoken for the bite of a dog.
'I have come forth from <term>Arkhah</term><note>Likely a mythological or ritual location, possibly representing the underworld or a place of primal power.</note>, (2) my mouth being full of the blood of a black dog. (3) I spit it out, the <unclear>substance</unclear> of a dog. O this dog, who is among the ten dogs (4) belonging to <term>Anubis</term><note>The jackal-headed Egyptian god of embalming and the underworld. Here, he is invoked as the master of dogs to command them to withdraw their harm.</note>, the son of his own body: extract your venom! Remove your saliva from me once more! (5) If you do not extract your venom and remove your saliva, I will take you (6) up to the court of the temple of <term>Osiris</term><note>The lord of the dead and supreme judge; the magician threatens the dog’s spirit with a formal trial before the highest authority.</note>, my watchtower. I will perform for you the <unclear>sacrifice</unclear> of birds (7) like the voice of <term>Isis</term> the sorceress, the mistress of magic, who enchants everything and is not enchanted herself, (8) in her name of "Isis the Sorceress."'

And you pound garlic with <term>kmou</term><note>original: "kmou" — likely a variant of cumin or a specific mineral powder used in ancient pharmacology.</note> (9) and you put it on the wound of the dog’s bite; and you recite the spell over it daily until it is healed.

(1) The bowl-divination<note>original: "vessel-inquiry"; a ritual known as lecanomancy where a practitioner gazes into a bowl of oil or water to see visions.</note> of **Osiris**<note>The Egyptian god of the afterlife and rebirth.</note>.

(6) Hail to him: **Iaho, Sabaho, Adonai**<note>Greek transliterations of Hebrew divine names: Yahweh, Sabaoth ("Lord of Hosts"), and Adonai ("Lord").</note>, **Mistemu**, **Iauiu**; hail to him: **Michael, Sabael**<note>Archangels frequently invoked in magical papyri for protection and revelation.</note>; (7) hail to him: **Anubis** in the land of the **dog-faces**<note>original: "nome of the dog-faces"; refers to the Cynocephali, mythical baboon-headed creatures often associated with the borders of the known world and the god Anubis.</note>, he to whom this earth belongs, who carries a wound on one foot. Recite this nine times, (9) until the god comes and the light appears.

You must perform it in the same manner as the previous instructions; the boy's face<note>In these rituals, a young boy often acted as a "medium" or "seer" to look into the bowl, as children were thought to be pure enough to see the gods.</note> should be turned toward the East and your own face toward the West; you call the invocation down into his head.

# (10) [The ritual] of the <term>scarab</term><note>The dung beetle (Scarabaeus sacer), which the Egyptians associated with the sun god Khepri and the cycle of rebirth.</note> of the cup of wine, to make a woman love a man.

You take a fish-faced(?) <term>scarab</term>, this <term>scarab</term> being small and having no horn, it wearing three plates (11) on the front of its head; you find its face thin (?) outwards—or again that which bears two horns. You take it at the rising of the sun; you bind yourself with a cloth on the upper part of your back, (12) and bind yourself on your face with a strip of palm-fibre, the <term>scarab</term> being on the front (?) of your hand; and you address it before the sun when it is about to rise, seven times.

When you have finished, you drown it (13) in some milk of a black cow<note>Ritual purity often required the milk of a cow that was entirely black, symbolizing the night or specific underworld powers.</note>; you approach its head with a hoop of olive wood; you leave it until evening in the milk. When evening comes, you (14) take it out, you spread its underpart with sand, and put a circular strip of cloth under it upon the sand, for four days; you perform a frankincense-burning before it. When the four days have passed, and it is dry, (15) you take it before your feet, there being a cloth spread under it. 

You divide it down its middle with a bronze knife; you take its right half, and your nails of your right hand and foot; (16) you cook them on a new <term>potsherd</term><note>original: "ostrakon"; a fragment of broken pottery used here as a ritual crucible.</note> with vine wood, you pound them with nine apple-pips together with your urine or your sweat free from oil (17) of the bath; you make it into a ball and put it in the wine, and speak over it seven times, and you make the woman drink it. 

And you take its other half, the left one, together with your nails of your left hand (18) and foot also, and bind them in a strip of fine linen, with <term>myrrh</term><note>A fragrant resin used in incense and medicine.</note> and <term>saffron</term><note>A costly spice used here for its solar associations and fragrance.</note>, and bind them to your left arm, and lie with the woman with them bound upon you.

### [The spell] which you utter to it when you cook it:

> "O my beautiful child, the youth who eats oil <note>The original phrase is uncertain; it may refer to the beetle’s diet or its role in anointing rituals.</note>, you who have cast your seed and who cast your seed among all the gods. You are the one whom the lowly and the great found among the two great <term>Enneads</term><note>The Enneads were councils or groups of nine major deities in Egyptian mythology who governed the world.</note> in the East of Egypt. ### [The spell] that you pronounce to it in the wine:

> "O scarab, O scarab! ## COL. XXII.

**(1)** Behold! This is the spell of the name of the <term>Great-of-Five</term> <note>A title belonging to Thoth, the god of wisdom, moon, and magic, who was the chief of the "Five" deities of the city of Hermopolis.</note> which they pronounce to every spirit. This is a charm which is in the power of a man to pronounce.

### Column XXIV

**(21)** **To divine, opposite the moon.** You do it by vessel-inquiry <note>original: "lekanomanteia"; a method of divination using a bowl of liquid.</note> alone or with a child assistant. If it is you who will inquire, you fill your eye **(22)** with green eye-paint and <term>stibium</term> <note>original: "stibi"; a black mineral powder (antimony) used as eyeliner and in medicinal/magical preparations.</note>, you stand on a high place, on the top of your house, and you address the moon when it fills **(23)** the **Eye of Horus** <note>original: "uzat" (udjat); the sacred eye of the sky god, representing wholeness and the full moon.</note> on the 15th day. You must remain pure for three days. You pronounce this invocation to the moon seven or nine times until he appears to you **(24)** and speaks to you:

> "Ho! Sax, Arnun, Sax, Abrasax; for you are the moon, **(25)** the chief of the stars, he that did form them, listen to the things that I have said, follow the words of my mouth, reveal yourself to me, Than, **(26)** Thana, Thanatha, otherwise Thei, this is my correct name."

Say it nine times until she <note>The text shifts gender here, reflecting the moon's dual associations with gods like Thoth/Khonsu and goddesses like Selene/Isis.</note> reveals herself to you.

**(27)** **Another form of it again, to be pronounced to the moon.**

You paint your eye with this paint, going up before the moon when it fills the **Eye of Horus**, then you will see the figure of the god in the **Eye of Horus** **(28)** speaking to you.

> "I am Hah, Qo, Amro, Ma-amt, Mete is my name, for I am... bai, So, Akanakoup, **(29)** Meikh, Akh, Akh, Hy, Meikh is my true... eternity, I am Khelbai, Setet, Khen-em-nefer <note>original: "Khen-em-nefer"; likely "He who appears in beauty," a common epithet for lunar or solar deities.</note> is my name, Sro, Oshenbet, is my correct name."

**(30)** Say it nine times. You stand opposite the moon, your eye being filled with this ointment: grind green eye-paint and stibium with Syrian honey and add the gall of a **(31)** full-grown chick to it.

## COLUMN XXIV

(1a) For inducing a trance <note>original: "catalepsy"; a condition of muscle rigidity and lack of response to external stimuli, often sought in ritual contexts.</note> — another version:

(1) A recipe for when you wish to drug <note>The text implies a state of sedation or stupefaction.</note> a man — tested: — (2) <term>scammony root</term> <note>original: "scammony"; a resinous root from the Convolvulus scammonia plant, known in antiquity as a powerful purgative.</note>, 1 <term>drachm</term> <note>a Greek unit of weight, roughly 3.4 to 4.3 grams.</note>, (3) opium, 1 <term>drachm</term>; crush these with milk, (4) form it into a ball and hide it in some food (5) that is being cooked, and let him eat it; he will then be overcome.

(6) Another recipe, for when you wish to make a person sleep for two days: — (7) <term>mandragora root</term> <note>original: "mandragora"; the mandrake plant, famous in the ancient world for its sedative, anesthetic, and hallucinogenic properties.</note>, 1 ounce, (8) liquorice(?), 1 ounce, (9) <term>hyoscyamus</term> <note>original: "hyoscyamus"; henbane, a highly toxic plant used in controlled doses as a sedative and for its psychoactive effects.</note>, 1 ounce, (10) ivy, 1 ounce; (11) crush these and mix with a <term>lok-measure</term> <note>an ancient Egyptian unit of liquid volume, roughly equivalent to 0.5 liters or a pint.</note> of wine.

If you wish to perform this with greater skill: (12) take four portions of each ingredient and mix with an <term>uteh-measure</term> <note>a specific Egyptian measure or vessel used for pouring liquids.</note> of wine, (13) soak them from morning until evening; then strain the liquid, (14) and have them drink it; it is very effective.

Another, the fourth recipe: — seeds [of] (15) apple, 1 <term>stater</term> <note>a Greek weight, usually equal to two drachms.</note>, 1 <term>kite</term> <note>an Egyptian weight, approximately 9.1 grams.</note>, crushed with flour. (16) Shape it into a small cake; have the person you choose eat it.

(17) A recipe for making a man sleep; very effective:

### A Ritual to Place the Heart of a Woman After a Man
A method to put the heart of a woman after a man; done in one moment, and it comes to pass instantly.

You take (24) a **swallow** <note>original: "chelidōn"; in ancient magic, birds were often used as messengers or symbols of the soul's flight.</note> alive, together with a **hoopoe** <note>original: "epops"; a bird with a distinct crest, frequently appearing in Egyptian and Greek magic as a creature of secret knowledge.</note>, both alive. An ointment is made for them: (25) blood of a male ass, and blood of the **tick** <note>the identification of this ingredient is difficult; it likely refers to a parasite found on the animal, believed to carry the "essence" of the beast.</note> of a black cow. You anoint (26) their heads with **lotus ointment**; you utter a cry before the sun in his moment of rising; (27) you cut off the heads of the two; you take the heart out of the right ribs (28) of both of them. You anoint it with the ass's blood and the blood of the tick of a black cow, (29) as aforesaid. You put them into an ass's skin; you lay them in the sun until they (30) are dry, for four days. When the four days have passed, you pound them and you put them into a (31) box; you lay it in your house.

When you wish to make a woman love a man, you take (32) the shaving of a **pleasure-wood** <note>original: "ksylon euphrosunon"; likely a fragrant wood such as cedar or sandalwood, or perhaps wood from a tree associated with Joy or Aphrodite.</note>; you recite these correct names before them; (33) you put it into a cup of wine or beer; you give it to the woman and she drinks it.

### **COLUMN XXVII.**

(1) Another **vessel-divination** <term>vessel-divination: a practice known as lekanomancy, where a magician gazes into a bowl filled with oil or water to see visions of the future or the gods.</term>, to be performed alone, for the purpose of seeing the **bark of Phre** <note>original: "Phre"; the Greek rendering of the Egyptian "Pi-Re," the Sun God. ### The Ritual of Spirit-Gathering

Its **spirit-gathering** <term>spirit-gathering</term> <note>Also known as *systasis*, this is a ritual intended to create a formal introduction or "gathering" between the practitioner and a supernatural being.</note>: you take a young boy who is pure, you make the **spirit-formula** <term>spirit-formula</term> (2) <note>A specific set of spoken words or incantations used to summon a spirit.</note> which is written for it; you take him before the sun; you make him stand on a new brick at the moment at which (3) the sun shall rise, and it comes up entirely with its full disk; you put a new mat of linen behind him; you (4) make him shut his eyes; you stand upright over him; you recite down into his head; you strike down on (5) his head with your **Ra-finger** <note>The ring finger of the right hand, which in ancient Egyptian tradition was associated with the sun god Ra and believed to possess special ritual power.</note> of your right hand, after filling his eye with the paint which you made before: (6)

> “Nasira, Oapkis, Shfe, Shfe, Bibiou, Bibiou is your true name, your true name! <note>These are *voces magicae*, or secret magical names, believed to be the true, hidden names of the deity being called.</note> **Lotus**, open to me heaven (7) in its breadth and height; bring to me the light which is pure; let the god who has the command come to me, and let him give me (8) an answer to everything which I am asking here today, in truth and without any falsehood. Arkhnoutsi, Etale, Tal, (9) Nasira, Yarmekh, Nasera, Amptho, Kho, Amamarkar, Tel, Yaeo, (10) Nasira, Hakia, **Lotus**, Khzisiph, Aho, Atone, I I E O, Balbel, (11) let the pure light come to me; let the boy be enchanted; let an answer be given to me; let the god who has the command come to me and tell (12) me the answer to everything about which I shall ask, in truth and without any falsehood therein.”

Thereafter, you recite his **compulsion** <term>compulsion</term> <note>A stronger form of invocation intended to "force" or "compel" the spirit to appear if the initial prayer does not succeed.</note> another (13) seven times, while his eyes remain shut.

**The Formula:**
> “Si, si, pi, thiripi, S. A. E. O. Nkhab (14) Hrabaot, Phakthiop, Anasan, Kraana, Kratris, Ima- (15) ptaraphne, Araphnu, come to the boy; let the god who has the command come to him, let him tell me the answer to everything (16) which I shall ask here today.”

# CURSE TABLETS

## *D.T. 237* <note>D.T. stands for "Defixionum Tabellae," a classic scholarly catalog of ancient curse tablets collected by Auguste Audollent</note>

I invoke you, <term>spirit of one untimely dead</term> <note>original: "aoros"; in ancient Greek and Roman belief, those who died before their time were thought to linger near their graves, making them available to be "hired" by magicians for tasks</note>, whoever you are, by the mighty names **SALBATHBAL AUTHGEROTABAL BASUTHATEO ALEO SAMMABETHOR** <note>original: "SALBATHBAL AUTHGEROTABAL BASUTHATEO ALEO SAMMABETHOR"; these are "voces magicae" or "magical words"—strings of powerful, untranslatable syllables used to compel spirits</note>.

<term>Bind</term> <note>The core action of a "defixio" (binding spell). The practitioner sought to "bind" or "fix" the physical abilities of their enemies so they could not function</note> the horses whose names and images/likeness on this <term>implement</term> <note>Referring to the lead tablet itself, which often featured crude drawings of the victims to ensure the spirit targeted the correct individuals</note> I entrust to you; of the **Red [team]** <note>The "Reds" and "Blues" were two of the major professional chariot racing factions (teams) in the Roman world, followed by fans with intense, often violent, devotion</note>: **Silvanus**, **Servator**, **Lues**, **Zephyrus**, **Blandus**, **Imbraius**, **Dives**, **Mariscus**, **Rapidus**, **Oriens**, **Arbustus**; of the **Blues**: **Imminens**, **Dignus**, **Linon**, **Paezon**, **Chrysaspis**, **Argutus**, **Diresor**, **Frugiferus**, **Euphrates**, **Sanctus**, **Aethiops**, **Praeclarus**. 

**Bind** their running, their power, their soul, their onrush, their speed. Take away their victory, entangle their feet, hinder them, hobble them, so that tomorrow morning in the <term>hippodrome</term> <note>A stadium designed for horse and chariot racing, the center of social and political life in many ancient cities</note> they are not able to run or walk about, or win, or go out of the <term>starting gates</term> <note>original: "carceres"; the stalls where chariots were held before the race began</note>, or advance either on the racecourse or track, but may they fall with their drivers, **Euprepes**, son of **Telesphoros**, and **Gentius** and **Felix** <note>Felix is a common Roman name meaning "Lucky"</note> and **Dionusios** "the biter" and **Lamuros** <note>Likely a nickname meaning "Greedy" or "Wanton"</note>.

# D.T. 241

->**ETERNAL SUN** <note>original: "SEMESILAM"; a magical name derived from the Hebrew *Shemesh 'Olam*</note> **SUBDUER** <note>original: "DAMATAMENEUS"; a magical word often used to address spirits to "tame" or "subdue" targets</note> **IESNNALLELAM LAIKAM ERMOUBELE JACOB** <note>original: "IAKOUB"; the use of biblical names was common in ancient magic to add authority to the spell</note> **IA IOERBETH IOPAKERBETH EOMALTHABETH ALLASAN.**<-

**A curse.** I invoke you by the great names so that you will bind every limb and every sinew of **Victoricus**—the charioteer of the **Blue team** <note>One of the four major professional racing factions in the Roman Empire</note>, to whom the earth, mother of every living thing, gave birth—and of his horses which he is about to race; under **Secundinus** are **Youthful** <note>original: "Iuvenis"</note> and **Advocate** <note>original: "Advocatus"</note> and **Wild Ox** <note>original: "Bubalus"</note>; under **Victoricus** are **Pompeian**, **Baian**, **Winner** <note>original: "Victor"</note>, and **Exceptional** <note>original: "Eximius"</note> and also **Master** <note>original: "Dominator"</note> who belongs to Messala; also bind any others who may be yoked with them. 

Bind their legs, their onrush, their bounding, and their running; blind their eyes so that they cannot see and twist their soul and heart so that they cannot breathe. Just as this **rooster** has been bound by its feet, hands, and head, so bind the legs and hands and head and heart of **Victoricus** the charioteer of the Blue team, for tomorrow; and also bind the horses which he is about to race; under Secundinus: Youthful, Advocate, Wild Ox, and **Laurel-Crowned** <note>original: "Lauriatus"</note>; under Victoricus: Pompeian, Baian, Winner, Exceptional, and Master who belongs to Messala and any others who are yoked with them. 

Also I invoke you by the god above the heaven, who is seated upon the **Cherubim** <note>A reference to the throne of the Judeo-Christian God, used here as a powerful "foreign" deity in a Greco-Roman magical context</note>, who divided the earth and separated the sea: **THE LORD** <note>original: "IAO"; the Greek transliteration of the Hebrew name for God, Yahweh</note>, **ABRIAO, ARBATHIAO, THE LORD** <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, **OF HOSTS** <note>original: "SABAO"; from the Hebrew *Sabaoth*</note>, so that you may bind **Victoricus** the charioteer of the Blue team and the horses which he is about to race; under Secundinus: Youthful and Advocate; and under Victoricus: Pompeian, Baian, Winner, Exceptional, and Master who belongs to Messala; so...

The spell uses a bound rooster as a ritual proxy to ensure the athlete's limbs and spirit are similarly constricted.</summary>

->**ETERNAL SUN** <note>original: "SEMESILAM"; a magical name derived from the Hebrew *Shemesh 'Olam*</note> **SUBDUER** <note>original: "DAMATAMENEUS"; a magical word often used to address spirits to "tame" or "subdue" targets</note> **IESNNALLELAM LAIKAM ERMOUBELE JACOB** <note>original: "IAKOUB"; the use of biblical names was common in ancient magic to add authority to the spell</note> **IA IOERBETH IOPAKERBETH EOMALTHABETH ALLASAN.** <note>These are "voces magicae" or "magical words"—strings of syllables believed to have power precisely because they were not part of everyday speech.</note><-

Now! Quickly!

## Papers on the Greek Magical Papyri

### Self-Identification with Deity and **Magical Words** <note>original Latin: "Voces Magicae," referring to the "barbarous" or secret names used in ancient spells</note> in Ancient Egyptian and Greek Magic

Modern occultists and students of the esoteric, such as the **Hermetic Order of the Golden Dawn**, have proposed that ancient Egyptian magic is the primary foundation for Western magical traditions. Since we know that the **Hermetica** <note>A body of mystical texts attributed to the legendary figure Hermes Trismegistus, blending Greek and Egyptian wisdom</note> and **Neo-platonic theurgy** <term>Theurgy: A type of ritual practice, often called "god-working," intended to invoke the presence of a deity or achieve union with the divine.</term> have had a deep influence on later European magic, studying the relationships between Egyptian and Greek magical ideas helps us test the claims made by these modern occult groups. This paper focuses on one specific set of ancient texts, the **Greek Magical Papyri**, which provide a wealth of information for investigating this relationship.

The **PGM** <note>Original Latin: *Papyri Graecae Magicae*</note> is the name given to a hoard of papyri containing magical spells, collected by **Jean d’Anastaisi** in Egypt during the early 1800s. **Hans Dieter Betz**, in his introduction to the most recent English translation, suggests that these papyri may have been discovered in a tomb or a temple library. He even considers that the largest scrolls might have belonged to a single collector living in **Thebes**. However, the exact origin of the **PGM** remains a mystery. Betz points out that according to ancient literary sources, many such books of magical spells were collected in antiquity, most of which were... 

<note>The source text ends here, likely continuing with a discussion on the destruction or loss of ancient magical libraries.</note>

<summary>This page introduces an academic inquiry into the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM), discussing its discovery in 19th-century Egypt and its significance in linking ancient Egyptian and Greek magical practices to modern Western occultism.</summary>

<keywords>Greek Magical Papyri, PGM, Hermetica, theurgy, Jean d’Anastaisi, Hans Dieter Betz, Thebes, Golden Dawn, Egyptian magic, voces magicae</keywords>

--- Page 198 ---
<meta>This page continues the analysis of the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM). It moves from the history of the collection to the nature of the spells themselves, highlighting their multicultural origins and the specific techniques of "self-identification" and "magical voices."</meta>

destroyed. Thus, the **Greek Magical Papyri** <note>Original: PGM</note> are a very important source for first-hand information about magical practices in the ancient Mediterranean.

The **PGM** spells run the gamut of magical practices, from initiatory rites for immortality to love spells and healing rites. Most of the papyri are written in **Greek** and **Demotic** <note>A late form of ancient Egyptian cursive script</note> with annotations in **Old Coptic**, and are dated between the 2nd century BC and the 5th century AD. The spells call upon **Greek, Egyptian, Jewish, Gnostic,** and **Christian** deities.

Two of the most intriguing aspects of these texts are the practice of self-identification with a deity and the use of **magical voices** <note>original Latin: "voces magicae," referring to secret or "barbarous" names used in ritual</note> in performing magical rituals. In many of the spells, the practitioner is told to use the phrase "I am" along with a specific deity's name to empower or activate the spell. For example, a spell for invisibility (found in PGM I 247-62) states: I am **Anubis**, I am **Osir-phre** <note>A name combining the gods Osiris and Ra</note>, I am **OSOT SORONOUIIER**, I am **Osiris** whom **Seth** <note>The Egyptian god of chaos and storms</note> destroyed. The use of specific magical language in these texts, the **magical voices**, is abundant. Most of these words are considered "untranslatable" by the scholars working with the papyri. Words of power in the incantations are composed of long strings of vowels—**A EE EEE IIII OOOOO, YYYYYY, OOOOOOO** <note>These seven vowels were often associated with the seven planetary spheres in ancient cosmology</note>—either alone or paired with special names of deities or **spirits** <term>Daimons: Intermediary divine beings in the ancient world that could be helpful or harmful; not necessarily "demons" in the modern sense.</term> which are often palindromes and significantly lengthy, such as:

->**IAEOBAPHRENEMOUNOTHILARIKRIPHIAEYEAIPIRKIRALIT HONUOMENERPHABOEAI**<-

The exact pronunciation of these **magical voices** was key to the success of the spells.

Since Egyptian funerary texts clearly identify the deceased with a deity, and the power of words and language is a predominant...

--- Page 199 ---
<meta>This page continues the investigation into the origins of the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM), shifting focus from the physical collection to the deep-rooted connections between ancient Egyptian funerary traditions and later magical practices. It explains how the Egyptian concept of "Heka" and the use of ritual scripts informed the spells found in the PGM.</meta>

feature of Egyptian magic, these notions found in the **Greek Magical Papyri** <note>Original: PGM</note> appeared to provide a possible link between ancient Egyptian and Greek magic.

Throughout the funerary literature of ancient Egypt, from the **Pyramid Texts** <note>The oldest religious writings in the world, carved onto the walls of Old Kingdom pyramids</note> to the **Book of the Dead**, there is abundant evidence that ancient Egyptians thought that human beings could become deities. Deities were seen as possessing **magic** <note>Original: *heku* (Heka), the personification of magic and the creative energy used by the gods to build the universe</note>, an aspect of the original creative power that formed the cosmos. Thus, magic was perceived to be an intrinsic part of reality and the divine. The **Coffin Texts** <note>A collection of magic spells written on coffins during the Middle Kingdom</note> provide a guide book for the deceased to help her or him retain what magic they already possess and to gain more. Naming is extremely important in this experience and it is the ability to name all the gods and objects encountered that proves one has acquired enough magic to sit with the gods. In these texts, the deceased is clearly identified with the god **Osiris** <note>The god of the afterlife, death, and resurrection</note>. By using **mythic narratives** <note>Original: *historaloe*, likely a variant of *historiolae*—short stories about the gods used in a spell to bring about a desired result in the real world</note> the deceased will successfully navigate the journey to the afterlife as did Osiris. The use of **mythic narratives** <note>Original: *historaloe*</note> in magical practice was common, particularly in healing rites. By knowing the names of all encountered in the afterlife and establishing a link with a deity that had already been successful in this realm, the deceased was well prepared for the journey.

In the **Pyramid texts**, the initial **Utterances** appear to be a script directing the different Egyptian deities to recite specific formulas on the deceased king's behalf. **Utterance 1** begins "recitation by **Nut** <note>The goddess of the sky, often depicted arching over the earth</note>, the greatly beneficent", **utterance 2**, "recitation by **Geb** <note>The god of the earth and husband to Nut</note>" and so forth. Evidence that these utterances were spoken during funeral rites are the notes after the recitations which give directions saying, for example, "pour water" (**Utterance** 23) and "cold water and 2 pellets of

<summary>This page explores the ancient Egyptian roots of the Greek Magical Papyri, specifically how the concepts of divine magic (Heka) and ritual storytelling allowed the deceased to identify with gods like Osiris. It describes funerary texts as active scripts used by priests to ensure a safe journey to the afterlife through the power of naming and recitation.</summary>

<keywords>Egyptian magic, Heka, Pyramid Texts, Coffin Texts, Osiris, historiola, naming magic, Nut, Geb, funeral rites, Greek Magical Papyri</keywords>

--- Page 200 ---
<meta>This page continues the investigation into the origins of the Greek Magical Papyri (PGM), shifting focus from the concept of "Heka" to the specific power of language and naming in Egyptian ritual. It follows the previous discussion on how the deceased is identified with Osiris to navigate the afterlife.</meta>

...natron <note>A natural salt used in mummification for purification and drying</note> (as in 32). The priests and priestesses take on the roles of the deities in preparing the deceased to join the gods in the afterlife, while also identifying the deceased with **Osiris** <note>The god of the afterlife, death, and resurrection</note>. This self-identification with a deity is an "authentically Egyptian trait."

Language, and particularly naming, carries substantial magical power in Egyptian thought. The goddess **Isis** <note>The goddess of magic, motherhood, and healing</note>, once she learns the true name of the sun god **Ra**, is then able to cure him of a snake bite. One of the oldest cosmologies <note>Stories explaining the origin of the universe</note> of the Egyptians from **Memphis** <note>An ancient capital of Egypt and center for the worship of Ptah</note> (approx. 2700 BC) describes the god **Ptah** <note>The creator god and patron of craftsmen</note> creating by his mind (the heart) and his word (the tongue). Thus, words contain a primal substance, and the act of speaking mirrors the original creation. Speaking creates reality. Writing was given to humans by the god **Thoth** <note>The scribe of the gods and deity of wisdom and magic</note>, and the Egyptians called their language the "words of the gods" and hieroglyphs the "writing of the sacred words."

The **Pyramid Texts**, **Coffin Texts**, and the **Book of the Dead** all exhibit the Egyptian belief in the power of language to affect the world. Words, whether spoken or written, were not just symbols, but realities in themselves. Hieroglyphs held a particular resonance with magical power, and most funerary texts were written in this script. The Egyptians clearly believed that humans have energetic doubles in the world beyond the physical, and it seems reasonable to suspect that the hieroglyphs were thought to have a similar existence since they were written on the *inside* of the pyramid tombs or coffins, or on scrolls placed *inside* the coffins for the deceased to use. Further evidence of the reality of the images themselves comes from the practice of cutting particular hieroglyphs in half <note>Often done to images of dangerous animals like snakes or scorpions to "kill" them and prevent them from harming the deceased in the tomb</note> to diminish their potential effect.

### Technical Vocabulary
*   <term>natron</term>: A naturally occurring mineral salt used for ritual cleansing and preserving the body.
*   <term>Osiris</term>: The primary deity of the dead; every deceased person hoped to be "Osirified" to attain eternal life.
*   <term>Ra</term>: The sun god, whose secret name held the ultimate power over the cosmos.
*   <term>Memphis</term>: An early religious and political hub where the "Memphite Theology" of creation was recorded.
*   <term>Ptah</term>: The deity who brought the world into being through thought and speech.
*   <term>Thoth</term>: The divine inventor of writing; the mediator between gods.
*   <term>Pyramid Texts</term>: The earliest collection of funerary spells, originally reserved for royalty.
*   <term>Coffin Texts</term>: Magic spells written on the coffins of non-royals during the Middle Kingdom.
*   <term>Book of the Dead</term>: A collection of spells (the "Book of Coming Forth by Day") to guide the soul through the underworld.
*   <term>hieroglyphs</term>: From the Greek for "sacred carvings"; the Egyptians viewed these as living images with their own agency.

<summary>This page explains the ancient Egyptian belief that language and writing are creative forces capable of shaping reality. It details how naming, the god Ptah’s spoken creation, and the physical "life" of hieroglyphs within tombs were essential to navigating the afterlife.</summary>
<keywords>naming magic, Ptah, Thoth, hieroglyphic power, funeral rituals, creative word, Memphis theology, ritual identification</keywords>
